### Settled

- **Release-path failure-outcome table (F1 to F20).** DECISION remedy.failure-outcomes.2026-10-18 (owner remedy 1): the only rule site for what a release path does when one of its steps fails. Rows are the release paths (`SlotClaimer.ReleaseSlot`, `releaseSlotRetrying` with `ReleaseSlotReservation`, `Binder.ReleaseSlot`, `Binder.Release`, `ReclaimClaimed`, the hook's step 5, D6, the failure funnel, `ReleaseUncommitted`, the resume compensation, `releaseEarlierBinding`, the suspension release, full_revoke steps 2, 3, 4(a), and 4(b), the `reserveSlot` rollbacks, the acquisition undos D9 and D10, the exclusive bind failures D15, and `rollbackBinding` with `releaseSingleShot`). Columns are context end, lock-wait error, store error, committed-then-error, superseded, and RPC cut; each cell gives action, return, and register row, with a row-level **Caller** column. Regenerated from it: register rows A4, A5, and A9, the A23 and A25 edge sites, the CODE-2 failure-outcome test (F4, F5), the CODE-6 writer-context case (F6, A5), the CODE-7 lead, 4(a) error, and `ReadPrimary` cases (F13 to F16), and the CODE-1 `podclaim` rollback case (A4); Races items 2 to 4 cite A17 to A19, the full_revoke post-row bullets cite A22 and F14, summary Decision 15 cites CODE-5 item 2, and the no-retry watch-out cites F2. The independent review then split F4's context-end cell (recycle patch detached; retire `Get` and delete on a context created after `shutdownAdapter`), named `rollbackBinding` in F3's caller cell, and added F3, F9, and F10 to A4 and A9.
- **CODE-6 step 5 context.** Step 5 keeps the writer's context. On `maxConcurrentSessions` above 1 a cut `Shutdown` releases the slot as leaked (counted, fail closed); on an exclusive pool CODE-2 item 8 writes the claim on a detached context; A5 bounds every caller-context end. The lock wait runs on `s.lifetime()`; two CODE-6 tier-1 cases pin it with the funnel holding the lock (writer ctx cancelled during the wait: release runs once after unlock; lifetime ctx cancelled: no `terminalReclaimPreRunning` or executor call).
- **Redis-outage rebuild.** DECISION owner remedy 2 (answer of 2026-10-18), CODE-1 item 8, summary Decision 21; A14 retired, A26 new. When an outage ends, `rebuildAfterOutage` deletes the `rehydrated` flag of every pod a non-terminal `best-effort-per-slot` row names (`ListPodsWithActiveSlots` on `SlotSource` and `sessionstore.Store`); it changes no set, and the pod's next reserve or release reseeds through §5.2 blocking rehydration, which REPLACES the set with the row identifiers. CORRECTED by non-spec.11.fix-G1.1: only `fallbackReserve` (as in the tree) and an unreachable `Counter.RunRecovery` tick (modelled on `storagequota.RecoveryReconciler`, 2-second default, flag-tunable, started on `w.watchdogCtx`) stamp the outage clock; a `Release` or rehydration error no longer stamps it. While the clock is set, `Reserve` and `Release` rebuild before their script, and only a successful rebuild clears it. A12 is widened in place: a reseed drops a reservation whose row does not yet name the pod (a create before its INSERT, a resume before its commit), on the release path (disposition under it) and the reservation path (one session over the cap per dropped reservation), once per replica per outage. A26: the one-probe-interval bound covers only a replica that has not observed the outage; a fallback-admitted session whose row lands after the listing or reseed stays uncounted until it ends; the third case is every observing replica exiting first. In the tree today `clearOutage` only zeroes `outageSince`, `fallbackReserve` adds no member, and `ReserveSlotUnderLock` counts rows only.
- **Flag lifetime in spec (spec round 22).** SPEC-1b's fifth substitution makes §5.2 **Post-recovery rehydration atomicity.** the single home of the `rehydrated` flag lifetime: a Redis restart clears it, the §10.1.4 whole-pod reset deletes it (as `Counter.Reset` does), and the gateway deletes it for each pod a non-terminal session names when a Redis outage ends (citing §12.4); its parenthetical reads "(indicating the set must be rehydrated)". SPEC-1c's second substitution cuts the §12.4 `rehydrated` key row to "from Postgres. No TTL." under its existing §5.2 pointer. "Non-terminal session" and CODE-1's `best-effort-per-slot` filter select the same pods (only `mcs >1` pods carry a flag). No test pins the replaced strings.

- **full_revoke fence against the own-Sweeper race (Decision 19).** **Order** step 3 opens each listed session's terminal claim through `BeginTerminal` before its cancel write; a write that does not apply or errors ends it at once; step 4 ends every claim after 4(a) and before 4(b), and the handler ends them on every return path. `startRelease` refuses while `claims > 0`, so neither `sweepTerminal` nor the resume tail can release an R2 binding before 4(a); `beginSuspension` and `releaseUnderSlotLock` refuse on `claimed`; the funnel, the hook, `rollbackBinding`, `releaseEarlierBinding`, and `ReleaseUncommitted` are ungated but none acts on a `ready` R2 binding; `/start`'s refused `commitStart` leaves the binding registered and releases nothing. `terminalClaimRecord.open` is refcounted with an idempotent `sync.Once` end, and the record is per-replica in-memory state.

- **Funnel order.** `applyFailureFromActive` writes the `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action` edge first and then runs `accountFailedSlot` and the failed-disposition release under `slotAccountLocks`.
- **remedy.review findings.** R5 adds D3c; R11 and R12 give D14 on `mcs 1` and D6a or D6b on `mcs >1`; **Release outcomes** adds the open terminal-writer claim to the refused `startRelease` case; the **Pool slot** column notes the N4 per-replica count; DOCS-1's definition adds "when the pool does not recycle"; DOCS-1 item 6 adds the glossary pod-reuse entry.
- **SPEC-1 vocabulary scope.** Text that defines REG-SLOTCOUNT as an atomic counter or describes INCR, DECR, decrement, or reset-to-0 is edited (SPEC-1g, 1h, 1i, 1j); nominal labels stay because they read true of a set's cardinality.
- **Release-path self-seed.** A release that finds the `rehydrated` flag absent rehydrates with its own member added to the seed and then removes it (`releaseScript` returns {-2,0}; `Release` gains `rehydrated`). A release that waits on a peer's lock, or finds that a reservation already seeded, removes nothing and skips the disposition; every interleaving traced still runs the occupancy-zero disposition exactly once where the set empties.
- **Leaked-slot lifetime home.** §6.2 **`leaked` slot semantics.** is the single home; SPEC-1a's leaked-lifetime sentence was deleted, and the reseed loss is a departure (A11).
- **Reservation checks membership first.** SPEC-1a and `reserveScript` check SISMEMBER before the cap; the tier-1 row "including at the cap" discriminates.
- **Redis-restart departures.** The reseed drops leaked members and unpersisted reservations, and a duplicate release after a restart can re-run the disposition (fails closed); SPEC-1a's "returned once" and Decision 3 carry "while Redis keeps the set". They land in the register (A11 to A13), REC-1, and docs, never as spec rules.
- **Shipped counter behaviour.** Today's `releaseScript` returns 0 on an absent key (a post-restart release runs the disposition under live siblings); a leaked release skips Redis entirely, whereas CODE-1 writes `leaked:<sid>`, so a leaked release can now fail during an outage (benign: the member stays counted).
- **Reservations precede the row.** `claimAtCreate` reserves before the INSERT sets `pod_assignment`, and `reserveResumeSlot` runs right after `claimPod` while the resume commit writes `pod_assignment`, so `GetActiveSlotsByPod` misses both in flight (the resume window spans the whole resume). Suspend release clears `pod_assignment`, so a reseed holds no member for a suspended session.
- **Counter.Reset.** Deletes `active_slots`, `rehydrated`, and `rehydrating` together and has no production caller, so the §10.1.4 reset is spec-only and flags outlive their pods.
- **Slot-set cost.** O(1) set operations on the same per-pod key, one reserve and one release script per session (about 400 Lua calls/s at 200 sessions/s against the ~6,500/s §12.4 budget), at most one `GetActiveSlotsByPod` per pod per flag loss on one replica, an emptied set deleted by Redis; SPEC-2 adds no etcd writes (a delete replaces the WarmPoolController's termination delete and SPEC-2d removes a status patch). After a Redis restart a release now reads Postgres; with a simultaneous Postgres failover it takes A4.
- **active_slots readers.** Only `pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter` reads or writes the key; the rehydration query uses the 0080 partial index; the §5.2 `WHERE state = 'active'` index note predates 0093.
- **go-redis re-send.** v9.19.0 re-sends after EOF or a read timeout, so an EVALSHA can run twice; the lost-reply release is in the edge case "A disposition failure after the member is removed" and CODE-1 item 5. `isRedisUnavailable` treats any `net.Error` and "i/o timeout" as unavailable.
- **Replacement trigger ignores the Redis set.** The fail-or-leak drain threshold reads the gateway's in-process `slothealth.Tracker` (failed events and leaked slots per pod, fed by `SlotBindError.Leaked`) plus the scrub-report leaked-pod drain stamp, so a reseed that drops `leaked:` members (A11) relaxes capacity only. `lenny_adapter_leaked_slots` comes from the same tracker.
- **Rehydration metric wiring.** CODE-1 item 4 sets `OnRehydrate: b.Rehydration` on every `podsession` `SlotClaimer` construction; `lenny_slot_rehydration_total` has no §16 or docs metrics row (pre-existing).
- **CODE-1 rollbacks and errors.** Item 4 routes the post-CREATE `reserveSlot` rollbacks through `ReleaseSlot(rctx, sb.Name, req.SessionID, false, false)`; the CREATE-loser withdrawal stays a raw `Counter.Release`. `ErrSlotCounterRelease` is a `podclaim` sentinel, so `slotcounter` tests assert only the raw source error and the wrapping is pinned at `podclaim`.
- **SPEC-2c home and predicate.** The §4.6.3 SandboxClaim row is the single home of which release deletes the claim: every exclusive release ends the occupancy, a concurrent one at slot occupancy zero (§5.2); it ends without reuse when the pool does not recycle, when no gateway replica published the session's binding (§7.3, with an in-place appositive defining publication), or on `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` when the session failed or crashed. "A slot reservation withdrawn after its claim `CREATE` fails (§4.6.1) ends no occupancy."
- **Binding publication in the tree.** `publishBinding` runs after `launchOnPod` on `/start` (through `registerBinding`, before `commitStart`), after the early running commit on create-and-start and delegated-child materialization, and after the resume commit (a bare `podRegistry.Put` on the checkpoint branch). `pod_assignment` is written at claim time and is no proxy. A `/start` refused at `commitStart` leaves the binding registered.
- **Occupancy projection.** A pure function of the current claim and the Sandbox phase: with no claim, a `claimed` pod drains, a `reserved` pod goes `idle`, and a warm-fill pod is left alone; an empty-phase claim also leaves it alone; a `failed` or `released` binding drains from any phase. The only gateway `failed` writes are the recycle boundary's `WriteDispositionStatus` callers.
- **SPEC-2d.** `failed` means scrub-failure retirement only; the tree's failed or crashed exclusive release deletes the claim and never writes `failed`.
- **Pre-projection departure (A15).** A release delete that lands while the Sandbox still reads `idle` or `reserved` leaves the pod `idle` with its `lenny.dev/tenant-id` label, a §5.2 pinned idle pod; the edge case points at §5.2 **Pinned idle inventory.** and **Deployer acknowledgment (runtime process kept across sessions).**. The label is admission-immutable and stamped on every pool kind right after the claim CREATE (a stamp failure fails the acquisition); every surviving joiner ran its own stamp.
- **Site classification membership.** A site is listed only when it contradicts §4.6.3 outright once SPEC-2c and SPEC-2d land; out of scope holds only §6.2 **Pre-attached failure retry policy:** (owner 0085; the summary 0085 row carries the re-baseline).
- **SPEC-2 pointer edits.** SPEC-2h (§6.2 recycle edges, label, **Concurrent pod lifecycle**), 2i (§28.5.1 drops "with the `failed` disposition"), 2j and 2k (§29.4 steps 17 and 12), 2l (§7.2 step 3), 2m (§4.7 `Shutdown` row), 2n (§6.2 `resuming → cancelled`), 2o (§7.1 `scrubPolicy`), 2p (§4.9), 2q (§8.3), 2r (§15.1 `created`), 2s (§24.11 force-terminate), 2t (§5.2 `Concurrent` preset), and 2u (§28.8 `CH-ATTACH` matrix cell: "names no retirement-counter reason for the retirement of a pod serving one session whose session failed or crashed"; counter reasons are only `session_count_limit` and `scrub_failure_limit`).
- **SPEC-2 lead wording.** "An edit that states the predicate uses the one wording "ends the occupancy without reuse", and no edit states the pod's resulting phase." The wording appears in 2a, 2b, 2c, 2e, 2f (across a wrap), 2g, 2h, 2j, 2k (across a wrap), 2m, 2o, and 2t; 2l, 2n, and 2p to 2s are bare `#463` pointers; 2i and 2u carry neither.
- **Sites deliberately not edited.** The §6.1 preConnect row; §5.2 **Scrub model.**; the §4.7 `ReportPodScrub` row (and its docs mirrors adapter-contract.md:86 and why-lenny.md:100); the §5.2 `Pod reuse` and `Bounded cohort` rows; the §7.2 step-3 tail; the §7.1 atomicity paragraph and diagram line; §6.2 suspension step 2; the §5.2 scrub-table closing sentence; the §6.2 `reserved ──→ idle` label and **Partial occupancy.**; SPEC-2c's §4.6.1 withdrawal citation; SPEC-2a's "§4.6 CRD table" label; the §16 orphan-alert cause list; the §15.1 `cancelled` row; the §5.2 burst sentence (scoped "After a Redis restart").
- **Orphan GC, RBAC, and admission.** The gateway is the sole SandboxClaim spec, status, and delete writer (RBAC `create`, `get`, `delete`; `sandboxclaims/status` `get`, `patch`; one SSA field manager); `lenny-sandboxclaim-guard` registers CREATE only and data-residency CREATE and UPDATE, so no webhook sees a claim DELETE; the CRD has no CEL rules; SandboxClaim has no finalizer or ownerReference; only hold-expiry DELETEs carry preconditions.
- **§11.4 staging.** SPEC-3a step 1 returns every enumerated lease and then deny-lists, before the first cancel write; step 6 points at step 1; step 2 writes `cancelled` and releases the binding, dropping `USER_REVOKED`; step 4 reclaims for "a cancelled session that never started and whose pod binding the handling gateway replica does not hold ... before that replica's step-2 releases". SPEC-3b keeps "The reported lease count is the number of leases step 1 adds to the deny list" and omits the uncounted pre-running reclaim from the Note.
- **Why step 1 returns the lease.** Removing the shared Postgres lease row makes every replica's proxy miss `GetByToken` (LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID before the deny check and before the fallback flow), independent of pub/sub, and `Release` returns the pool slot; both `Release` implementations find a peer-minted lease through the shared store and no-op on an unknown ID; renewal's `Advance` never inserts.
- **Release-before-deny home.** CODE-7 item 7 releases every enumerated lease before the first deny-list write; summary Decision 8 and the P5b row state no order. The discriminating tier-1 case wires the real propagator with `directModeRevocationRotator` and two direct-delivery leases on one pool credential.
- **ReleaseRevokedBinding contract.** Its nil set means this replica owed no release (non-terminal row, refused `startRelease`, no binding, evicted stale binding); every other unreleased outcome is `(false, err)`, including the executor error and the lock-wait expiry (binding stays registered; the Sweeper retries on its next 15 s listing). Its outcome cases sit in a `sessionserver` CODE-7 test bullet because `slotAccountLocks` and `startRelease` are unexported. `terminateLocal` maps released to `PodsTerminated`, an error to `FailedSessions`, and `(false, nil)` to neither.
- **CODE-7 tests and context.** `callCtx = context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), userTerminateRPCTimeout)` per binding (20 s); step 4(a) also survives a client disconnect (`ReclaimClaimed` and `ReleaseSlotReservation` are detached). The admin tier-1 bullet reseeds `TestPartialFullRevokeFansOutOnlyTheCancelledSessions_spec_11_4` in `ready` (`RevokeUserLeases` receiving all three sessions discriminates), seeds a superseded row, and asserts that a superseded or failing write's claim ends before the next recorded call and that no `BeginTerminal` claim is open at return; `recordingRevokeHook` gains `BeginTerminal` and `ReclaimRevokedPreRunning`.
- **Credential facts.** Production uses `credassign.Client` (Token Service): `Release` is a synchronous `RevokeCredentials` RPC (5 s, breaker after 5 failures, 30 s cooldown) and removes the record whatever the outcome, emitting `token.revoked` "session_released"; the in-process `credassign.Service` is the §17.4 dev profile and never errors. The revoker and assigner share `w.llmLeases`. `denyList.Revoke` returns nothing, so `leasesRevoked` equals the enumerated count.
- **Deny-list revoke hook.** On an in-process assigner `Propagator.Revoke` runs `directModeRevocationRotator.onRevoke` synchronously: it marks the pool credential unselectable, lists `LeasesByCredential`, and dispatches `proxyFallbackRotator.Rotate`, which mints with `AssignProto` and pushes `RotateCredentials` with no session-state check.
- **USER_REVOKED and the response.** `USER_REVOKED` appears in spec/ only at §11.4 step 2; the adapter never reads `ShutdownRequest.reason`, and nothing sets it after CODE-7 item 6. The full_revoke response and audit keys are untyped Go map keys; the OpenAPI operation declares a bare `200 OK`; docs/api/admin.md names no fields; the error path answers `INTERNAL_ERROR` after running the fan-out for the cancelled IDs. The `lenny:session:terminate` request has no schema and no spec home beyond the §11.4 Note.
- **Sweeper and unbind.** The Sweeper lists every row each pass (15 s default) and hands a terminal row bound here to `ReleaseTerminalBinding`; `PodExecutor.Release` unbinds before the binder runs, so a release cut after the unbind leaves nothing for the Sweeper. `adapterclient.Shutdown` has no internal timeout.
- **Session identity facts.** Delegated children inherit the parent's `user_id`; `session.StateStarting` has no gateway writer; a `/start` binding carries generation at least 1, so D6's `bindGen == 0` arm never evicts it; `terminateLocal` matches only the cancelled `SessionIDs`.
- **§11.4 step citations.** Step 1 (sessionstore List), step 2 (propagator.go, billing_security_workers.go), step 6 (user_revocation.go, retargeted to step 1 by CODE-7 item 8), step 7 (interactionstore); CODE-7 targets name propagator.go, so item 8 reaches its package comment. No spec/ or docs/ site cites a §11.4 step number.
- **§11.4 Note enumeration.** One response key per listed effect; `AfterRevokeWrite` and the pre-running reclaim are uncounted and stay in the numbered steps. R4 (a materializing delegated child) names no pod, so step 4 has no object there.
- **Resume ordering.** `releaseEarlierBinding` runs before `resumeOnPod`, and the `resuming` write runs later in the entry hook; a stale resume-family binding exists only on a replica that lost a running-state takeover. `ReleaseUncommitted(requireReclaimed=true)` returns before `releaseResumeSlot` and `releaseAttemptCredentials` on both pool kinds, and `notLanded` only logs.
- **Tier-4 resume-family case.** Takeover, then a pod failure on the adopter, then `resuming`, then DELETE on the old coordinator; the assertion is no seal RPC through the stale binding.
- **Seal loss filed.** REC-1 item 4 annotates F-10.1.33 with a pointer to **A superseded coordinator's terminal write loses the seal**, owner P2; the loss is new with CODE-5.
- **Double-decrement home.** Summary Decision 17 (tested paths plus the under-count consequence); CODE-2's Depends line points at it; the CODE-2 tier-1 double-release test list stays a test list.
- **CODE-6 lock safety.** No deadlock: `transitionToFailed` unlocks before the hook, `applyFailureFromResuming` takes no lock, `completeResume` calls no hook, `slotAccountLocks` is per session, and D6 runs on its own goroutine. `sessionLocks.Lock` returns `ctx.Err()` and holds nothing when its context ends.
- **Finalize exits.** On `mcs >1` `ResolvePool` failure is the only prepare-failure exit and through-Prepare failures (D15) are exclusive only; plan parse precedes it on every pool kind; a second by-name delete after `failPhase` is benign. CODE-3 Part B asserts end states of the claim, slot set, and lease.
- **D8 and rollbackBinding tests.** Tier-1 `podclaim` constructs the set-emptying withdrawal against a winner in `bound`, `recycling`, and `reserved`; tier 7a asserts the loser makes no claim write. The `rollbackBinding` test asserts D18 and D18b by citation with "no `recycling` patch and no recycle `Shutdown`".
- **Harness checker.** The slot-set checker clause reads "keeps a `bound` claim", matching `BoundClaims`.
- **Tier-11 gates.** The spec breaks `claimOwnershipRowSentence` and `claimWriterSetCell`, the 0067 staged-block comparison (entries for SPEC-1e and SPEC-2g only; SPEC-1h, 2i, and 2u are outside 0067's block), and the §11.4 "publishes the step-2 `Shutdown` request" literal; DOCS-1 retargets them. `session_policy_table_mirror_reconciliation_test.go` compares the docs mirrors only with each other. Closing F-11.4.16 needs a `**Resolution` note for `TestSuspendResumeFiledFindingsAreFiledOnce_spec_7_2`.
- **Spec lane.** A spec-lane step runs no tier and commits only spec/, so S1 to S3 declare "Tiers 0." and the retargets ride in DOCS-1 at S4.
- **CODE-4 manifest.** CODE-4 deletes the `createdsweeper` move line in `scripts/refactor/manifest`; no chart value renders a created-sweeper flag; the watchdog's store-based sweep covers crashed replicas.
- **CODE-2 item 3 discriminator.** `claimAtCreate` and `scrubPolicyForPool` use the same `MaxConcurrentSessions > 1` condition on every create path.
- **Plan conformance.** The P1 entry is gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md §10.2 (around :2745) with drafting rules and the test instruction above it. Every deliverable is staged or declined (single-releaser rule: Non-goals; durable release record: Decision 7; owner check: Non-goals; C3: Decision 18 to P5b; F-4.9.34 retry: credential-accounting proposal; claim-register row in tests/claim-map.json: REC-1 lead; plan tick: REC-1 item 7). Downstream consumers: P5b ("P1's lease reclaimer"), P2 ("P1's release owner"), R21 (declined record).
- **Open decisions for the human.** None; the section reads "None. The review loops and the open-decisions firings left no decision open for the human."

### Traps

- **Rule-site discipline.** The failure-outcome table, the accepted-departure register, the disposition table, and the full_revoke release table are each the only rule site (the full_revoke table the only full one) for their content. A change starts at the row, re-derived from the functions it names, then moves to the register row and tests; cut copies to citations, never re-synchronise them. Copies drifted every time they existed (D11 against R5; Invariant 1; Races items 2 to 4 against A17 to A19; the A13 and A15 bullets; the summary fence watch-out). Facet-by-facet rounds and per-site prose were tried and withdrawn.
- **Rule-site naming is load-bearing.** The A13 and A15 register cells name the spec-changes bullet headings verbatim, and A16 to A19 and R3 cite Races items by position; renaming a heading or renumbering an item breaks them. Races item 2's "where today's rollback deletes no joined claim" is the marker the lead's "except where the item says otherwise" relies on and must stay. Never cite A14 outside the log.
- **Recovery rebuild placement and scope.** The rebuild must run before the fast-path script on a replica whose clock is set; keep `clearOutage` off the bare-answer path. Scope the flag delete to pods a non-terminal session names; "every flag" reseeds pods holding only leaked members and over-admits. The rebuild changes no set, so a test must drive a reservation or release before asserting set contents, and a counted fallback case must let the create return before Redis comes back. Withdrawn: replica-memory tracking, marking the admitted row, rebuild at start, a Postgres check per disposition, eager reseed, a union (SADD) reseed (contradicts SPEC-1b's "replaces"), a once-per-outage Redis marker (cross-replica clock comparison plus an exception), gating on "this replica admitted", and stamping on every call (fail-open cluster-wide rebuilds).
- **Spec sweep after a non-spec mechanism change.** Decision 21 claimed the rebuild was "SPEC-1d's sentence" and needed no spec edit; three spec sentences still made the flag restart-only, and every spec lens filed it in round 22. When a non-spec decision changes a mechanism the spec describes, grep spec/ for every statement of it, and do not restate CODE-level mechanics (clock, probe, listing) in spec.
- **Redis restart semantics.** The chart runs Redis with `appendonly yes` on a PVC, so a restart may keep sets and flags; the spec frames a restart as data loss, and the CODE-1 clock and probe observe a restart as an outage. Do not treat the restart clause as the only trigger. A Sentinel failover that loses writes but keeps the flag triggers no reseed (the INCR counter had the same exposure; fails closed like A12).
- **CODE-6 step 5 alternatives withdrawn.** Running step 5 on `s.lifetime()` or `context.WithoutCancel` was rejected (`adapterclient.Shutdown` has no timeout); a bounded detached context was rejected (no bound fits both budgets). Some `AfterTerminalWrite` callers pass a request-derived ctx (`expireSession`, `afterFailed`, `recordSessionCompleted`) and others `WithoutCancel` (mcptools), so a discriminating test uses the former. Any lock-wait cancellation test must hold the lock before cancelling, because `sessionLocks.Lock`'s select picks at random when both cases are ready.
- **A25 fix withdrawn.** Re-reading after the cancel write's store error runs on the failing store and cannot tell this cancel from another writer's; a duplicate by-name reclaim can delete a successor's claim (A21); running 4(a) for every listed pre-running row deletes live rows' claims. A hidden-commit row holding a binding is still released by D6 only because step 3 ends the claim on any write error; keeping the claim open after an error would strand it. `pgtenant.InTx` can return a commit error after `fn` succeeded.
- **full_revoke fence traps.** The step-3 claims must end before 4(b), or `ReleaseRevokedBinding`'s own `startRelease` refuses. A leaked claim makes `startRelease` refuse that session on the handling replica for the process lifetime, so the superseded and store-error branches (absent from `[]RevokedSession`) need explicit ends. A step-4(a) reclaim with no deadline would hold off D6 indefinitely (check in CODE-7). Withdrawn fence alternatives: a departure, 4(a) skipping a started release, 4(a) after each cancel, holding `slotAccountLocks` across the write and drain.
- **Fence statements stay put (MISTAKE).** Round 7 deleted the admin test's claim-end assertion along with its restatement of **Order**, leaving superseded and failed writes unpinned; the remedy was a seeded case plus a leak oracle, not a restored restatement. Do not reintroduce a summary watch-out on when the claims open or end (the round-7 copy had already dropped the end-before-4(b) half). Decision 19 and CODE-7 item 3's doc-comment clause omit that half but do not contradict **Order**; keep `RevokedSession.EndClaim` and its gloss when trimming CODE-7 item 2.
- **Lease-return MISTAKE (spec round 1).** spec.1.fix-G3 moved the lease return off `RevokeUserLeases` and kept only the deny write, weakening the shipped fleet-wide durable rejection (peer proxies accept the token until the Sweeper releases; a binding-replica exit leaves it valid until ExpiresAt). Do not return to deny-only, add a revoked mark plus a proxy rule, or restore the bare `Remove` (strands the pool slot, F-11.4.10).
- **Per-lease interleaving reopens the rotation push.** Release A, deny A, Release B lets the deny for A find B on the same pool credential and rotate a fresh key onto a revoked session; every `Release` precedes the first deny write. A single-lease rotator test or `TestUserLeaseRevokerRevokesAndDenies` (distinct credentials, proxy delivery) cannot tell the orders apart. Do not add a per-session guard to `onRevoke`.
- **Step-1 crash and latency.** A crash after some `Release` calls and before any deny write cannot be repaired by a repeat (`LeasesBySession` no longer lists them); token rejection still holds through record removal. In Token Service mode the critical path waits on N serial RPCs (about 25 s before a hung service opens the breaker; a slow service costs N times latency); a concurrent `Release` fan-out is allowed if every Release finishes before the first deny write. Moving `Release` after the cancel writes needs a spec edit.
- **No "no release precedes its deny-list entry" guarantee.** A lease minted after the step-1 enumeration (in-flight start, fallback rotation, R5, R6) gets no entry; its token dies at the session's release (A22).
- **Deny-list granularity and session set are P5b's (C3).** Entries are per credential, so step 1 deny-lists a shared pool credential platform-wide and returns pool leases on it; full_revoke entries live in memory and the §4.9 startup rebuild never restores them; §4.9's populators omit full_revoke; an in-memory lease store lets a peer rotate a lease it minted; direct-mode slots return before the pods drop the key; SPEC-3b's narrowing to non-terminal sessions is the P5b session-set question. Do not file any of these against 0093.
- **Per-replica credential accounting (N4).** With the in-process `credassign.Service`, step 1's `Release` decrements the handling replica's count; `Client.Release` removes the record even when the RPC fails. Both predate 0093.
- **Do not renumber §11.4 steps.** interactionstore cites step 7.
- **Tree versus post-CODE-7 full_revoke.** Today the cancel writes commit, then the fan-out runs the pod terminate before `RevokeUserLeases` (deny then `Remove` per lease), with no pre-running reclaim; `DismissByUser` runs in `handleInvalidateUser`; the handler's store-error comment becomes false under CODE-7 item 1. Checking SPEC-3a or the table against HEAD shows a false mismatch.
- **§11.4 Note list is the counted list.** An added effect owes a response key and partial-failure detail. Withdrawn: an exemption clause, a reclaim count key, folding the reclaim into `podsTerminated`.
- **SPEC-3a step 4 wording.** Use "the handling gateway replica"; "before" names only that replica's releases because a peer's D6 can precede 4(a) (Races item 3). Withdrawn: dropping only "instead", "no binding on any replica" (false for R3).
- **Executor error surfacing.** `releaseReportedBinding` (failure.go, which CODE-6 says is unchanged) drops the executor error; CODE-7 item 4 works by inlining `releaseExecutor` or by changing that signature (three callers). A missing registry entry under the lock means "no binding" (nil), never a failure. Keep the "a later `ReleaseTerminalBinding` releases it once" assertion: it pins `endRelease` on the lock-wait error path.
- **Release self-seed.** A rehydrating release must seed its own member, or a post-terminal release removes nothing and Pass 1 reuses the unscrubbed pod. Withdrawn: "dispose when the rehydrated set is empty", dropping the `removed` gate, a phantom releaser member. Do not seed in `waitForFlag`, and do not file the peer-seeded release that removes nothing as a lost disposition.
- **Duplicate release after a Redis restart.** `WriteRecyclingStatus` patches any non-`recycling` phase, `reserved` included, though the state package admits no `reserved → recycling` edge; a duplicate post-restart release re-patches a held claim (re-scrub or retire) or deletes it, fail closed (A13). The resource-to-owner row "removes nothing" holds only while Redis keeps the set; do not add a per-row exception.
- **Pass 1 joins any live tenant claim.** `ClaimSlot` Pass 1 reserves onto any same-tenant claim not `released` or `failed`, including fresh empty-phase claims and `bound` claims on an empty set, and GC predicate 1 never fires once a new row names the pod. A skipped disposition leaves a joinable `bound` claim reused without the whole-pod scrub, and any by-name delete of a claim a sibling may have joined can drain a pod under it. No UID or resourceVersion precondition helps (a sibling reservation never writes the claim); do not re-file the by-name release delete as a missing precondition (Races item 1, A21).
- **No acquisition-undo universal (MISTAKE).** "Rollback" as a claim-delete edge licensed `reserveSlot`'s unguarded delete, and a later undo universal let the CREATE loser's withdrawal delete the winner's claim. The withdrawal stays a raw `Counter.Release`; D8 leaves the winner's claim intact; do not re-add a universal, even for the exclusive `claimIdle` delete. Reordering `reserveSlot` to CREATE first was withdrawn.
- **No pod outcome at a releasing site.** Do not write "retires", "drains", or "not reused" beside a claim delete; §4.6.1 **Occupancy projection** decides.
- **No unconditional recycling statement.** Do not restore "A recycling pool's release patches the claim to `recycling` instead" in §4.6.1; "after clean session termination" was a partial predicate copy that excluded a concurrent pool's failed last release, which recycles.
- **Publication predicate rather than §6.2 running.** The gateway cannot observe the adapter's record after an unknown start outcome. Withdrawn: reclassifying D18, D18b, and D14 as `running`; keying on the running commit; keying on `pod_assignment`; a separate definition site. "Pre-running" still names session states; do not change D3b or D4b to delete or add the publish-versus-`commitStart` order to the spec.
- **Pre-projection departure: do not restate §5.2.** Rounds 3 to 5 each summarized which acquisition admits, refuses, stamps, or retires the pinned pod and each was falsified (the fallback claim stamps no drain request; a failed stamp defers retirement). Withdrawn remedies: restoring the `failed` write, writing `failed` on every no-reuse release, a controller drain on an `idle` or `reserved`-level delete, waiting for the projection. Do not re-file SPEC-2d's delete as removing a level-triggered control (rounds 7, 12, 16, 18, 21 declined it).
- **Site classification by contradiction.** Do not re-add agreeing or departure-only sites; a missing-site finding must show an outright contradiction outside the departure window. Never classify a site out of scope with its own section as owner; convert it to a pointer edit. Sweep "recycle disposition" and "default disposition" too, and when a §28.5 card sentence changes, check the §28.8 matrix row for that channel (rounds 1 to 15 missed SPEC-2u's cell).
- **Pre-existing duplicates are not (g) findings.** The reserved-hold rule at §4.6.1 and §6.2, the §4.6 CRD row's trigger lists, the §4.6.1 **Pod claim mechanism** list, and §5.2's failure-retire sentence predate 0093; SPEC-2 adds only cited clauses.
- **Residual "slot counter" names are settled.** "Redis-counter occupancy" (spec/06), "Redis slot-counter occupancy" (06:166), §5.2 slot cleanup, §4.6.1, §12.4 (including the "Slot counter rehydration flag" label), §18, the `slotCounterPostgresFallbackMaxSeconds` key, chart comments "atomic slot counter", and the metric help text are naming; do not file them.
- **D10 and D9 by-name deletes.** `fallbackClaim`'s delete on stamp failure is no precedent for a `claimIdle` delete (both share the sibling-join hazard on `mcs >1`). Decision 11's old D10 reason was false on `mcs 1`; CODE-1 item 4 still cites "the reason Decision 11 gives", so keep Decision 11's wording general.
- **CODE-2 item 7 and the departure.** D18b's delete joins A15 in the pre-projection window although the tree recycled there at 85c9ab19a; judged too narrow to file.
- **Races-list windows.** A Pass 1 join onto a `recycling` claim mid-scrub and a cross-replica `ready`-row D2 removing a running session's member predate 0093 and are owned (REC-1 item 6, R16).
- **Spec-lane red window.** A `spec-only` run leaves the three tier-11 gates red until S4 lands; placing the docs step between S2 and S3 breaks the spec prefix.
- **Checklist renumbering.** Ledger entries before reconcile 2026-10-18 use older step numbers (S4 CODE-1, S5 CODE-2, S6 CODE-3, S7 CODE-4, S10 CODE-7, S11 TEST-1); the current checklist is under **Checklist** in **Settled**.
- **Build-tagged tiers escape tier 0.** `runStaticTier` vets only untagged packages and `contract`; a signature change breaks `chaos`, `integration`, `component`, `load_local`, or `security` files silently until their tier runs (CODE-1 item 2's `Reserve` broke tests/tier8_chaos/slot_counter_redis_outage_test.go, hence S6 runs tier 8). Grep tagged tiers when a step changes a signature.
- **Supersession chaining.** The 0093 REG-CLAIM entry must follow 0079's and use 0079's `to` as its `from`.
- **Failure-outcome doc pin.** `failureOutcomeEdgesStated` requires state-machines.md to keep "a failed session on a pool with `maxConcurrentSessions: 1`"; DOCS-1's retargeted want-string is a substring of the new row and contains no retired phrase.
- **Part A call counts.** They hold only where the hook is the sole releaser; borrow them as end states on any path with the handler revoke, `failPhase`, or D6.
- **Test rows land with their deliverable.** A test list under a code step must not include rows a later step makes true. The CODE-7 Files-touched line names no test file; do not add one alone.
- **Signature changes outside "every caller in the same step".** CODE-2's `ReclaimClaimed` change leaves `reclaimFinalizedPod` and `createdSweeperReclaim` on the old form until CODE-3 and CODE-4; pass `false` there. `resume.go:295` is a fourth `SlotClaimer` construction the CODE-1 item 4 rule covers (see **Open**).
- **Resume-family test discrimination.** "Released once" passes with and without CODE-5; the old coordinator's binding survives only until its next Sweeper pass or a `ReportAttachStreamFailure`, so hold both off.
- **Seal loss is P2's.** Do not add a seal to D6 or let the stale replica seal.
- **7a CREATE race and the harness.** Keep the winner live or opt into `RelaxOrphanClaims`; keep the checker's `bound` filter.
- **rollbackBinding test pool.** It discriminates only on a `recycle.enabled` pool.
- **Inverted existing tests.** The landing step inverts the named stamp-failure, ready-write, and `credential_lookup` tests rather than treating failures as regressions; `compensation_loss_test.go` is unchecked (see **Open**).
- **Materiality.** Docs-only staleness and Go-comment polish are refuted on materiality: the adapterclient `Shutdown` doc, the `shutdown` builder doc, users_invalidate.go:28, the `userTerminateRPCTimeout` comment, pkg/adapter/mcpruntime.go:278, `AfterRevokeWrite`'s "its fan-out sends Shutdown", and tests/tier5_e2e_kind/eager_claim_e2e_test.go comments; an implementor of CODE-4 or CODE-7 may fix them in passing. Departures this proposal creates (CODE-3 items 2 and 3, CODE-5's seal loss, A25) need a record. Register rows A16 to A19 and A21 carry "None" doc sites without a reason, and rows A1, A3, and A24 name DOCS-1 item 10, which states only the claim GC; both are non-spec register cells that a spec round must not file.
- **Single-source items left unfiled.** The F-7.3.34(b) reason at three sites, the F-10.1.21 residue at two, the GC bound clause, and the summary watch-outs; only "A disposition error after the member is removed must not be retried" copies both rule and reason (CODE-1 item 5). The summary **Problem statement.** under-count sentence stays.
- **Plan-conformance traps.** Grep summary, non-spec, and spec staging before filing "member never mentioned"; R21 and P5b's plan entry lacking C3 are plan-side staleness; the claim-register row is never a spec-loop finding.
- **Hard wraps defeat literal greps.** spec/29 is hard-wrapped (about 105 columns; 3-space step and 2-space bullet indents), SPEC-2f and SPEC-2k carry the predicate across a wrap, and CODE-8's comment anchors wrap across comment lines; collapse whitespace before counting. The §4.7 `Shutdown`, §15.1 `created`, §24.11, §28.8 `CH-ATTACH`, and §5.2 `Concurrent` rows are one physical line each.
- **Shell traps.** The glob `*spec-changes.md` matches both files, the non-spec file first; name the file exactly. The ugrep wrapper aliased as `grep` fails on long bounded-repeat patterns; use `/usr/bin/grep -E`. Do not run `pkill -f <script>` in the same Bash command as the script's heredoc, because it kills the invoking shell (exit 144). A per-literal regex scan of tests/ over spec/ takes minutes; pre-filter on 400-character windows.
- **Kubernetes recheck scope.** No staged change adds a non-owner status write, a command inbox, a finalizer, an admission dependency, or a synchronous controller step; by-name delete ABA readings without a concrete duplicate-release path were rejected in rounds 9, 12, 15, 19, and 24.

### Open

DECISION: SPEC-2c's §4.6.3 SandboxClaim row is the single home of the release-delete predicate: every exclusive release ends the occupancy, a concurrent release ends it at slot occupancy zero (§5.2 cited), and it ends without reuse when the pool does not recycle, the released slot is pre-`running` (§6.2 term, either concurrency), or on an exclusive pool the session failed or crashed; an undone acquisition or slot reservation is a pre-`running` release — BECAUSE the code deletes on the exclusive pre-running reclaim even on a recycling pool (reclaim.go ReclaimClaimed → deleteClaimDetached, no recycle check; Decision 5 keeps it) and Binder.Release drains on failed/crashed even with Recycle set (release.go `result.Recycle && disposition != dispositionFailed && !result.Crashed()`) — ALTERNATIVES: keying on "session never reached `running`" (a `ready` row can hold a registered binding that recycles), keying on "holds no adapter connection" (BindReservedSlot failure holds one and still deletes via recycle=false), patching the exclusive reclaim to `recycling` (no adapter connection to carry the whole-pod Shutdown).

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: a release that finds the `rehydrated` flag absent rehydrates the set with its own session's member added to the seed, then removes it; the disposition predicate ("removed the member and left the set empty") stays the one rule — BECAUSE the session row is already terminal at a post-terminal release, so a plain rehydrate-then-SREM removes nothing and skips the disposition again, and Pass 1 (slotclaimer.go ClaimSlot) then reuses the still-`bound` pod without the occupancy-zero scrub/delete — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "disposition when the rehydrated set is empty" adds a second disjunct to the predicate (hair) and, if the process dies between seed and SREM, a retried release finds the flag set and skips the disposition (fails open); seeding the releaser instead leaves a phantom member that over-counts (fails closed) until another release path removes it.
DECISION: leaked-slot lifetime keeps its single home in §6.2 **`leaked` slot semantics.**; SPEC-1a's "A `leaked` member stays counted until ..." sentence is deleted and the Redis-restart loss stays a recorded departure (spec-changes Edge cases) — BECAUSE owner preference is residual gaps as bounded departures rather than new spec rules, and the loss predates the proposal (today's seed from rows drops it too) — ALTERNATIVES: rewording §6.2/§5.2 Slot cleanup/Slot (session mode) to cite SPEC-1a codifies a weaker guarantee in the spec and pulls docs/reference/state-machines.md and glossary.md into the edit.
DECISION: register-vocabulary scope rule for REG-SLOTCOUNT restatements: text that DEFINES the register as an atomic counter or describes increment/decrement/INCR/reset-to-0 is edited (grep `atomic[^.]*counter` near slot, plus the §6.2 diagram labels); nominal labels "slot counter", "slot-counter keys", "Redis-counter occupancy" stay — BECAUSE the set's cardinality still counts slots and SPEC-1g itself keeps "Redis-counter occupancy" — ALTERNATIVES: a global vocabulary sweep (spec/04 §4.6.1, spec/05:587, spec/06:132/135/137/166, spec/12:202, spec/18:532, docs/reference/state-machines.md:258/268) is a separate decision, not owed by finding 0.
FACT: today's leaked release skips the counter entirely and returns (false,nil) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go ReleaseSlot `if leaked { return false, nil }`; today's releaseScript returns 0 on an absent key, so the post-restart release runs the disposition (deletes the claim under live siblings) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go releaseScript `if current <= 0 then return 0`.
FACT: `lenny_slot_rehydration_total` is emitted only from reserveSlot via SlotClaimer.OnRehydrate(podID, pool); a release that performs the seed must report it too or the per-restart event is lost for that pod — EVIDENCE: slotclaimer.go reserveSlot `if rehydrated {` and recordRehydration.
FACT: the Redis outage path does not clear the `rehydrated` flag on recovery (clearOutage only resets the outage clock), so "On Redis recovery, the counter is rebuilt through the standard blocking-rehydration path" (§12.4 failure table) holds only when Redis restarted — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go clearOutage.
OPEN: a reservation admitted by the §12.4 Postgres fallback adds no member; when Redis kept its data, that session is uncounted and, if it outlives its siblings, the last sibling's release empties the set and runs the disposition under it (and its own release then removes nothing). ### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: The slot set adds no etcd write and no new hot key; Redis ops per session stay one reserve script and one release script (SADD/SREM/SCARD are O(1)); set memory is at most maxConcurrentSessions plus leaked members per pod. No write-amplification finding at the top tier. — EVIDENCE: proposals/0093_*/0093_*.non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 1; pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go reserveScript/releaseScript
FACT: SlotClaimer.ClaimSlot Pass 1 prefers any same-tenant pod with a live non-terminal claim and calls Counter.Reserve on it, which rehydrates an absent set from the session rows. A post-Redis-restart release that removes nothing therefore leaves a `bound` claim that Pass 1 reuses before the claim-orphan GC acts, and once a new row names the pod the GC never reclaims it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:412-470; spec/04_system-components.md:517 (GC predicate needs no active session referencing the pod)
FACT: The shipped release on an absent key clamps to 0 and runs the occupancy-zero disposition (recycle patch or delete). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go releaseScript; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:853-899
FACT: The coordination Sweeper (default 15s) on any replica that binds a terminal session calls ReleaseTerminalBinding, which returns and removes the session's lease records (credassign releaseLocked -> leases.Remove). It runs independently of the full_revoke fan-out, so it can precede the handling replica's deny-list enumeration. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:528-535, 243-248; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:42-80; pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:458-470; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go:463-500
WATCHOUT: SPEC-3b's "so the release that returns a lease to its pool follows the lease's deny-list entry" holds only for releases the handling replica performs itself. The Sweeper race predates 0093 (0092 D6), and the new sentence turns it into a spec claim. — EVIDENCE: as above
WATCHOUT: The slot-counter code never rebuilds on Redis recovery without data loss (it rehydrates only when the flag is absent), although §12.4 says "On Redis recovery, the counter is rebuilt". That is a pre-existing code gap; the proposal keeps the spec text, so it is not a finding against the staging. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go clearOutage; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:219

FACT: ClaimSlot Pass 1 reserves onto any same-tenant claim whose phase is not released/failed, including a `bound` claim on an empty pod and a just-created claim with empty status (IsTerminal("") is false) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:423, :460; pkg/sandboxclaim/state/state.go:62-74

FACT: go-redis v9.19.0 transparently re-sends a command after EOF/read-timeout on the reply, so an EVALSHA can execute twice with only the second reply seen — EVIDENCE: ~/go/pkg/mod/github.com/redis/go-redis/v9@v9.19.0/error.go:84-110, redis.go:1001
FACT: the rehydrated flag is never cleared on a non-restart Redis outage recovery (clearOutage only resets the window), so §12.4's "rebuilt through blocking rehydration on recovery" has no trigger after a partition — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go:132-140; slotcounter.go grep rehydratedKey (no DEL outside Reset)
FACT: the holder replica's Sweeper releases a bound terminal row on every listing (15s default), returning leases, independent of the full_revoke fan-out — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:530-535, :248
WATCHOUT: "a release that removes no member changes no claim" leaves the claim `bound` and joinable; the next same-tenant cohort skips the whole-pod scrub / bounded-cohort retirement. Any GC-bound claim for that case must account for Pass 1 reuse — EVIDENCE: slotclaimer.go:417-466
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: Edge case "A reservation made during a Redis outage ... the rehydration seeds the set from the session rows" is false for an outage without a Redis restart: the flag persists and no reseed runs (fallback.go:132-140). True: the fallback-admitted session stays uncounted until a Redis restart or a reset.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-1 item 5 says only a disposition error must not be retried; a counter error whose script ran but whose reply was lost (go-redis transparent re-send, or releaseSlotRetrying's own retry) also returns removed=false on the repeat and skips the disposition. The edge-case list should name this alongside the disposition-failure case.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-1 item 5 IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE bounds the pause but does not require jitter; synchronized retries across replicas against a recovering Redis are possible.
OPEN: a full_revoke retried after the handling replica crashed post-cancel finds no live sessions (users.go:563 then len(liveSessions)>0 gate) and never writes the deny-list entries; pre-existing, security lens / P5 to judge.

### [spec.1.review-security.1]
FACT: Slot placement Pass 1 reuses any same-tenant pod whose claim is live (bound or reserved) and whose Redis reservation succeeds; a bound claim on an empty, unrehydrated set is therefore reoccupied after rehydration seeds it empty, not left for the orphan GC. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:411-470
FACT: The whole-pod replacement trigger (failed+leaked threshold) is counted by the in-memory slothealth tracker, not by the Redis set, so a Redis reseed that drops `leaked:` members relaxes the capacity gate but not the drain trigger. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:146-220, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:1494-1510
FACT: §12.4 failure-behavior row rebuilds the slot store through blocking rehydration on every Redis recovery, not only after a restart, so SPEC-1a's reseed drops leaked members on any outage recovery. — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:219
FACT: Exclusive-pool failed/crashed release deletes the claim (drain), it never patches `failed`; SPEC-2d aligns §4.6.3 with code and is fail-closed (deleted claim on a claimed pod projects draining). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go:66-120, spec/04_system-components.md:416
WATCHOUT: §11.4 step 6 ("Credential leases ... revoked (returned to pool)") is unchanged by SPEC-3a and sits after step 2's release, while the SPEC-3b Note insertion requires the deny-list write before any release. — EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:271-278
UNVERIFIED: a claim deleted right after a reserved→bound rebind, before the controller projects claimed, may project reserved→idle for a pod whose failed bind touched the workspace (§4.6.1 projection reads the controller's last level). Pre-existing in code and spec, not introduced by 0093; a controller/projection lens should check. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:415

### [spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: A releasing site states only whether the release deletes the claim (SPEC-2c is the home); the pod's resulting phase is §4.6.1 **Occupancy projection** and is never restated at a release site. SPEC-2e lost "and the pod retires", SPEC-2f lost "in which case the pod drains", the spec-changes Edge-cases bullet "Pre-running release on a recycling pool" was deleted, and summary row 0079 lost "retires the pod" — BECAUSE a claim deleted before any binding state, or while the pod projects `reserved`, leaves the pod `idle` — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "rather than patching it to `recycling`" (X-not-Y) and "a pod that projects `claimed` drains" (copy of §4.6.1); a per-binding-state outcome table (copy of §4.6.1).
WATCHOUT: Do not write a pod outcome ("retires", "drains", "not reused") next to a claim delete anywhere in the staging. The projection decides: no binding state leaves the warm-fill phase; deleted while `reserved` returns `idle`; deleted while `claimed` drains — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go (default branch "Leave the warm-fill phase untouched"); spec/04_system-components.md §4.6.1 projection bullets ("`idle` for a pod in a warm-inventory phase with no claim", "`idle` when the claim is deleted while the pod projects `reserved`").
DECISION: SPEC-2c's universal "The undo of an acquisition or a slot reservation that did not complete is a pre-`running` release under these rules" was replaced by "A slot reservation withdrawn after its claim `CREATE` fails (§4.6.1) ends no occupancy" — BECAUSE the post-CREATE rollbacks (writeBoundStatus, StampPodTenant, StampMaxPodUptime) already fall under SPEC-2c's "released slot is pre-`running`" clause, and the only act the universal got wrong was the CREATE loser, whose set-empty withdrawal would otherwise delete the winner's `recycling`/`reserved` claim — ALTERNATIVES: appending ", except that an acquisition whose claim CREATE failed deletes no claim" (narrows a failed universal); scoping to "after its claim CREATE succeeded" (misses Pass 1 joins of an existing claim); deleting with nothing added (SPEC-1a calls every keyed removal "a release", so the loser still meets "the release that brings the slot occupancy to zero ends it"); reordering reserveSlot to CREATE first (hot-path change; tree is already right).
WATCHOUT: Do not restore any universal covering "every acquisition undo". The CREATE-failure withdrawal is a raw Counter.Release with no disposition, and must stay so — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go reserveSlot (Counter.Reserve before CreateClaim; CreateClaim error branch calls only c.Counter.Release); spec/04_system-components.md §4.6.1 **Pod claim mechanism** "every other replica receives an `AlreadyExists` conflict and retries with a different idle pod".
DECISION: §6.2 Recycle edges header now reads "occupancy reaches zero and the release does not delete the claim under §4.6.3" (SPEC-2h first substitution); "after clean session termination" was removed rather than kept beside the pointer — BECAUSE "clean" was a partial copy of SPEC-2c's predicate that excluded a concurrent pool's failed last release, which recycles (slotbinder.go Binder.ReleaseSlot has no failure gate) — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's additive wording (two predicate copies whose conjunction contradicts §4.6.3 on concurrent pools).
DECISION: New SPEC-2i deletes "with the `failed` disposition" from §28.5.1 `CH-ATTACH` **Degradation.** and keeps its §6.2 "Pod crash during an active session" citation — BECAUSE SPEC-2d narrows `failed` to scrub-failure retirement, and the code's crashed exclusive release deletes the claim (podsession/release.go drain → podclaim.DeleteClaim) — ALTERNATIVES: "released and its claim deleted (§4.6.3)" (a third statement of SPEC-2c's rule).
FACT: The §28.5.1 anchor lines and the §6.2 Recycle edges header anchor each match the tree once (grep -c), and the new header lines are no wider than the old 103-column first line — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md §28.5.1 (heading "#### 28.5.1 Gateway-to-pod", **Degradation.** bullet); spec/06_warm-pod-model.md "Recycle edges (recycle.enabled: true; ..." line.
DEFERRED [/home/ec2-user/lenny/.claude/worktrees/draft-0093/proposals/0093_fix_release-ownership-for-pods-concurrent-workspace-slots-per-po/0093_fix_release-ownership-for-pods-concurrent-workspace-slots-per-po.implementation-checklist.md]: S2 ("all of SPEC-2") now also lands SPEC-2i into spec/28_communication-channels.md §28.5.1; S2's account stays true, but any S2 doc-reconciliation or spec-file list must include §28.5.1.
OPEN: non-spec lane coverage gap (not falsified, not authored here): no test asserts that a CreateClaim AlreadyExists/Forbidden withdrawal that empties the set leaves the winner's `recycling` or `reserved` claim intact; that is the case that discriminates SPEC-2c's new withdrawal sentence (non-spec-changes.md Tier 1 podclaim row on reserveSlot rollbacks).
OPEN: docs/reference/state-machines.md mirrors the §6.2 Recycle edges header ("Occupancy zero, claim patched `bound → recycling`") and now also needs the §4.6.3 qualification; this extends the docs deliverable already owed per spec.1.fix-G2.1's DEFERRED.

### [spec.2.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: full_revoke returns each lease at §11.4 step 1: `RevokeUserLeases` deny-lists the credential and then calls the credential assigner's `Release(leaseID)` in place of the shipped bare `Remove`; step 6 becomes the pointer "Step 1 revokes the credential leases held by those sessions." — BECAUSE deleting the shared Postgres lease row makes every replica's LLM proxy miss on `GetByToken` and answer LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID, independent of the Redis pub/sub deny-list publish (the §4.9 startup rebuild restores only `status='revoked'` credentials, so a lost publish or a restarted replica is never repaired), and `Release` also returns the pool slot, so a binding-replica exit before its release no longer strands it — ALTERNATIVES: see the WATCHOUT below.
WATCHOUT: do not return to a deny-list-only `RevokeUserLeases` with the lease return left to the session release (round-1 staging). Under a Redis reset, peer proxies accept the token until the coordination Sweeper releases the row (15s default), and if the binding replica exits before its release, the token stays valid until ExpiresAt. The shipped design had a zero window. Also rejected: a revoked mark on the lease record plus a new §4.9 proxy rule (a new field inside the sealed blob, which renewal's `Advance` rewrites; it still leaks the slot on a binding-replica exit; it contradicts summary Decision 1's no-new-field claim), and restoring the bare `Remove` (it strands the pool slot, which is the F-11.4.10 slot-return half). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go (GetByToken miss -> LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID); cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy.go buildLLMProxy passes w.llmLeases (Postgres store when pgPool is set, executor_credentials.go); pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go Sweeper Interval default.
MISTAKE: round 1 (spec.1.fix-G3) moved the lease return off `RevokeUserLeases` onto the session release, and summary Decision 8 kept "only the deny-list write". That weakened a security control below the shipped fleet-wide durable rejection. The finding cost a round.
FACT: both `Release` implementations are keyed by lease ID and do nothing for an unknown ID (credassign.go Service.Release -> releaseLocked `GetByID` miss returns; client.go Client.Release `GetByID` miss returns nil). The Token Service answers a duplicate revoke with NotFound, which the client treats as clean. So the session's later `ReleaseThrough`/`ReleaseSession` finds no lease and does nothing, and step 1's call is the one real release. `credassign.Assigner` already exposes `Release(leaseID string) error`, and `w.credAssign` is built in buildStores (stores.go -> buildExecutorAndCredentials) before buildAdminRouter (main.go). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go Service.Release/releaseLocked; client.go Client.Release; cmd/lenny-gateway/main.go call order.
WATCHOUT: with the in-process `credassign.Service` (dev mode, no --token-service-grpc-addr), pool `active` counts are per replica, so a step-1 `Release` on the handling replica decrements its own count rather than the minter's. This is the moved-out N4 per-replica accounting defect (problem-statement Scope), not this finding. Do not stage a fix here. — EVIDENCE: credassign.go releaseLocked `ps.active[...]--` guarded by `> 0`.
DECISION: the tier-9 wording became "deny-lists and returns every lease ... before its first cancel write" rather than requiring all deny-list writes before any return — BECAUSE the deny entry is per credential and the row deletion is per lease, so ordering across leases buys nothing; a per-lease deny-then-release loop is what CODE-7 item 7 builds. The new tier-8 case (non-binding replica, dropped deny-list publish) is the assertion that separates this design from the deny-list-only one; the tier-1/4/9 rows pass under both.
FACT: the count sentence "The reported lease count is the number of leases step 1 adds to the deny list." stays true (every enumerated lease is deny-listed, and a `Release` error still counts it per CODE-7 item 7).

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2c's undo universal ("The undo of an acquisition or a slot reservation that did not complete is a pre-`running` release under these rules") is deleted and replaced by one sentence saying a slot reservation withdrawn after a failed claim `CREATE` ends no occupancy — BECAUSE every post-CREATE undo (reserveSlot's writeBoundStatus/StampPodTenant/StampMaxPodUptime rollbacks) is already a release of a slot §6.2's `idle ──→ claimed` label counts as assigned (CREATE won and the reservation succeeded) and that is pre-`running`, so the base predicate covers them; only the CREATE loser, which SPEC-1a's script vocabulary still calls a release, needed a statement — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "except that an acquisition whose claim CREATE failed deletes no claim" (narrows the round-1 universal, same kind; the measured failure pattern); "undo after its CREATE succeeded" (also needs "or on an existing claim" for Pass 1 joins: an enumeration that grows); reordering reserveSlot to CREATE before Reserve (code churn, and the post-CREATE reserve failure still needs the keyed release).
WATCHOUT: deleting the universal leaves the exclusive Claimer.claimIdle fresh-claim delete after a writeBoundStatus failure unnamed as such; it is a release of a pre-`running` exclusive occupancy under the base predicate (and the tree row never named it). Do not re-add an undo universal to cover it — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go claimIdle (`_ = c.Client.Delete(ctx, claim)` after writeBoundStatus).
FACT: the CREATE loser's withdrawal is today a raw `c.Counter.Release` with no disposition, and CODE-1 item 4 keeps it raw — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go reserveSlot (`CreateClaim` error branch).
DECISION: a releasing site never states the pod's resulting phase; §4.6.1 **Occupancy projection** owns it. SPEC-2e drops "and the pod retires", SPEC-2f drops "in which case the pod drains", the spec-changes Edge-case bullet "Pre-running release on a recycling pool" is deleted, and the summary 0079 impact row drops "and retires the pod" — BECAUSE a delete of a claim with no binding state leaves the pod idle (occupancy.go "Leave the warm-fill phase untouched") and a delete while the pod projects `reserved` returns it to idle; both pods are clean, so the outcome is safe but the sentences were false — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "and a pod that projects `claimed` drains" (restates §4.6.1 at a second site).
FACT: a failed `reserved → bound` rebind (WriteRebindStatus error) deletes nothing in either claimer; the rebind-then-reserve-failure path is a keyed slot release on a `bound` claim — EVIDENCE: claimer.go rebindReserved, slotclaimer.go rebindReservedSlot.
DECISION: the §6.2 Recycle edges header replaces "after clean session termination" with the §4.6.3 pointer rather than adding the pointer beside it — BECAUSE "clean session termination" is a partial copy of SPEC-2c's failed/crashed clause and is wrong for a concurrent pool, whose failed last running release recycles (SPEC-2c's failed/crashed clause is exclusive-only; slotbinder.go Binder.ReleaseSlot has no failure gate) — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's additive wording keeps both, which excludes the concurrent failed-last case §4.6.3 recycles.
DECISION: §28.5.1 **Degradation.** deletes "with the `failed` disposition" and keeps its existing §6.2 "Pod crash during an active session" citation as the home of the outcome — BECAUSE pointing beats restating SPEC-2c's delete rule — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "released and its claim deleted (§4.6.3)" restates the rule.
OPEN: no test pins the CREATE-loser case (a withdrawal that empties the set leaves the winner's `recycling` or `reserved` claim intact). The non-spec Tier 1 `podclaim` row's "deleted only when the set is then empty" stays true but does not discriminate; the non-spec lane should add the row.
WATCHOUT: docs/reference/state-machines.md Recycle transitions intro and the `claimed → claimed` "Occupancy zero, claim patched bound → recycling" row mirror the §6.2 header; the docs deliverable owed since spec.1 G2 must also take the §4.6.3 qualification — EVIDENCE: docs/reference/state-machines.md "### Recycle transitions".

### [spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: full_revoke returns each lease at §11.4 step 1: `RevokeUserLeases` adds the credential to the deny list, then calls the credential assigner's `Release(leaseID)` (consumer interface over `w.credAssign`); `userLeaseStore` drops `Remove`; step 6 becomes a pointer to step 1 — BECAUSE `Release` deletes the Postgres lease row (proxy `GetByToken` miss -> LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID on every replica, no pub/sub dependency) and returns the pool slot (Token Service `RevokeCredentials` -> `assign.Release`), closing both the Redis-reset window and the binding-replica-exit leak with no new field, column, or proxy rule — ALTERNATIVES: a revoked mark on the lease record plus a proxy check (new lease field or column, a new §4.9 matching rule, a lost-update hazard against renewal's `Advance` that rewrites the whole blob, and the slot still leaks when the binding replica dies); keeping the deny-list-only design as a departure (degrades the shipped zero-window revocation; conflicts with owner fail-closed preference); restoring the shipped bare `Remove` (durable, but strands the pool slot, which is the F-11.4.10 slot-return defect).
CORRECTS [summary.md alternatives "Returning leases from `RevokeUserLeases` through `credassign.Release`"]: the stated costs do not hold. Both `Release` implementations are keyed by lease ID and no-op on a missing record (credassign.go `releaseLocked` GetByID miss; client.go `Release` GetByID miss), the Token Service answers a second revoke NotFound which the client treats as clean, and no session release runs on a non-terminal row, so step 1's `Release` is the only one and the session's later `ReleaseThrough` lists no lease.
FACT: the LLM proxy reads the lease row straight from Postgres per request (pgstore `GetByToken`, no cache) and rejects a miss with LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID before the deny-list check — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credleasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:176-181; pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go:322-332
FACT: renewal writes leases only through `Advance`, which updates and inserts none, so a lease row deleted at step 1 is not resurrected by a peer's renewal worker — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:334-360; pgstore.go:228-280
FACT: production gateways use `credassign.Client` (Token Service, central slot accounting); the in-process `credassign.Service` with per-replica `ps.active` is the §17.4 dev profile only — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:129-149; charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:498
WATCHOUT: `credassign.Client.Release` removes the lease record even when the revoke RPC fails (breaker open), so the token dies but the Token Service slot may not return; this is the pre-existing behavior of every Client release, not a full_revoke regression, and is not staged — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:363-376
WATCHOUT: in a multi-replica in-process (dev) deployment, step 1's `Release` on the handling replica decrements that replica's `ps.active`, not the assigning replica's; this is the per-replica credential-accounting class (N4) the problem statement moved out — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:458-470
WATCHOUT: the tier-9 line said the deny-list writes precede "any" lease return; a per-lease loop (deny, release, deny, release) violates that wording. The redesign restates the tier-9 assertion as "deny-lists and returns every lease before the first cancel write"; do not impose a two-pass loop to satisfy the old wording.

### [spec.2.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The occupancy projection decides what a claim DELETE does to the pod from the phase the controller last projected, not from the gateway's intent. A delete while the pod projects `claimed` drains it. A delete while it projects `reserved` returns it to idle. A delete of a claim that never got a binding state (the projection leaves the warm-fill phase untouched) leaves the pod `idle`. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:410-416 (projection bullets); pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:99-140
WATCHOUT: SPEC-2c now counts the undo of an acquisition or slot reservation as a pre-`running` release. reserveSlot's writeBoundStatus-failure undo deletes a claim that has no binding state. On a recycling pool, SPEC-2e's "and the pod retires" and the edge-case bullet "The pod is not reused" are therefore false on that path, because the pod stays idle. Retirement is the projection's decision. A gateway-side sentence should say only that the claim is deleted rather than patched to `recycling`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:720-724
UNVERIFIED: On a concurrent pool, a slot release empties the set (SREM) and then deletes the claim by name. A sibling reservation that lands between the two joins the existing claim without writing to it, so no UID/resourceVersion precondition can detect it. This is a Redis-to-apiserver TOCTOU that predates this proposal, and no edge-case bullet names it. Owner: a later full-pool reviewer should decide whether it belongs in the departures.

### [spec.2.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: A podRegistry binding exists only from the §4.7.1 record step onward, which is where §6.2 says a slot reaches `running`. So D6 (`ReleaseTerminalBinding`) and the hook's executor release never release a pre-`running` slot, and SPEC-2c's "released slot is pre-`running`" clause does not reach them. An exclusive recycling D6 recycle is therefore not a contradiction of SPEC-2c. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:66-78; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md "Pre-`running` slot cleanup." paragraph
WATCHOUT: SPEC-2c's undo clause ("The undo of an acquisition or a slot reservation that did not complete is a pre-`running` release under these rules") drops r1's "the claim it created" bound. CODE-1 item 4 keeps the failed-CreateClaim rollback as a raw `Counter.Release` with no disposition, which is correct because the existing claim belongs to the CREATE winner. Keep the undo clause scoped to a claim the undoing path created or reserved onto. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:220; non-spec-changes.md:70; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:704-716
FACT: Counter.Reset deletes active_slots, rehydrated, and rehydrating together, so a §10.1.4 whole-pod reset re-arms rehydration on the next reservation or release. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:482-488
FACT: WriteRecyclingStatus has no phase guard beyond the recycling no-op, so the edge-case bullet's reserved→recycling patch on a duplicate post-restart release applies as stated. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/bindingstate.go:92-110

### [spec.2.review-performance.1]
FACT: In production the LLM proxy resolves a lease token against the shared Postgres lease store (GetByToken, LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID on a miss), so the shipped full_revoke `Remove` (a Postgres DELETE) invalidates the revoked user's leases on every replica independent of Redis pub/sub — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go:322-327; cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:74-83; cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy.go:141; pkg/gateway/credentials/credleasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:282-285; cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:184-190
FACT: full_revoke deny-list entries are in-memory, propagated only by pub/sub, and are not covered by the §4.9 startup rebuild query (it rebuilds only credentials with status='revoked') — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1792-1804
FACT: the coordination Sweeper releases a terminal row this replica binds once per interval (default 15s), which is the durable fallback for a lost pub/sub release request — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:243-249, 428-433, 530-533
FACT: Counter.rehydrate re-checks the rehydrated flag under both the in-process mutex and the SET NX lock and returns without seeding when it is set, so a release-path rehydration adds its own member only when it is the seeder; at most one GetActiveSlotsByPod per pod per Redis restart still holds with release-path rehydration — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:326-395
WATCHOUT: RevokeUserLeases' Deny write (`Propagator.Revoke`) returns no error, so SPEC-3a step 1's deny-list-before-cancel order cannot block the cancel writes on a Redis failure — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:162-165,184-190

### [spec.2.review-reliability.1]
FACT: Counter.rehydrate re-checks the `rehydrated` flag under both the in-process mutex and the SET NX lock, so a release-path rehydration (SPEC-1a) that loses the race to a peer's seed does not seed its own member; its retried release script then removes nothing and skips the disposition, and the peer's removing release runs it once. Release-path seeding therefore adds no double disposition outside the recorded Redis-restart departures. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:326-395
FACT: The occupancy projection drains a pod whose claim is deleted while it projects `claimed`, and returns it to `idle` only when the claim is deleted from `reserved`. So SPEC-2c's claim delete for a failed mcs1 session or a pre-running release fails closed, and the duplicate-release departure's "deletes the held claim" returns an already scrubbed pod to idle. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:415-416
DECISION: did not file the §12.4 "On Redis recovery, the slot set is rebuilt" sentence (SPEC-1d) — BECAUSE that sentence is correct as spec intent, and the gap is in code (clearOutage never clears the flag after an outage with no restart). Its remedy is code or the non-spec departure, which is outside the spec loop. This round-2 reliability pass agrees with spec.1.review-performance.1 WATCHOUT — ALTERNATIVES: a new spec rule that clears the flag of fallback-admitted pods on recovery; rejected because the owner prefers bounded departures to new rules, and the fix is code-side.
USEFUL [spec.1.review-reliability.1]: the go-redis re-send and clearOutage FACTs saved re-deriving the recovery paths.
OPEN: spec.1.review-reliability.1's OPEN (the sibling's last release runs the disposition under a fallback-admitted session after an outage in which Redis kept its data) is still unresolved in the non-spec Edge case "A reservation made during a Redis outage". That case states only "the session's release finds no member and changes nothing" and omits the disposition-under-live-session consequence. The between-loops pass or the non-spec loop owns it.

### [spec.2.review-security.1]
FACT: The §4.9 credential deny list is credential-granular, not lease-granular: the entries are `{pool, poolId, credentialId}` or `{user, tenantId, credentialRef}`, and full_revoke adds `lease.CredentialKey()`. "Adds the credential leases ... to the deny list" in SPEC-3a step 1 therefore deny-lists the shared pool credential for every lease on it. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1792-1795; pkg/credential/lease.go:296; cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:185-187
OPEN: For pool-backed leases, step 6 returns the lease to a pool whose credential step 1 has deny-listed. The startup rebuild does not reseed full_revoke entries, because it seeds only status='revoked' credentials. This predates 0093 (the old step 6 and the Note's deny-list publish say the same thing). It is for the human or P5 (0094) to decide whether user-backed-only deny-listing is intended. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1799-1804; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:275,278
WATCHOUT: §28.5.1 Gateway-to-pod still says "A pod serving one session is released with the `failed` disposition". After SPEC-2c (a failed or crashed session on maxConcurrentSessions: 1 deletes the claim) and SPEC-2d (`failed` means scrub-failure retirement only), that is stale, and it is in no edit list. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:264-265
WATCHOUT: §6.2 **Pre-attached failure retry policy:** says "The pod is marked `failed` and released back to the pool". That contradicts SPEC-2c (the undo of an acquisition is a pre-running release, which ends the occupancy without reuse). The problem statement excludes the pre-attached seam disposition (draft 0085), so it was not filed. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:293; problem-statement.md:49
UNVERIFIED: §29.4 step 17 says "On the default disposition the claim is deleted" at every session end with no occupancy qualifier, while SPEC-2c deletes on a concurrent pool only at occupancy zero. §29 traces run under the concurrent condition (spec/29:17-20). The problem statement names step 17 as a contradiction, yet the staging never edits it. A consistency lens should judge whether "default disposition" implicitly means occupancy zero. EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:771-781
USEFUL [review-log FACT slothealth]: the whole-pod replacement trigger is counted in-memory and not from the Redis set, so a reseed dropping leaked members does not reset the drain trigger. That saved re-deriving the security-bound durability question.
