OPEN: [retry jitter] Decide in the non-spec loop whether CODE-1 item 5's IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE pause in non-spec-changes.md requires jitter, so that replicas do not retry in step against a recovering Redis. Carried from spec.1.review-reliability.1.
OPEN: [row D9] On `mcs >1`, row D9 (`Binder.Resume` → `claimPod` → `Claimer.claimIdle` deleting a fresh claim when `writeBoundStatus` fails) deletes a claim that a sibling's `ClaimSlot` Pass 1 may have joined, and SPEC-2c has no edge for it. Stage either a code change or a REC-1 departure in non-spec-changes.md, and add the case to **Races the table does not close**. Carried from spec.3.review-fresh.1.
OPEN: [invariant 2] The disposition table's invariant 2 in non-spec-changes.md restates SPEC-2c's full "ends the occupancy without reuse" predicate; make it cite SPEC-2c rather than copy it. Carried from spec.3.review-fresh.1.
OPEN: [row D10] Decide whether CODE-2 deletes the claim `Claimer.claimIdle` leaves when `StampPodTenant` fails, as `fallbackClaim` already does, or whether REC-1 files it as a GC-bounded departure, in non-spec-changes.md. The summary's **Defects in the shipped tree that this proposal does not stage** already names the choice. Carried from Standing context Deferred item 7.
DECISION: Added no entry to the summary's **Open decisions for human to make** — BECAUSE no unclosed OPEN in this log routes a decision to the human. The OPENs at spec.1.fix-G1.1 (rehydration metric `pool` label), spec.9.follow-up-fix.1 (whether the SPEC-2 lead covers SPEC-2d's retained projection clause), the spec.3.fix-design-G1.1 OPEN (REC-1 filing of the pre-projection delete departure), and the carried items above are routed to the non-spec loop or a later spec round. The spec.2.review-security.1 OPEN on deny-listed pool leases was routed by the owner to P5b (summary Decision 18), and the summary's P5b impacts row and **Defects** list state the interim effect. The spec.1.review-reliability.1 OPEN on a retried full_revoke after a post-cancel crash no longer applies, because SPEC-3a step 1 writes the deny list and returns the leases before the first cancel write. The OPENs at spec.1.fix-G3.1 (0094 row), spec.1.review-applicability.1 and spec.1.review-fresh.1 (step 6 order), and spec.4.review-security.1 (publication definition) were settled by later fixes.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-7 item 5 derives `callCtx` as `context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), userTerminateRPCTimeout)` — BECAUSE on the handling replica `ctx` is the admin `req.Context()` and `PodExecutor.Release` unbinds before the binder runs, so a cancel after the unbind loses the release for every replica (the Sweeper's D6 acts only when `boundHere`) — ALTERNATIVES: `s.lifetime()` (unbounded: item 6 drops `userTerminateDeadline`, contradicts SPEC-3b "each local release is bounded"); detaching the exclusive `Get`/`drain` for every caller (changes D3a for all callers; belongs to the table, not CODE-7); background release past the wait (a second release mechanism).
FACT: the exclusive retire path of `Binder.Release` (`shutdownAdapter`, `Client.Get`, `drain`) and the per-slot `Shutdown` in `Binder.ReleaseSlot` run on the caller's ctx; only the CODE-1 claimer call and `ReclaimClaimed`'s by-name delete are detached. A cancelled slot `Shutdown` marks the slot `leaked`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go:115-120; slotbinder.go:813-814; pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:271-283; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:532
FACT: admin full_revoke passes `req.Context()` into `terminateUserSessions` and `runFullRevokeFanOut`, which calls `userPods.TerminateUserSessions(ctx, ...)`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go:563,568,577; users_invalidate.go:151; cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:129
WATCHOUT: the tier-1 `TestPodTerminateFanOut*` tests use a fake releaser, so the discriminating assertion is the context `ReleaseRevokedBinding` receives (not done, carries the `userTerminateRPCTimeout` deadline), not a claim or set-member effect. A reviewer asking for "claim deleted, no leaked: member" at tier 1 asks for something tier 1 cannot observe.
WATCHOUT: the remaining timeout-expiry case (exclusive adapter `Shutdown` outlasting `userTerminateRPCTimeout` after the unbind) is a departure in **Releases no replica performs**, bounded by the claim-orphan GC. The concurrent-slot analogue stays `leaked:` by design (fail closed, §6.2) and is not a departure to fix.
OPEN: whether `Binder.Release`'s retire-path `Get` and `drain` should run on `detachedReleaseContext` for every caller, which would close the timeout-expiry departure for D11 and the same expiry for other callers; it is a disposition-table (D3a) change and needs its own finding.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: Row D10 (`Claimer.claimIdle` leaves a `bound` claim when `StampPodTenant` fails) is an accepted GC-bounded departure, filed by REC-1 item 5 beside N2 and listed in the non-spec Edge cases departures; summary Decision 11 carries the reason for both. This discharges Standing context Deferred item 7 and the OPEN [row D10] — BECAUSE `claimIdle` is reached on mcs >1 through `Binder.Resume`, where `SlotClaimer.ClaimSlot` Pass 1 can join the `bound` claim before the stamp fails, so a by-name delete is the same unsafe delete Decision 11 rejects for N2 — ALTERNATIVES: a CODE-2 `claimIdle` delete mirroring `fallbackClaim` (drains a pod a sibling joined); an exclusive-only delete (pool-kind branch for a fail-closed, GC-bounded case).
WATCHOUT: `fallbackClaim`'s delete on `StampPodTenant` failure is not precedent for a `claimIdle` delete; on concurrent pools reached through Resume it has the same sibling-join hazard (row D9). Do not cite it to reopen D10 — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/fallbackclaim.go (delete on stamp failure); pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go `claimIdle` (StampPodTenant error returned, no delete; writeBoundStatus failure deletes).
OPEN: Row D9's by-name deletes (`claimIdle` after `writeBoundStatus` fails, `fallbackClaim`) run on mcs >1 through `Binder.Resume`, where a Pass 1 sibling may already have joined the claim. That predates this proposal and was marked separate-finding by the G2 design; a later round decides whether it is filed.
DECISION: Deleted disposition-table Invariants 1 and 2 outright (remaining invariants renumbered 1 to 3) — BECAUSE they copied SPEC-2c and SPEC-1a, Invariant 1 had dropped SPEC-1a's "while Redis keeps the set" qualifier, and the table preface and the **Claim** column already point at SPEC-2c and SPEC-1a — ALTERNATIVES: a pointer line (third statement of where the rule lives); re-adding the Redis qualifier (re-synchronises a copy). Closes the review-log DEFERRED/OPEN [invariant 2].
DECISION: **Races the table does not close** is the one home of each claim race, owner included (item 2 now names the recycling follow-up; item 3 already names R16 with S17; item 1 has no owner and none is invented). The summary Defects list replaces its three race bullets with one pointer bullet, and REC-1 item 6 files the summary list "taking each race from **Races the table does not close**", which also drops the unmet "each with its owner".
WATCHOUT: Decision 11 is cited by CODE-1 item 4 for the `reserveSlot` fresh-claim rollback, which is not a `claimPod` claim. Keep Decision 11's reason general ("a freshly created claim before the failing call gives up"); the design's literal "the claim `claimPod` created" would have narrowed the pointer's target away from CODE-1 item 4 — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 4 "for the reason Decision 11 gives".

### [non-spec.1.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: the tier-11 gate retargets live in TEST-1 **Tier 11 (docs).** (lands at S11) as one pointer sentence, and S1, S2, and S3 now run "Tiers 0." only — BECAUSE the spec lane (implement-proposal-build.js runSpecStep) applies SPEC-n text, runs no tier, and commits only spec/ files, so a tests/ retarget staged against S1 to S3 is never committed, and no code step S4 to S10 runs tier 11, so the red window between S1 and S11 is unobserved — ALTERNATIVES: retargets in S1 to S3 (spec lane cannot commit tests/); a new test step after S3 (renumbers S4 to S12 while other groups edit the checklist); keeping "Tiers 0, 11" with a red-until-S11 note like 0079's S14 (describes a run the spec lane never performs).
FACT: the gates the staged spec breaks are `claimOwnershipRowSentence` and `claimWriterSetCell` (requireCellEquals, byte-exact) in tests/tier11_docs/spec_28_register_writers_test.go, the 0067 staged-block comparison in tests/tier11_docs/spec_28_index_rows_test.go (`stagedLineSupersessions` holds only 0079's REG-CLAIM entry), and the "publishes the step-2 `Shutdown` request" literal in tests/tier11_docs/redis_key_prefix_registry_test.go — EVIDENCE: spec_28_register_writers_test.go:104-108, :138-140, :764, :767; spec_28_index_rows_test.go:1073-1088, applySupersessions; redis_key_prefix_registry_test.go:119-121; proposals/0067_...:537, :539.
WATCHOUT: `applySupersessions` applies register entries in slice order to the running line, so the 0093 REG-CLAIM entry must come after 0079's and take 0079's `to` ("and the deletes at hold expiry and when an acquisition ends the hold,") as its `from`; a `from` copied from 0067's staged line is reported stale. SPEC-1h and SPEC-2i touch §28 text outside 0067's staged block and need no entry — EVIDENCE: tests/tier11_docs/spec_28_index_rows_test.go applySupersessions.
OPEN: the second half of Standing context Deferred item 2 and of the OPEN [tier-11 gates and assertions] (extend TEST-1's tier-11 assertion list to SPEC-1g to SPEC-1j, SPEC-2j to SPEC-2o, SPEC-2t, and the pointer edits) is not discharged here; it is test-inventory work for a later group. A blanket "describes a set" assertion would be false for SPEC-1g (a diagram label) and SPEC-1h. The retarget half of both entries is discharged and compaction can retire it.
FACT: CODE-4's deletion of `pkg/gateway/session/createdsweeper/` breaks `TestCommittedManifestDestinationsExist` unless the manifest move line goes too; the unit tier's `go test ./...` covers scripts/refactor (single root module), so S7's existing tier 1 is the check — EVIDENCE: scripts/refactor/manifest:80; scripts/refactor/manifest_layout_test.go:59-66; cmd/lenny-test/cmd_run.go:283. CODE-4 Targets, Files touched, and the summary Deliverable index CODE-4 line now name `scripts/refactor/manifest`.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G4.1]
FACT: At S5 (before CODE-3) `failFinalizing(..., true)` sets `from = failed`, so the hook skips its reclaim, and the pre-Prepare exits have already deleted the claim by name via `reclaimFinalizedPod`; a Part A `failFinalizing` row cannot pass on a concurrent pool with a sibling until CODE-3 lands. On a concurrent pool the only reachable pre-Prepare exit is the `ResolvePool` failure (prepareAtFinalize returns nil,nil for mcs>1 right after it). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_terminal.go failFinalizing (`if podReclaimed`); pkg/gateway/sessionserver/finalize.go prepareAtFinalize
FACT: full_revoke today reaches only `AfterRevokeWrite` (CancelResumeAttempt, drainMessagingOnTerminal); no pre-running reclaim exists until CODE-7 adds `ReclaimRevokedPreRunning`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go terminateUserSessions; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go AfterRevokeWrite
DECISION: Removed `failFinalizing` and full_revoke from CODE-2 Part A's rows; Part B (CODE-3) runs its applied column on Part A's pool columns, and CODE-7's tier-1 `admin` bullet tests `ReclaimRevokedPreRunning` ordering and Part A's assertions — BECAUSE each row must land with the deliverable that makes it pass — ALTERNATIVES: "after CODE-n" tags inside Part A (one table across three steps, skips at S5); moving Part A to S10 (CODE-2 lands untested).
FACT: No test drives `rollbackBinding` with a slot result; TEST-1 tier 2 calls `SlotClaimer.ReleaseSlot(recycle=false)` below the binder and cannot see whether `rollbackBinding` forwards `result.Recycle`. `Server.podBinder` is the concrete `*podsession.Binder`, so a tier-1 test over a fake client and miniredis observes the `recycling` patch and `ShutdownRecycle`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_rollback.go rollbackBinding; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go Binder.ReleaseSlot
WATCHOUT: The `rollbackBinding` test only discriminates on a `recycle.enabled` pool; on a non-recycling pool old and new code agree. Callers (start.go, create_and_start.go, delegated_child_materialize.go) return right after the rollback, so asserting the caller's `BindResult.Recycle` is unchanged has no observer and was not staged. — EVIDENCE: grep 'rollbackBinding(' pkg/gateway/sessionserver
OPEN: Deferred item 6 (recycle axis on Part A's exclusive column, row D1) is still unauthored; this group did not take it.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G5.1]
DECISION: Deferred item 5 (CREATE-loser test) is discharged: the CODE-1 tier-1 `podclaim` bullet now splits rollbacks by row (D7 asserts its Claim cell; D8 asserts its cells for each CREATE error class against a winner's claim in `bound`, `recycling`, `reserved`, with the set-emptying withdrawal as the discriminating case), and TEST-1 Tier 7a adds a two-claimer CREATE race with the winner live — BECAUSE the old "claim is deleted only when the set is then empty" asserted D7's disposition on the D8 path, so routing the CREATE withdrawal through `ReleaseSlot` passed every test — ALTERNATIVES: requiring the 7a race to empty the set (needs injected rehydration with a live winner; and if the winner releases first the lifecycleharness no-orphan-claim invariant flags the claim D8 correctly leaves unless `RelaxOrphanClaims` is set).
WATCHOUT: a 7a scenario in which the CREATE winner's session ends before the loser withdraws trips the harness orphan-claim check on a claim D8 legitimately leaves; opt into `RelaxOrphanClaims` or keep the winner live — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness/checker.go (`h.RelaxOrphanClaims`), harness.go `RelaxOrphanClaims`.
DECISION: CODE-1 item 4's every-construction sentence now also sets `OnRehydrate` to `b.Rehydration`, and the tier-1 `podsession` bullet asserts one `Binder.Rehydration` call per release through `ReleaseSlotReservation` and `Binder.ReleaseSlot` with the flag absent — BECAUSE those two constructions set no `OnRehydrate`, so item 3's release-path `recordRehydration` emits nothing — ALTERNATIVES: a podclaim-level test (supplies its own callback, cannot detect the missing wiring); one Binder helper building every SlotClaimer (wider restructure than the finding needs).
FACT: podsession builds `SlotClaimer` at resume.go `reserveResumeSlot` and slotbinder.go `connectSlot` (both wire `OnRehydrate: b.Rehydration`), and at slotbinder.go `ReleaseSlotReservation` and `Binder.ReleaseSlot` (neither does); cmd/lenny-gateway/metricsbackfill.go sets `podBinder.Rehydration = gwMetrics.IncSlotRehydration` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:659-665, :768, :822-830; resume.go:295-301.
OPEN: row D8 does not state that when the winner's session released while the loser's member was still counted, the loser's withdrawal empties the set and leaves the winner's claim with no occupancy for the GC; **Races the table does not close** omits it too. The G5 design marked it separate-finding for the table owner; not edited here.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G6.1]
DECISION: TEST-1 Tier 4 resume-family bullet now names the running-state takeover, then a pod failure on the adopter, then `resuming`, then a DELETE on the old coordinator; the discriminating assertion is no seal RPC through the stale binding — BECAUSE within one attempt releaseThenResumeOnPod calls releaseEarlierBinding before entryHook.run writes `resuming`, so the old "DELETE between `resuming` and releaseEarlierBinding" window does not exist — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "peer adopts resume_pending while old coordinator still holds the binding" (unreachable: the old coordinator's failure funnel unbinds when it writes resume_pending); deleting the bullet (leaves cross-replica reachability of CODE-5's branch untested above tier 1).
FACT: the resume driver releases first and writes `resuming` later, in the binder's post-claim hook — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go:144-148, resume_driver.go:300-312
FACT: before CODE-5, releaseTerminalBinding already evicts a stale binding without a release (terminal_binding.go:71-75), so "pod released once" or "no leak" does not tell old behavior from new. The adopter's funnel already released the pod, and CODE-1 idempotence makes CODE-5's duplicate a no-op. Only the seal step still acts on the stale binding before CODE-5 — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:57-59
WATCHOUT: Do not write a resume-family test whose main assertion is "released once" or "does not leak to the GC". It passes with and without CODE-5 — EVIDENCE: terminal_binding.go:71-75
OPEN: Summary Decision 15's reason ("`MarkTerminated`'s `resuming` bump makes the earlier pod's binding look stale and no adopter holds that pod") does not describe a reachable state. A same-replica binding never exists at `resuming`. In the reachable case the handoff bump made the binding stale, and the adopter held the pod and then released it. A later round should file this as a finding and re-decide evict versus release for CODE-5's resume-family branch (CODE-5 item 2).

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-7 item 5 derives `callCtx` as `context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), userTerminateRPCTimeout)` per binding, and the false "binder's claim, slot, and lease effects keep their own detached contexts" clause is deleted — BECAUSE on the handling replica `ctx` is the admin request context (users.go `runFullRevokeFanOut(req.Context(), ...)`), and `PodExecutor.Release` unbinds before the binder runs, so a cancel after unbind loses the exclusive claim delete (drain is `podclaim.DeleteClaim(ctx, ...)`, not detached) and turns a concurrent slot's `Shutdown` into a `leaked:` member; the Sweeper cannot recover either because `boundHere` is false once unbound. SPEC-3b's "Because each local release is bounded" requires the timeout, so the release cannot simply run on `s.lifetime()` with no deadline — ALTERNATIVES: (a) run `releaseTerminalBinding` on `s.lifetime()` like D6, deleting item 4's context parameter (rejected: the adapter `Shutdown` then has no deadline, since item 6 drops `userTerminateDeadline` and a zero deadline makes the adapter use the gRPC context, so the admin call is unbounded against SPEC-3b); (b) make `Binder.Release`'s retire-path `Get` and `drain` run on `detachedReleaseContext` so even timeout expiry keeps the claim delete (rejected for this round: it changes D3a for every caller and its table cell; recorded as an OPEN); (c) a bounded wait with the release continuing in the background (rejected: a second release mechanism, hair).
FACT: the per-binding `callCtx` is created inside the `terminateLocal` loop, so each binding gets a fresh 20 s budget; the peer path's `ctx` is the propagator `Run` context (propagator.go `apply` -> `TerminateLocal(ctx, ...)`), so only the handling replica is exposed to request cancellation — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:129, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podterminate/propagator/propagator.go:141,156
FACT: a `Shutdown` with `DeadlineMs` 0 makes the adapter bound `Runtime.Close` by the incoming gRPC context only (`contextWithGraceDeadline` returns the parent for grace <= 0), so the gateway's `callCtx` is the effective bound on the runtime close — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:459, :535-540
WATCHOUT: timeout expiry (not cancellation) still cuts an exclusive release after unbind: a `Shutdown` that runs the full `userTerminateRPCTimeout` leaves the `Sandbox` `Get` and claim delete on an expired context. The design records it as a GC-bounded departure in **Releases no replica performs**; a conforming runtime closes within the §15.4.6 5 s shutdown check (cmd/lenny-compliance/main.go:698), so it is a hung-adapter case. On a concurrent pool the same expiry yields a `leaked:` member, which is the spec'd leaked slot rather than a departure — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go:115-120, reclaim.go:141-143, slotbinder.go:813-814
OPEN: whether `Binder.Release`'s retire path should run its `Get` and `drain` on `detachedReleaseContext` for every caller (closing the timeout departure above and making D3a's claim delete detached like D1). It is a disposition-table change (row D3a cell) and belongs with the table, not with CODE-7.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: Row D10 (claimIdle leaves the `bound` claim when StampPodTenant fails) is an accepted departure filed with N2 by REC-1 item 5, with its reason folded into summary Decision 11; the summary Defects bullet for D10 is deleted — BECAUSE claimPod/claimIdle runs on mcs >1 through Binder.Resume, and ClaimSlot Pass 1 joins any found, non-terminal, tenant-matching claim, so a by-name delete after writeBoundStatus succeeded can drain a pod a sibling reserved onto (the same reason Decision 11 gives for N2); an exclusive-only delete would add a pool-kind branch for a case the claim-orphan GC bounds and that fails closed (a joining sibling's reserveSlot stamps its own tenant label, so no session runs on an unlabelled pod) — ALTERNATIVES: CODE-2 delete mirroring fallbackClaim (reintroduces the unguarded delete WATCHOUT spec.1.fix-G2.1 forbids, on the Resume path); mcs 1-only delete (pool-kind conditional, hair); keeping D10 in the Defects list as well as item 5 (double filing).
DECISION: REC-1 item 6 drops "each with its owner" rather than inventing owners for the retried-/start, occupancy-zero-race, Counter.Reset, and S17 entries — BECAUSE a BUILD-GAPS filing needs no owner and no one has decided those owners; entries that name one keep it.
DECISION: Disposition-table Invariants 1 and 2 are deleted rather than replaced by a pointer line — BECAUSE the table preface already names SPEC-2c as the rule the table instantiates and the **Claim** column's "At zero" definition already cites SPEC-1a's removal gate, so a pointer line would be a third statement of where the rule lives.
DECISION: The race mechanisms have one home, non-spec **Races the table does not close**; the summary keeps one pointer bullet and REC-1 item 6 takes each race's text from that list; the Pass 1 race's owner ("the recycling follow-up") moves into Races item 2.
FACT: fallbackClaim (podsession/fallbackclaim.go) is reached through claimPod too (claimconnect.go claimPod -> fallbackClaim), so row D9's deletes run on mcs >1 through Resume — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/claimconnect.go:44,68; resume.go:183.
OPEN: Row D9 records by-name deletes (claimIdle after writeBoundStatus fails; fallbackClaim after its stamp/status failures) on mcs >1 through Binder.Resume. ClaimSlot Pass 1 accepts an empty-phase or `bound` tenant-matching claim (slotclaimer.go Pass 1 `!found || claimstate.IsTerminal(...)`), so these are the same unguarded delete Decision 11 calls unsafe for N2. Pre-existing and not staged; a later round should decide whether D9 joins the Races list or the departures.
USEFUL [remedy.mechanism-table.2026-10-16]: the WATCHOUT on unguarded by-name deletes decided D10 without new analysis.
NOTE for compaction: Deferred item 7 (Row D10) is discharged by this design once applied.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
FACT: A spec-lane checklist step runs no test tier and commits only the spec/ files its SPEC-n deliverables target. Its "Tiers 0, 11" line is never executed by implement-proposal, and a tests/ retarget cannot ride in a spec step. EVIDENCE: .claude/workflows/implement-proposal-build.js runSpecStep (the "commit only the files under spec/ this step touched" constraint; no tier run), and the dispatch "A spec-lane step applies staged specification text; it does not build code".
FACT: The 0067 staged-block gate compares the staged lines as an ordered subsequence of spec/28, so a 0093 edit needs a `stagedLineSupersessions` entry only when it rewrites a line inside 0067's staged block. Of 0093's §28 edits, only SPEC-1e (REG-SLOTCOUNT row) and SPEC-2g (REG-CLAIM row) do. SPEC-1h (§28.6) and SPEC-2i (§28.5.1 Degradation) text is absent from 0067's block. EVIDENCE: tests/tier11_docs/spec_28_index_rows_test.go applySupersessions and stagedSectionMismatch; proposals/0067_...md:537, :539.
FACT: Supersession entries apply in register order to the running staged copy, so the 0093 REG-CLAIM entry chains after 0079's: its `from` is 0079's `to` phrase ("and the deletes at hold expiry and when an acquisition ends the hold,"). EVIDENCE: spec_28_index_rows_test.go applySupersessions.
DECISION: The three tier-11 gate retargets (two supersession entries, `claimOwnershipRowSentence` and `claimWriterSetCell`, and the §11.4 "step-2 `Shutdown` request" literal) land in TEST-1's **Tier 11 (docs).** bullet at S11, and S1 to S3 declare "Tiers 0." BECAUSE the spec lane cannot commit tests/, no step between S3 and S11 runs tier 11, and TEST-1 already edits redis_key_prefix_registry_test.go. ALTERNATIVES: retargets inside S1 to S3 (rejected: the spec handler commits spec/ only); a new test-lane step right after S3 (rejected for this round: it renumbers S4 to S12 while other groups edit the same checklist, for a shorter red window that no executed tier observes); 0079's "leaves the case red until S19" annotation on the spec steps (rejected: hair, and it describes a run that never happens).
WATCHOUT: A `spec-only` implement-proposal run (close-build-gaps --mode proposals) lands S1 to S3 and stops, leaving those three tier-11 gates red until S11 lands. No placement fixes this, because only a spec step precedes the stop and it cannot commit tests/. EVIDENCE: .claude/skills/implement-proposal/SKILL.md (`spec-only` paragraph).
DECISION: CODE-4 gains `scripts/refactor/manifest` (delete the `createdsweeper` move line) in its targets and in Files touched; S7's "Tiers 0, 1" already covers TestCommittedManifestDestinationsExist, so no tier changes. BECAUSE that test stats every destination and runs in the root module's `go test ./...`. EVIDENCE: scripts/refactor/manifest:80, scripts/refactor/manifest_layout_test.go TestCommittedManifestDestinationsExist. A grep for createdsweeper|createdSweeper|CreatedSweep outside the package finds no other unlisted site except BUILD-GAPS.md (REC-1's).
OPEN: Deferred item 2's second sentence (extend the TEST-1 tier-11 set assertions to SPEC-1g to SPEC-1j and SPEC-2j to SPEC-2l) is not closed by G3. It is a test-coverage widening for the test-inventory groups. Trap: SPEC-1h's replacement ("ceilings a pod's concurrent slots") names no set, and SPEC-1g is a diagram label, so a blanket "every SPEC-1 site describes a set" assertion is false for 1h.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: CODE-2 Part A keeps only the pre-running writers that reach `terminalReclaimPreRunning` at S5 (DELETE, terminate, `cancel_child`, the watchdog's three timeouts). `failFinalizing` moves into CODE-3 Part B by a clause that runs Part B's applied column on Part A's pool columns with Part A's assertions. full_revoke moves into CODE-7's tier-1 `admin` bullet as one sentence pointing at CODE-7 item 1's order and Part A's assertions — BECAUSE at S5 `failFinalizing` still takes `podReclaimed` (finalize.go `finalizePrepareFailed` passes true after the pre-Prepare `reclaimFinalizedPod` by-name delete, so from=failed skips the reclaim and the concurrent-with-sibling cell sees the shared claim gone), and full_revoke reaches no reclaim until CODE-7 adds `ReclaimRevokedPreRunning` (users.go only calls `AfterRevokeWrite`) — ALTERNATIVES: keeping both rows in Part A tagged "after CODE-3"/"after CODE-7" (staggered rows in one table landing at three steps; S5's run would need skips); moving all of Part A to S10 (CODE-2 would land untested).
DECISION: The rollbackBinding test asserts rows D18 and D18b by citation, with "no `recycling` patch and no recycle `Shutdown`" named as the discriminating assertion; it does NOT assert that the caller's `BindResult.Recycle` is unchanged — BECAUSE every caller of `rollbackBinding` (start.go, create_and_start.go, delegated_child_materialize.go) returns after it and the result is unregistered, so an in-place clear has no observer; the reviewer's suggested restatement of the D18b cells would be a second statement of the table.
FACT: `Server.podBinder` is the concrete `*podsession.Binder` (bind_rollback.go, admission_ratelimit.go reads `.Client`), so a tier-1 `sessionserver` test of `rollbackBinding` runs the real `Binder.ReleaseSlot` over a fake client, miniredis, and a fake adapter; no binder fake can hide a forwarded `Recycle`. No test calls `rollbackBinding` with a slot result today. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_rollback.go `rollbackBinding`; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go `ReleaseSlot` passes `result.Recycle` then `ShutdownRecycle`.
WATCHOUT: Standing-context Deferred item 6 (Part A's exclusive column on both recycle settings) also edits the Part A bullet. This group did not add the recycle axis; whoever lands item 6 edits the shortened row list. On concurrent columns a recycle axis tests a constant (`ReclaimClaimed(perSlot=true)` passes `recycle=false`), so only the exclusive column discriminates.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
DECISION: D8 tests: tier 1 `podclaim` constructs the set-emptying withdrawal deterministically (winner's claim in `bound`, `recycling`, `reserved`; each CREATE error class); tier 7a only races two `SlotClaimer`s on one fresh pod and asserts the loser makes no claim write — BECAUSE emptying the set with a live winner needs the winner's member absent, which in a randomized run only arises through the rehydration departure (a create reserves before persisting its row), so the emptying condition belongs in the deterministic tier — ALTERNATIVES: a 7a case that also requires the loser's withdrawal to empty the set (rejected: needs injected rehydration, and if the winner's session released first the harness checker's no-orphan-claim invariant fails on the D8 claim).
WATCHOUT: a 7a CREATE-race scenario in which the winner's session releases before the loser withdraws leaves a claim with an empty set and no non-terminal row (winner's release saw remaining 1; D8 does not dispose). The lifecycleharness checker flags that as an orphan claim unless the scenario keeps the winner live or opts into `RelaxOrphanClaims` — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness/checker.go:195.
FACT: the live-winner, empty-set D8 case is real: loser's `Counter.Reserve` hits `-2`, rehydrates from rows that do not yet carry the winner's session (create reserves before persisting), adds only its own member, then CREATE fails AlreadyExists and the withdrawal empties the set. A `ReleaseSlot` there would delete or patch the live winner's claim — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:710-718; spec-changes.md **A reseed drops leaked members and unpersisted reservations (departure).**
DECISION: release-path rehydration: extend CODE-1 item 4's existing "Every `SlotClaimer` construction in `podsession`" sentence to also set `OnRehydrate: b.Rehydration`, and pin wiring plus pool label in the tier-1 `podsession` bullet — BECAUSE a `podclaim` test with `OnRehydrate` set cannot see the missing wiring; only a test through `ReleaseSlotReservation` and `Binder.ReleaseSlot` discriminates — ALTERNATIVES: one `Binder` helper building every `SlotClaimer` with all callbacks (coherent, prevents the next drift, but restructures four constructions beyond what the finding needs); putting the wiring sentence in item 3 (splits "every construction sets X" across two items).
FACT: `podsession` builds `SlotClaimer` in four places: slotbinder.go connectSlot (:659, sets OnRehydrate), ReleaseSlotReservation (:768, bare), Binder.ReleaseSlot (:822, RecycleBoundary only), resume.go reserveResumeSlot (:295, sets OnRehydrate). cmd/lenny-gateway/metricsbackfill.go:156 wires `podBinder.Rehydration = gwMetrics.IncSlotRehydration`.
OPEN: CODE-1 Targets names "the `SlotClaimer` constructions in that file" (slotbinder.go) while item 4 requires `RollbackTimeout` on every construction in `podsession`, which includes resume.go:295. Targets omits resume.go. Separate finding for a later round.
OPEN: row D8 leaves the winner's claim with no occupancy when the winner's session released while the loser's member was counted (winner's release saw remaining 1); the GC reclaims it. Neither the D8 row nor **Races the table does not close** names that outcome. Candidate finding for the table owner.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G6.1]
DECISION: Replace TEST-1 Tier 4 fourth bullet with the takeover-then-adopter-failure interleaving (peer takes over a running session after the old coordinator's lease lapses, the pod fails on the peer, the peer's resume driver writes `resuming`, DELETE handled on the old coordinator), naming "no seal RPC from the old coordinator to the earlier pod" as the discriminating assertion — BECAUSE it is the one resume-family stale-binding interleaving the tree produces — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "peer adopts a `resume_pending` row while the old coordinator still holds the earlier binding" (rejected: unreachable, see FACT below); deleting the bullet and relying on the CODE-5 tier-1 table (rejected: tier 4 is where multi-replica reachability of the branch is shown, and the replacement is the same length).
FACT: Within one resume attempt `releaseEarlierBinding` always runs before the `resume_pending → resuming` entry write, so no DELETE can land between them on the driver's replica. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go `attemptResume` (calls `releaseThenResumeOnPod`), resume_rebind.go `releaseThenResumeOnPod` (release, then `resumeOnPod`), resume_driver.go `entryHook.run` (the entry write, called by the binder after its claim).
FACT: A replica cannot keep a binding across its own move to `resume_pending`/`awaiting_client_action`: `applyFailureFromActive` releases the snapshotted binding under `slotAccountLocks`, and `PodExecutor.Release` unbinds before calling the binder, so even a binder error drops the registry entry. The resuming-timeout watchdog and `enterResumePending` write `resume_pending` only from states with no published binding. So a stale binding seen with a resume-family `fromState` lives on a replica that lost a running-state takeover (`RecordHandoff` bumps the generation, `PodHeld` re-adopt), whose pod then failed on the adopter. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go `applyFailureFromActive`; pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go `PodExecutor.Release`; pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go `sweepResuming`; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go `RecordHandoff`, `adoptedState`.
FACT: tests/tier4_integration/coordination_fence_split_brain_test.go already models a two-replica lease lapse and a takeover Sweep; the new bullet and Tier 4 bullet 3 can share that fixture.
WATCHOUT: In the reachable interleaving the adopter's failure funnel already released the earlier pod with the failed disposition, so CODE-5's resume-family `releaseEarlierBinding` on the old coordinator is a duplicate release. CODE-1's per-session idempotence and the exclusive pod's retirement make it a no-op, so "released once" does not tell CODE-5 apart from the pre-CODE-5 hook. The assertion that does tell them apart is the absence of a seal (Checkpoint) RPC through the stale binding. EVIDENCE: terminal_binding.go `releaseTerminalBinding`; failure.go `applyFailureFromActive`.
OPEN: summary.md Decision 15 gives the reason for the resume-family release as "`MarkTerminated`'s `resuming` bump makes the earlier pod's binding look stale and no adopter holds that pod". Within the tree no same-replica binding exists while a row is `resuming`, and in the reachable case the handoff bump already made the binding stale while the adopter held, and then released, that pod. Whether the branch should evict rather than release, and whether Decision 15's reason holds, is a separate finding for a later round. G6 does not change it.

DECISION: kept the round-1 choice (REC-1 files D10 rather than CODE-2 deleting the claim) — BECAUSE "keep the original fix"; the finding was the false rationale, not the choice — ALTERNATIVES: staging a CODE-2 delete mirroring `fallbackClaim` (a reframe of the round-1 resolution of Standing context Deferred item 7; not taken here).
OPEN: whether D10 should instead get a by-name delete. On mcs 1 it is as safe as row D9's `writeBoundStatus` delete one step earlier; on mcs >1 through `Binder.Resume` the sibling-join argument applies equally to D9's delete, so D9 and D10 differ only by what the tree does today. A later round or the human decides whether the proposal fixes D10 or leaves it filed.
WATCHOUT: CODE-1 item 4 cites "the reason Decision 11 gives" for the `reserveSlot` rollbacks, but Decision 11's wording names "the failed resume reservation" (N2-specific). The citation predates round 1 and was left unchanged; a reviewer may flag the mismatch. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 4.
FACT: the citer sweep for "Decision 11" and "D10" across all proposal files found only summary Decision 11, summary **Rejected alternatives** (N2 only, true), non-spec rows D9/D10, REC-1 item 5, CODE-1 item 4, and the edge-case departure item. ### [non-spec.1.review-security.1]
DECISION: filed no security finding in non-spec round 1 — BECAUSE CODE-7 keeps the shipped fleet-wide durable token rejection (lease record removal) and strengthens it: `Client.Release` removes the record even when the Token Service revoke RPC fails, and `Service.Release` never errors; the slot set stays gateway-sourced (session IDs, not pod self-report); every new release outcome fails closed or lands in a recorded departure — ALTERNATIVES: filing the lease-minted-between-step-1-and-cancel window (rejected: today's post-cancel enumeration has the mirror window for post-enumeration mints, and the session's release still removes the record, so the change only shifts the window).
FACT: `Client.Release` calls `c.leases.Remove(leaseID)` unconditionally after the revoke RPC, so CODE-7 item 7's "a Release error still counts" never leaves a live lease record — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:363-377
FACT: the LLM proxy rejects a deny-listed credential (CREDENTIAL_REVOKED) before any upstream call, so step 1's deny write cannot itself trigger the §4.9 Fallback Flow mint; only an upstream fault on a request already in flight can, and `proxyFallbackRotator.Rotate` checks no session state — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go:324-347,745-749; cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_fallback.go:41-57
FACT: renewal never re-mints a lease whose record step 1 removed (`Renew` returns ErrLeaseReleased on a missing record and releases a replacement minted concurrently) — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:174-215
FACT: after CODE-7, `RevokeCredentials` on the Token Service emits `token.revoked` with reason "session_released" for full_revoke lease returns (Client.revoke hard-codes it); today full_revoke emits no Token Service receipt at all, so this is an addition rather than a regression — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:382-400; pkg/tokenservice/grpc.go:196-217
WATCHOUT: CODE-2 item 7 (row D18b) changes a recycling concurrent pool's last release of an unregistered binding from a `recycling` patch (whole-pod scrub) to a claim delete. When the pod already projects `claimed` that drains (stricter); in the pre-projection window it joins the spec-changes departure "A release delete before the pod projects `claimed`", whose "the tree deletes on these paths" is not true of D18b at 85c9ab19a. Judged too narrow to file (tenant pin kept, per-slot cleanup still runs) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_rollback.go:20-28; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:831
WATCHOUT: a disposition skipped after the member is removed (patch failure, or the new lost-reply retry in CODE-1 item 5) leaves a `bound` claim on an empty set, and Pass 1 rejoins it for the same tenant without the whole-pod scrub, before the claim-orphan GC can act. This predates 0093 (today's single-attempt release has the same outcome) and stays same-tenant — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go Pass 1
UNVERIFIED: no lens has checked whether docs/operator-guide/security-principles.md:37 and docs/operator-guide/multi-tenancy.md:68-72 (the unconditional "whole-pod scrub runs when occupancy reaches zero") are in the docs deliverable that Standing context Deferred item 1 still owes. The docs lens should fold them in when it stages that deliverable.
USEFUL [spec.2.fix-G2.1 FACT]: both `Release` implementations are no-ops for an unknown lease ID, which settled the double-release question for CODE-7 step 1 against the session's later `ReleaseThrough`.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: Part B's applied column and the plan-parse store-error column import Part A's assertions as end states of the claim, slot set, and lease rather than call counts; the clause "Assert one release per resource after the write applies" is deleted — BECAUSE on the applied prepare-failure row the lease is released by the hook's ReclaimClaimed and again by CODE-3 item 5's unconditional revokeFinalizeLease, and after a through-Prepare failure also by failPhase when leaseAssigned (2 or 3 ReleaseSession calls; two by-name deletes, the second finding no claim) — ALTERNATIVES: "expects two ReleaseSession calls" plus limiting the row to pre-Prepare exits (count is three with leaseAssigned, and drops through-Prepare coverage); removing item 5's revoke (it is the only lease return when failFinalizing commits and reports a store error).
WATCHOUT: Part A's call counts ("one by-name claim delete and one ReleaseSession") hold only where the hook is the sole releaser. Do not import them as counts onto any finalize exit that also runs revokeFinalizeLease or failPhase. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/finalize.go finalizePrepareFailed (failFinalizing then revokeFinalizeLease); pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go failPhase, ReclaimClaimed.
FACT: finalizePlanParseFailed runs before prepareAtFinalize on every pool kind, so its store-error reclaimFinalizedPod (CODE-3 item 4) reaches concurrent pools; the discriminating test cell is concurrent with a live sibling (claim intact). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/finalize_handler.go storedWorkspacePlanForFinalize / finalizePlanParseFailed before prepareAtFinalize; finalize.go finalizePlanParseFailed store-error branch.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Part B (non-spec-changes Testing, "Tier 1, finalize exits (Part B)") asserts Part A's assertions as end states of the claim, the slot set, and the lease rather than as call counts, on the applied column and on the plan-parse row's store-error column; the "one release per resource after the write applies" clause is deleted — BECAUSE on the applied prepare-failure row the hook's ReclaimClaimed and CODE-3 item 5's unconditional handler revoke both call ReleaseSession, and after a through-Prepare failure row D15's failPhase has already deleted the claim (and released credentials when leaseAssigned) before the hook's by-name delete, so any call count is wrong or incidental — ALTERNATIVES: "expect two ReleaseSession calls" and limit the row to pre-Prepare exits (rejected: through-Prepare with leaseAssigned makes three calls, the count pins an incidental detail, and it drops through-Prepare coverage); dropping the handler revoke on an applied write (rejected: CODE-3 item 5 keeps it as the only lease return when failFinalizing commits and reports a store error).
FACT: The second by-name claim delete after a through-Prepare failure (failPhase, then the applied failure write's hook once CODE-3 item 1 removes podReclaimed) is benign: the first delete happens while the pod projects `claimed`, so the pod projects draining → terminated (§4.6.1 Occupancy projection) and no successor can recreate claim-<podName> before the hook's delete. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go failPhase; spec/04_system-components.md Occupancy projection bullet "draining, then terminated ... deleted while the pod projects claimed"; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_terminal.go failFinalizing
FACT: Through-Prepare finalize failures exist only on exclusive pools: prepareAtFinalize returns (nil, nil) for mcs>1 right after ResolvePool, so the concurrent prepare-failure cell is the ResolvePool failure alone, while finalizePlanParseFailed runs before prepareAtFinalize on every pool kind. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/finalize.go prepareAtFinalize (`match.MaxConcurrentSessions > 1`); finalize_handler.go storedWorkspacePlanForFinalize before prepareAtFinalize
WATCHOUT: "Part A's assertions" are call counts that hold only where the hook is the sole releaser. Any later bullet that borrows them for a path with a second releaser (handler revoke, failPhase, D6) must borrow them as end states. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Part A bullet; CODE-3 item 5
USEFUL [non-spec.2.review-mechanism WATCHOUT on importing Part A's assertions]: named both the handler revoke and failPhase traps.

### [non-spec.2.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: On the exclusive executor release, `Binder.Release` runs the adapter `Shutdown` and then the Sandbox `Get` and the claim delete (`drain`) on the same caller context, with no detached context of its own. The new non-spec edge case (a full_revoke release whose `Shutdown` outlasts `userTerminateRPCTimeout` leaves the claim delete on an expired context) is accurate. The plain retire `Shutdown` passes deadline 0, and the adapter then bounds `Runtime.Close` only by the gRPC request context (`contextWithGraceDeadline` returns the parent when grace <= 0). A slow runtime close therefore reaches the 20s cap, and the GC-bounded departure follows. FACT: A loser of the claim `CREATE` race (row D8) writes no claim even when the retry joins the winner's claim. The non-fresh `reserveSlot` path stamps only the pod (`StampPodTenant`, `StampMaxPodUptime`) and does not call `writeBoundStatus`, so the new tier-7a "loser makes no write" assertion is satisfiable. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:703-765

FACT: On an exclusive pool, `Binder.Release` runs the plain `Shutdown`, the Sandbox `Get`, and the drain (claim delete) on the caller's context, with no detached context of its own. On a concurrent pool, the claimer call runs on `detachedReleaseContext` under CODE-1 item 5. That asymmetry is why CODE-7 item 5's new edge case names only the exclusive claim. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go:67-120; pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:269-284
FACT: Shipped `terminateLocal` is already a serial loop with a separate 20s `callCtx` per registry entry, so CODE-7's `WithoutCancel` change adds no new serialization. The only behavior it removes is the early stop when the admin request is cancelled. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:111-140
FACT: Shipped `releaseTerminalBinding` ignores any caller context and uses `s.lifetime()`, so its D6 `Shutdown` has no deadline. CODE-7's 20s-bounded full_revoke release is therefore no less reliable than the shipped D6 path when an adapter hangs. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:56-79
WATCHOUT: Round-2 hunks add no per-session or per-request write to etcd, Postgres, or Redis beyond one `ReadPrimary` per locally bound revoked session. A later performance pass need not re-derive write rates for these hunks.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-7 item 7 is the one home of the Release-before-deny order and its one-clause reason; summary Decision 8 and the P5b impact row drop "after its deny-list write" rather than restating the reversed order — BECAUSE Decision 8's reason justifies returning the lease at step 1, not the order inside the step — ALTERNATIVES: flipping the phrase in Decision 8 and the P5b row (a second and third statement of an ordering whose home is item 7); restaging SPEC-3a step 1 (it fixes no order inside the step, so no spec edit is owed).
FACT: on the in-process path the deny list's revoke hook `directModeRevocationRotator.onRevoke` runs synchronously inside `Propagator.Revoke` -> `applyLocal`, lists `LeasesByCredential` from the shared lease store, and dispatches `proxyFallbackRotator.Rotate`, which mints with `AssignProto` and pushes `RotateCredentials` with no session-state check. `credassign.Service.Release` removes the lease record from that same store, so releasing first hides the revoked sessions' leases from the hook — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/revocation.go:121-129; pkg/gateway/credentials/credrenewal/propagator/propagator.go:151-152,240-249; cmd/lenny-gateway/credential_revocation.go:120-139; cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_fallback.go:41-80; pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:394-405
FACT: a `Release` error cannot co-occur with the rotate hook: the hook is wired only when `w.inProcessAssign != nil`, and `credassign.Service.Release` always returns nil, so item 7's "a Release error still counts the lease" never leaves a record for the hook to rotate — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/revocation.go:121; pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:394-405
WATCHOUT: with the in-memory lease store (no pgPool) each replica sees only its own leases, so a peer applying the pub/sub deny copy still rotates a revoked session's lease that peer minted. That is the C3 listing/granularity residual owned by P5b (Decision 18), not a CODE-7 defect; do not add a per-session guard to `onRevoke` here — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:74-90
DECISION: `ReleaseRevokedBinding`'s error contract is stated by its nil set ("this replica owed no release": non-terminal row, refused `startRelease`, no binding, evicted stale binding) with every other unreleased outcome an error, rather than by adding "executor error" to the list of error cases — BECAUSE the previous text enumerated the error cases ("A store read error") and this round found a missing member; a defined complement covers the lock-wait timeout on the bounded callCtx too — ALTERNATIVES: appending the executor error to the error enumeration (same answer one member longer); changing `releaseReportedBinding`'s signature in the proposal (left to the implementor; it has callers in failure.go and suspend_release.go).
CORRECTS [non-spec.2.review-reliability.1]: the FACT that a binder failure on the full_revoke path is reported as `(false, nil)` described CODE-7 item 4 before this round; item 4 now requires the executor release error to surface as `(false, err)`.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: Close the seal-loss filing gap with one sentence appended to REC-1 item 4: annotate F-10.1.33 by pointing at the non-spec edge case **A superseded coordinator's terminal write loses the seal**, with P2 as owner. This discharges Standing context Deferred item 8's seal-loss clause and OPEN [seal-loss filing] — BECAUSE F-10.1.33's resolution note already names P2 as owner of the forward, the superseded coordinator is an off-holder writer, and the edge-case bullet is the one home of the mechanism — ALTERNATIVES: a new numbered item between 4 and 5 (renumbers items 5-8 and breaks "filed by REC-1 item 6" in the disposition table's **Races the table does not close**); folding it into item 3 (item 3 carries GC bounds, and the seal loss has none); restating the mechanism in REC-1 as the reviewer suggested (a second statement of the bullet); a new summary Defects entry (that list holds pre-existing defects, and CODE-5 introduces this case).
FACT: The terminal hook's seal is a Checkpointer workspace checkpoint over the local binding, and the adapter's Checkpoint RPC carries no coordination generation. Today a stale binding still seals, so the loss is new with CODE-5's PodHeld eviction branch. The holder-side `ReleaseTerminalBinding` runs no seal, so the off-holder write already loses it, though the F-10.1.33 record does not say so — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go Checkpointer.Seal / snapshot (ErrNoBinding -> nil); pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go Server.Checkpoint (no generation check); pkg/adapter/sessionseal.go sealTarget (only the SealSession/UnsealSession idle-seal RPCs are fenced); pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go (no seal call).
WATCHOUT: Do not "fix" the loss by adding a seal to the D6 release or by letting the stale replica seal. Both are mechanism changes that belong to P2 with the off-holder forward. This finding only asks for a record of the loss.

### [non-spec.4.review-citations.1]
FACT: The round-3 rewrite of the CODE-3 Part B test bullet is accurate against the tree. On a concurrent pool `prepareAtFinalize` returns (nil, nil) right after `ResolvePool`, so the `ResolvePool` failure is the only prepare-failure exit there. On an exclusive pool, a Prepare failure reaches `failPhase` through `reclaimOnFailure`, `ReclaimClaimed` on a reconnect failure, or nothing on a slot-bind refusal. `finalizePrepareFailed` revokes the lease unconditionally. Call counts therefore differ by path, and the end-state assertions are the right ones. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/finalize.go:227-243, :420-422; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindprepare.go:78-103
FACT: The disposition-table "Tree at 85c9ab19a" cells for D1-D18b match the code. FACT: The CODE-7 targets and the current state match the staging: `leasesRevoked` is computed today inside `runFullRevokeFanOut` (users_invalidate.go:159), `userLeaseRevoker` is wired at adminrouter.go:869, the credassign `Release(leaseID string) error` exists (credassign.go:400, client.go:363), `sessionstore.ReadPrimary` takes (ctx, st, timeout, tenantID, id), and the adapter `Shutdown` handler (pkg/adapter/session.go:279) never reads the reason. — EVIDENCE: the cited lines

### [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]
FACT: In Token Service mode (the chart wires `--token-service-grpc-addr`, gateway-deployment.yaml:482), `credassign.Client.Release` is a synchronous RevokeCredentials RPC on a 5 s per-call timeout (client.go:112, :363-372, :390) through a breaker that opens only after 5 consecutive failures (core/subsystem/breaker.go:63), and it removes the local lease record only after the RPC returns (client.go:368-369). The shipped `RevokeUserLeases` is local-only (cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:184-191) and runs after the cancel writes. CODE-7 items 1 and 7 put one such RPC per lease ahead of the first cancel write — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:205, :214.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-3a step 1 (spec-changes.md:507) requires the lease return before any cancel write, so moving `Release` after the cancel writes needs a spec edit. A non-spec remedy that keeps the order writes every deny-list entry first and then runs the `Release` calls concurrently under one bound.
FACT: The per-unit write rates are unchanged or lower. The slot set uses the same per-pod key and O(1) set ops. Release-path rehydration adds at most one Postgres `GetActiveSlotsByPod` per pod per Redis restart, which is the same burst that reservations already produce. CODE-2 removes the shared-claim delete and the drain on concurrent pre-running reclaims, which lowers etcd and pod-churn writes. `slotAccountLocks` is per session (sessionserver.go:395), so CODE-6 serializes nothing across sessions. D6 `ReleaseTerminalBinding` runs on its own goroutine (terminal_binding.go:46-52), so the hook's new lock hold does not stall the Sweeper — EVIDENCE: slotcounter.go:327-388, terminal_binding.go:41-80.

### [non-spec.4.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings under the reliability lens in non-spec round 4 — BECAUSE the r3-to-r4 diff is only the CODE-3 Part B test bullet, and the recovery traces it touches hold: the plan-parse store-error reclaim plus a later finalizing-watchdog hook release is a keyed no-op on mcs>1 and a benign second by-name delete on mcs1 (the pod projected `claimed` long before /finalize, so the first delete drains it). A whole-proposal pass found no new defect in the retry, rehydration, lock-ordering, or full_revoke recovery paths — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing CODE-6 skipping the lock-free pre-running reclaim when the lifetime context is done (rejected: the proposal states the skip explicitly, and it falls under the process-exit departure, because runserver.go drains httpSrv before sessionSrv.Close); (2) filing D7's fresh-claim delete as newly gated on counter success (rejected: a counter error cannot prove removal, the claim is GC-bounded when no row names the pod, and the UNVERIFIED at non-spec.1.review-reliability already owns it).
FACT: the release-path rehydration (CODE-1 item 2) adds the releasing session's extra seed member only when that call itself seeds. When a peer holds the `rehydrating` SET NX lock, or seeded first (the flagSet early return), the release re-runs the script and finds no member. Every interleaving checked (two last releases; a release racing a Pass 1 reserve) still runs the occupancy-zero disposition at most once, and only where the set really empties — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:326-395
FACT: no holder of slotAccountLocks runs the post-terminal hook. transitionToFailed unlocks first (failure.go:507-512), applyFailureFromResuming takes no lock (failure.go:364-381), and completeResume holds deliveryLocks but calls no hook (resume_driver.go:577-614). The CODE-6 claim that it adds no lock cycle holds — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:248, :507
FACT: the watchdog's pre-running sweep is store-based across all tenants and runs the hook synchronously through OnSessionTerminal. Deleting the created sweeper (CODE-4) therefore leaves no crashed-replica gap for `created` rows — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:715-775, :1575-1578
FACT: the full_revoke lease revoker and the credential assigner share one lease store instance (`w.llmLeases`, Postgres-backed when pgPool is set), so CODE-7 item 7's `Release(leaseID)` on the admin replica finds a lease minted on a peer. Only the per-replica pool count drifts (N4) — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:74-83, :137, :148; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:868-869
USEFUL [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]: its CancelResumeAttempt FACT and its single-5s-budget FACT settled two traces without re-reading the resume driver.

### [non-spec.4.review-security.1]
FACT: The credential deny list that full_revoke writes is the `credrenewalprop.Propagator`, and on an in-process assigner it carries a synchronous revoke hook, `directModeRevocationRotator.onRevoke`. For a pool key, that hook marks the credential unselectable, lists every lease on the credential with `LeasesByCredential` (the shared Postgres store), and for each direct-delivery lease dispatches `proxyFallbackRotator.Rotate`. Rotate mints a replacement with `AssignProto` for the lease's session, with no session-state check, and pushes it with `RotateCredentials` to the binding when this replica holds one. The hook runs on every replica that applies the revocation. Any change to the order of full_revoke's deny-list step relative to the session teardown changes which live sessions receive a fresh direct-mode key. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/revocation.go:121-129; pkg/gateway/credentials/credrenewal/propagator/propagator.go:151-152, :239-249; cmd/lenny-gateway/credential_revocation.go:119-139; cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_fallback.go:41-80
WATCHOUT: CODE-7 item 7 orders the per-lease steps as deny then `Release`. Under that order, onRevoke still finds the revoked user's own lease and rotates the user's still-live direct-mode session onto a new pool credential before any cancel write. Today the local Shutdown precedes the deny-list write (users_invalidate.go:150-160), so the push reaches a session that is already torn down. Returning every listed lease before writing any deny entry removes those leases from the hook's enumeration. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:205, :214, :278; summary.md:22
UNVERIFIED: the full extent of the deny-list hook's effect on the in-memory (no-Postgres) multi-replica store, where a peer's lease never reaches the handling replica's `LeasesBySession`. Nobody runs that topology in production, but a P5b reviewer should confirm it.

### [spec.13.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-3a staged step 4 now reads "For a cancelled session that never started and whose pod binding the handling gateway replica does not hold, the gateway reclaims the pod or the slot that the session claimed, before that replica's step-2 releases." BECAUSE `terminalReclaimPreRunning` returns false when `lookupBinding` finds the session on this replica, so a pre-running session bound on the handling replica takes the step-2 release (row R2), a peer-bound one is reclaimed here and also released by the peer (row R3), and the reclaim must precede the handling replica's release (full_revoke table **Order** step 4, row D3b hazard). "Handling gateway replica" is the existing §11.4 Note term. ALTERNATIVES: dropping only "instead" (unconditional reclaim, brings back reclaim-after-release by-name delete); conditioning on "no binding on any replica" (false for R3); extending "before" to peer releases (a peer Sweeper's D6 can precede step 4(a), **Races the table does not close** item 3).
FACT: the pre-running reclaim's bound-session skip is in `terminalReclaimPreRunning` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go, the `if _, bound := s.lookupBinding(sess.ID); bound` guard), and it reads only this replica's registry. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:519

### [spec.13.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-3a step 4 is replaced in place with the predicate and the ordering the tree uses: the reclaim covers a never-started session whose pod binding the handling gateway replica does not hold, and runs before that replica's step-2 releases — BECAUSE `terminalReclaimPreRunning` returns false when `lookupBinding` finds the session (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go `terminalReclaimPreRunning`), so a handling-replica-bound pre-running session takes the step-2 release (row R2), and a reclaim evaluated after that release no longer sees the binding and deletes by name (full_revoke table **Order** step 4) — ALTERNATIVES: dropping "instead" only (an unconditional "also reclaims" re-creates the reclaim-after-release delete); folding the reclaim into step 2 (SPEC-3a fixes the step numbers, and CODE-7 item 8 maps the reclaim to step 4); conditioning on "no binding on any replica" (false for row R3, where the handling replica reclaims and the peer also releases).
WATCHOUT: "the handling gateway replica" is the term the §11.4 **Note** already uses; do not introduce "the replica handling the request" or a new definition. The step-4 "before" clause is the only spec statement of the reclaim-before-release order; the full_revoke table **Order** step 4 stays its rule home in the non-spec staging, and no other site restates it. EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md §11.4 **Note** ("On the handling gateway replica").
FACT: A peer's Sweeper D6 release can still precede step 4(a) for a peer-bound pre-running session; that is **Races the table does not close** item 3, so step 4's "before" names only the handling replica's releases on purpose. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md **Races the table does not close**.

### [spec.13.review-fresh.1]
DECISION: filed one finding, the SPEC-3a step 4 "never started ... instead reclaims" predicate against full_revoke table row R2. The citations and mechanism lenses filed the same defect this round. BECAUSE `terminalReclaimPreRunning` returns false when this replica holds a binding for a created, finalizing, or ready session (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:519-525), and step 2 already releases that binding. The smallest fix is to qualify step 4 with "whose pod binding no replica holds", which leaves R3 (a peer-held binding) as the recorded race in **Races the table does not close** item 3. ALTERNATIVES: reading "never started" as "the pod never accepted the start" (rejected: R2's pod accepted the launch, but the row is still `ready`, so a spec maintainer cannot settle the reading from the text).
FACT: the only production callers of `publishBinding` are `registerBinding` (from `/start`, start.go:91, after `launchOnPod`), delegated_child_materialize.go:213, create_and_start.go:530, and resume_driver.go:663. No finalize-time binding is published, so the usage.go:519-523 comment and full_revoke row R2's "SDK-warm preConnect pod attached at finalize" describe a path that is absent from the tree. The only reachable pre-running local binding is a `/start` between `registerBinding` and `commitStart`. EVIDENCE: grep publishBinding pkg/gateway/sessionserver
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: full_revoke table row R2 lists "an SDK-warm preConnect pod attached at finalize" as a binding on the handling replica. No finalize path calls `publishBinding` or `podRegistry.Put`, so that case does not exist. The only R2 case is a `/start` between `registerBinding` and `commitStart`.
FACT: §6.2 line 183 states that the `lenny.dev/state` label "transitions to `idle` only when the claim is deleted at hold expiry or when an acquisition ends the hold". SPEC-2c does not falsify it, because a no-reuse delete from `claimed` projects `draining`. The only path to `idle` is the pre-projection departure, which spec Edge cases records. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:414-415
USEFUL [spec.12.review-edit-sites.1 USER_REVOKED FACT]: it settled the dropped Shutdown reason without a re-grep of schemas.

FACT: SPEC-3a step 4 ("For a cancelled session that never started, the gateway instead reclaims ...") disagrees with full_revoke table row R2: a pre-running session bound on the handling replica is skipped by `terminalReclaimPreRunning` (`lookupBinding` hit returns false) and released by step 4(b) `ReleaseRevokedBinding`. Filed. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:519-525; non-spec-changes.md:108 (R2); spec-changes.md:510
FACT: no spec/ commit since 2e157af44, so the round-9 anchor FACTs still hold at aacee2a02. — EVIDENCE: git log 2e157af44..HEAD -- spec/ (empty)
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: the full_revoke table's **Order** lead says "No other site states the order", but SPEC-3a step 1 (after remedy 2) states both the lease-return-before-cancel order and the return-then-deny order in one clause each. True instead: SPEC-3a step 1 is the normative statement of those two orderings; the table's Order steps 2 to 4 are their derivation. Reword the lead to cite SPEC-3a step 1 rather than claim exclusivity.
UNVERIFIED: the exclusion bullet "A lease minted for a listed session after step 2's enumeration (rows R2, R4, R11, and R12)" omits R5/R6 (a running session can mint a renewal or rotation replacement lease, spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:447, between enumeration and cancel). Non-spec loop should check whether renewal or rotation can run for a session whose leases step 2 released.

### [spec.13.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode findings in the staged spec edits at round 13 — BECAUSE the only mechanism change since round 12 is SPEC-3a step 1 (lease return, then deny-list, before any cancel write). Its cost is bounded: the in-process assigner's `Release` is an in-memory call under `Service.mu`, and the Token Service client's `Release` is one `RevokeCredentials` RPC per lease under `c.timeout` and the §4.3 breaker, so the synchronous call stays bounded as the SPEC-3b Note says. The binding releases and claim writes that full_revoke now performs inline replace the D6 releases the shipped cancel writes triggered later, so the per-session etcd write count does not change — ALTERNATIVES: filing the crash window between step 1's last `Release` (which removes the lease records) and its first deny-list write, where a retried full_revoke enumerates no leases and writes no entry. Rejected: the shipped order (cancel writes first, then `RevokeUserLeases` in the fan-out) has the same failure class, since a retry after a crash past the cancel writes lists no live sessions (review-log OPEN at line 197). The new window is narrower, so the change does not make this failure mode worse.
FACT: the shipped `RevokeUserLeases` writes the deny-list entry and then calls `Remove`, one lease at a time, and it runs in `runFullRevokeFanOut` after the cancel writes. A crash at any point therefore leaves each removed lease already deny-listed, but a crash after the cancel writes leaves every lease without an entry — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:184-190.
FACT: `credassign.Client.Release` removes the local lease whatever the RPC outcome, and the RPC is bounded by `c.timeout` and runs through the Token Service breaker. An open breaker makes the step-1 returns fail fast, and they still deny-list — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:363-376, 382-395.
WATCHOUT: the §4.9 startup rebuild seeds only credentials with `status = 'revoked'` and a live lease (spec/04_system-components.md:1799-1804). A full_revoke deny entry is in memory only and is never rebuilt after a replica restart, either before or after this proposal. That is P5b's deny-list granularity territory and not a 0093 finding — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1776, 1804.
USEFUL [spec.12.review-performance.1]: its etcd accounting for SPEC-2c (DELETE plus a fresh CREATE replacing about 3 PATCHes, within the non-recycling Tier 3 base) and its decision on the burst-mitigation sentence meant SPEC-1 and SPEC-2 did not need to be re-derived.

FACT: The plan's P1 entry (gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md §10.2, "P1. Release ownership for pods, slots, and credential leases") assigns exactly three spec-level deliverables: the single-releaser rule, the durable release record for a writer without the binding, and "§11.4 gains the revocation set for pool credentials". The proposal declines each with a reason: summary Non-goals (§7.3 lease-return rule; §7.1/§4.7.1/§10.1.5 release paragraph), Decision 7 (no durable pending-release record), and Decision 18 (C3 deny-list granularity to P5b after the 0094 D20 split). EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:2745-2766.
FACT: The plan's drafting rule "a claim-register row" per departure targets tests/claim-map.json (§28.4 Claim register), so it is never a spec-loop finding. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:163-165.
FACT: Every plan member ID (F-4.6.26..29, F-4.9.32..35, F-5.2.49, F-7.3.33, F-7.3.34, F-10.1.21, F-10.1.28, F-10.1.30, F-10.1.32, F-11.4.10, F-11.4.11, F-11.4.15) is either closed by a deliverable in REC-1 item 1 or moved out in summary Non-goals or Decision 18.

### [spec.13.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings on the staged spec edits under the reliability lens in round 13 — BECAUSE the only spec change since round 12 is SPEC-3a step 1 (return every lease, then deny-list, before any cancel write), and its crash and retry traces hold: the assigner `Release` is a no-op on an unknown lease in both implementations, so a binding release that runs after step 1 does not return a pool slot twice (pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:400-405, client.go:363-367); the Token Service `Release` runs under `c.timeout` and the §4.3 breaker, so step 1 cannot hang on one dependency (client.go:384-399) — ALTERNATIVES: filing a handling-replica crash between step 1's last `Release` and its first deny-list write (a repeat full_revoke enumerates no lease records and writes no deny entry). Rejected: Decision 8 rests token rejection on the record removal, which already happened, and the lost entry is the deny-list effect P5b owns (C3). The security lens owns whether that entry is load-bearing.
WATCHOUT: under the new order a crash inside step 1 (some leases `Release`d, none deny-listed) is unrecoverable for the deny list by a repeat call, because the repeat lists leases by `LeasesBySession` and the returned ones are gone. If P5b or a later pass makes the deny-list entry load-bearing for a direct-mode key, this window needs a durable record of the enumerated leases. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:184-190 (today: deny then Remove per lease); non-spec-changes.md full_revoke release table **Order** step 2.
USEFUL [spec.12.review-reliability.1]: its GC-predicate trace for a handling-replica crash after the cancel writes settled SPEC-3a steps 2 and 4 without re-deriving the Sweeper and GC paths.
USEFUL [non-spec.4.review-reliability.1]: its shared-lease-store FACT settled that step 1 on the handling replica finds a peer-minted lease.

### [spec.13.review-security.1]
DECISION: filed no security finding on the staged spec edits — BECAUSE SPEC-3a step 1's Release-then-deny order does not open a proxy-mode window (the LLM proxy rejects a token whose lease record `Release` removed, before any deny-list or fallback check), the renewal worker forgets a released lease on `ErrLeaseReleased`, and every other weakening I traced is either a recorded departure or owned by P5b — ALTERNATIVES: filing the pre-cancel window in which a direct-mode fault report could mint an un-deny-listed replacement for a still-running listed session (rejected: the same window existed after the cancel in the old order, the session's own release returns the lease, and it is less strict rather than a bypassed control).
FACT: the LLM proxy resolves the bearer lease token through `Leases.GetByToken` and rejects an unknown token before the deny-list check, so removing the lease record is sufficient to stop proxy-mode use on every replica that shares the Postgres lease store — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go:324-333
FACT: the gateway stamps `lenny.dev/tenant-id` on the pod immediately after the claim CREATE and the first `bound` status write, and a stamp failure fails the acquisition, so the "release delete before the pod projects `claimed`" departure always leaves a pinned (tenant-scoped) idle pod and never an unpinned one — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:206-229
FACT: production delegation never sets `delegation.Request.UserID` (mcptools_register.go constructs the request without it), so a child always inherits the parent's `user_id` and SPEC-3a's drop of "the user's task tree" drops no child in today's tree — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:2146; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegation/service.go:1355-1357
USEFUL [review-log FACT slothealth / DrainLedger]: the whole-pod replacement trigger does not read the Redis slot set, so the reseed departure does not reset a residual-state bound; saved re-deriving the durability check.

### [spec.13.review-single-source.1]
DECISION: no single-source findings in the staged spec edits at round 13 — BECAUSE SPEC-3a step 1 is the only spec statement of the full_revoke lease order (return every lease, then deny-list, before any cancel), SPEC-3b's Note lists the effects in one clause and adds the only spec statement of the lease count, and SPEC-3a step 6 now cites step 1 instead of restating it; the slot-set rules remain stated only in SPEC-1a and the claim-delete predicate only in SPEC-2c, with every other site carrying a one-clause "(§4.6.3)" or "(§5.2)" citation — ALTERNATIVES: filing the pre-existing parallel reserved-hold paragraphs (§4.6.1 **Reserved hold** and §6.2 **`reserved` hold semantics.**), which SPEC-2b and SPEC-2h each extend by the same one-clause citation (rejected: the copies predate the proposal, and the staged text adds only a citation).
FACT: `userLeaseRevoker.RevokeUserLeases` calls `denyList.Revoke`, which returns nothing, so the SPEC-3b count ("the number of leases step 1 adds to the deny list") and the non-spec **Release outcomes** count ("the number of leases step 2 enumerated") cannot diverge — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:184-191
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: the **full_revoke release table** **Order** heading says "No other site states the order." That is false: staged SPEC-3a step 1 states the return-then-deny order and its precedence over the cancel writes, normatively (spec-changes.md:507). The normative home is SPEC-3a step 1, and the table derives that order for the code. Reword the sentence to "SPEC-3a step 1 states the order normatively; no other proposal site restates it."

### [spec.15.review-mechanism.1]

### [spec.15.review-performance.1]
DECISION: returned no findings on the staged spec edits under the performance and failure-mode lens in round 15. BECAUSE the only change since round 13 is SPEC-3a step 4 ("whose pod binding the handling gateway replica does not hold ... before that replica's step-2 releases"), which matches full_revoke **Order** step 4(a) before 4(b) and rows R1, R2, and R3. The set primitive (SPEC-1a) keeps O(1) per-pod Redis ops (SISMEMBER/SCARD/SADD, SREM/SCARD) on the same per-pod key. Release-path rehydration shares the reservation path's flag and SET NX lock, so a Redis restart still causes at most one `GetActiveSlotsByPod` per pod. SPEC-2 replaces the retired `failed` status patch with a delete, so etcd claim writes per occupancy do not grow. full_revoke's per-session work stays sequential and bounded, as `terminateLocal` already is (userTerminateRPCTimeout 20 s). ALTERNATIVES: filing the §12.4 "On Redis recovery, the slot set is rebuilt" sentence (SPEC-1d), rejected because rounds 2 and 7 declined it as spec intent whose gap is in code (review-log DECISION near "clearOutage"); filing the full_revoke deny entry's missing startup-rebuild fallback, rejected because it is P5b deny-list territory (review-log WATCHOUT on spec/04_system-components.md:1799-1804).
FACT: the shipped `Counter.Release` never rehydrates and writes no key when the counter is absent. Under SPEC-1a, a release after `Counter.Reset` (pod retire, §10.1.4) or after a Redis restart rehydrates and re-creates the no-TTL `rehydrated` flag for that pod ID. The flag is a small key that persists until the next Redis restart. An emptied set is deleted by Redis itself. This is negligible at Tier 3 pod churn. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go Release and Reset (lines ~470-490); non-spec CODE-1 item 2.
FACT: no binding is published at finalize. registerBinding is called only from start.go (before commitStart), and publishBinding only from create_and_start.go, delegated_child_materialize.go, and resume_driver.go. The "SDK-warm preConnect pod attached at finalize" comment in usage.go terminalReclaimPreRunning, and full_revoke row R2's mention of it, describe no live publish path. A peer-held binding of a pre-running session therefore exists only in the /start window that **Races the table does not close** item 3 covers. EVIDENCE: grep registerBinding/publishBinding in pkg/gateway/sessionserver; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:519-526.
UNVERIFIED: whether R2's "SDK-warm preConnect pod attached at finalize" case is reachable at all. The non-spec loop should check bindfinalize/finalize paths before relying on R2's second clause.
