### [spec.15.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings on the staged spec edits under the reliability lens in round 15 — BECAUSE the only hunk since spec-r13-prefix is SPEC-3a step 4, and its predicate is correct as long as the handling replica's binding cannot vanish between step 3 and step 4(a) (see DEFERRED below, whose remedy is non-spec). A re-walk of SPEC-1a (keyed release, release-path rehydration, leaked members), SPEC-1b/1f (reseed), SPEC-2c, and SPEC-3a/3b under crash, retry, and Redis restart found nothing beyond the recorded departures — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the step-1 crash window (Release done, deny list not written, so a repeat call enumerates no leases). Not filed: the round-9 and round-13 reliability passes and the round-13 performance pass already considered and rejected it (review log, entries near "crash between step 1").
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, full_revoke release table **Order** step 3/4 and **Races the table does not close**]: Order step 4 says (a) runs before (b) so that a by-name reclaim never deletes a claim that a binding release just patched to `recycling` (row D3b). The handling replica's OWN coordination Sweeper can still release an R2 binding in the window between step 3's cancel write and step 4(a). The Sweeper lists every row every 15 s, and `sweepTerminal` calls `ReleaseTerminalBinding` for any terminal row bound here. full_revoke opens no terminal claim, so `startRelease` is not refused, and `PodExecutor.Release` unbinds before the binder runs. Step 4(a)'s `terminalReclaimPreRunning` then finds no local binding and calls `ReclaimClaimed`, an unconditional by-name `deleteClaimDetached`. On a recycling `mcs 1` pool that deletes the D3b `recycling` claim mid-scrub, and the pod drains, which fails closed. Item 3 of the races list covers only a PEER's D6. Remedy: either fence the cancelled rows against the Sweeper for the duration of steps 3 and 4 (open a terminal claim, or have 4(a) skip a session whose release has started), or list the race as an accepted departure. SPEC-3a step 4's text stays correct under the fence remedy. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:406,433,530-535 (List all rows, sweepTerminal → ReleaseTerminalBinding), :246-248 (15 s default); pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:42-52; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:510-531; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:79-88.
FACT: on a `/start` refused at `commitStart`, the binding is left registered (start.go:116-120 returns the precondition error and does not roll back), so an R2 binding persists until step 4(b) or a Sweeper D6 release — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:116-167.

### [spec.16.review-performance.1]
DECISION: No performance or failure-mode finding on the staged spec edits — BECAUSE the set scripts are O(1) per call on the same per-pod key the counter used (no new hot key, no etcd writes added; a pre-running release that now deletes instead of patching `recycling` costs fewer claim writes), the release-path rehydration adds at most one `GetActiveSlotsByPod` query per pod per Redis restart under the existing `SET NX` lock, and every store failure degrades no worse than the shipped clamp-at-zero counter (which ran the occupancy-zero disposition on a false zero) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the §5.2 burst paragraph's "first slot allocation attempt ... triggers" wording (rejected: the one-query-per-pod bound still holds; wording only).
FACT: `slotcounter.Counter.Reset` has no caller under pkg/ or cmd/, so `active_slots` and `rehydrated` keys already persist for every retired pod; a late release that creates a flag key after a restart is not a new leak class. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go `Reset`; `grep -rn "\.Reset(ctx" pkg cmd` empty.
FACT: suspend release clears `pod_assignment`, so a post-restart seed from `GetActiveSlotsByPod` holds no phantom member for a suspended session. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/suspend_release.go:173 `r.PodAssignment = ""`.
FACT: Sessions admitted through the §12.4 Postgres fallback during a Redis connectivity outage get no set member, and nothing clears the `rehydrated` flag on reconnection, so the §12.4 failure row's "rebuilt on recovery" holds only after a restart. This is the same as the shipped counter, which also could not count them; it is not a regression from this proposal. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go `fallbackReserve`; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:219.
WATCHOUT: the shipped leaked release skips Redis entirely (podclaim/slotclaimer.go `ReleaseSlot` `if leaked { return false, nil }`), while CODE-1 writes `leaked:<sid>`, so a leaked release now fails during a Redis outage. The effect is benign: the session member stays and occupancy stays counted. Do not file it as a regression.

### [spec.16.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings on the staged spec edits under the reliability lens in spec round 16 — BECAUSE the spec staging is unchanged since round 14's SPEC-3a step 4 hunk (git diff aacee2a02 HEAD touches one line of spec-changes.md), spec/ is unchanged since 85c9ab19a, and a re-walk of SPEC-1a (keyed release, release-path rehydration, leaked members), SPEC-1b/1f (reseed, §10.1.4 reset), SPEC-2c, and SPEC-3a/3b under crash, lost reply, peer-held rehydration lock, and Redis restart found only outcomes that are already recorded departures or bounded by the claim-orphan GC — ALTERNATIVES: (1) a release blocked on a peer's `rehydrating` lock does not add its own member, so SPEC-1a's "adding its own session's member to the seed" does not literally hold; rejected because every peer that seeds either adds its own member (reservation) or runs the disposition itself (release), and a peer crash between seed and reserve is the process-exit departure, GC-bounded; (2) the lost disposition on a crash between the release script and the claim write; rejected, non-spec Edge cases "A disposition failure after the member is removed" and D17 cover it; (3) re-filing the §12.4 "On Redis recovery, the slot set is rebuilt" sentence or the round-15 handling-replica Sweeper race; rejected, the first is declined spec intent (review-log near "clearOutage"), the second's remedy is CODE-7 or the non-spec races list.
FACT: `Counter.Reset` deletes the set, the `rehydrated` flag, and the `rehydrating` lock together, so after a §10.1.4 whole-pod reset any late keyed release takes the SPEC-1a release-path rehydration; the spec Edge cases reseed bullet already records the resulting disposition-under-dropped-reservation case — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:482-487
USEFUL [non-spec.4.review-reliability.1]: its FACT on the release-path rehydration seeding only when that call seeds settled the peer-lock interleaving without re-reading slotcounter.go.
USEFUL [spec.15.review-reliability.1]: its DEFERRED on the handling replica's own Sweeper racing step 4(a) is the live reliability item; it stays with the non-spec loop.

### [spec.16.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding on the staged spec edits in round 16 — BECAUSE every security-relevant change checked either keeps the control or is a departure already recorded and adjudicated: the slot cap stays a gateway-side Redis Lua gate with the §12.4 Postgres fallback (SPEC-1c/1d), leaked members still count toward the cap, the whole-pod drain threshold reads the gateway's in-process slotHealth rather than the set or an adapter self-report, and the SPEC-2d removal of the `failed` write for failed or crashed mcs1 sessions is the recorded "release delete before the pod projects `claimed`" departure — ALTERNATIVES: filing SPEC-2d as removing a fail-closed drain (rejected: review-log lines 328-471 adjudicated it over five rounds; the tenant label bounds it, and the owner prefers bounded departures).
FACT: the `failed` and `released` claim dispositions drain the pod from any projected phase, but a claim delete drains only when the pod projects `claimed`. With no claim and a current phase of `idle`, the projection leaves the pod alone. That projection lag is the only gap separating SPEC-2d from the old spec — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go ProjectOccupancyPhase (terminal case returns Draining; no-claim switch handles only Reserved and Claimed).
FACT: the reorder that revokes leases before the cancel write (SPEC-3a step 1) cannot set off a credential-fallback mint from a deny-listed lease, because the LLM proxy checks the deny list before it forwards upstream. Only a fault already in flight on the lease can still mint a replacement, and the table's "A lease minted ... after step 2's enumeration" case covers that — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go:332 (DenyList.Revoked); pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/fallback.go driveFallback; cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_fallback.go Rotate.
USEFUL [review-log line 700 and 1403 FACTs on "task tree"]: these settled that dropping "the user's task tree" from §11.4 step 2 drops no delegated child, which saved re-deriving the delegation user_id inheritance.

### [non-spec.5.review-security.1]
FACT: the LLM proxy resolves every inbound lease token against the lease store (`h.Leases.GetByToken`) before any upstream call and answers 401 `LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID` for a missing record, so a removed lease record never reaches the credential Fallback Flow (`proxyFallbackRotator.Rotate`), which fires only on an upstream fault. Decision 8's "removing the record rejects the token on every replica" holds whenever `llmLeases` is the Postgres store (pgstore `GetByToken` has no per-replica cache). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go:322-327; cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:74-89; pkg/gateway/credentials/credleasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:176
FACT: proactive renewal cannot re-mint a lease that full_revoke step 2 released. `credRenewalWiring.Renew` returns `ErrLeaseReleased` when the spent lease has no record, and after `Assign` it looks the record up again and releases the replacement if the record is gone. The step-2-before-cancel order therefore opens no renewal hole for running sessions. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:177-214
FACT: both assigners' `Release` find a lease minted by a peer through the shared store (`Service.releaseLocked` and `Client.Release` both call `leases.GetByID`) and remove the record. The Client removes it even when `RevokeCredentials` fails. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:459-468; pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:363-377
WATCHOUT: once CODE-7 item 3 lands, the `recordingRevokeHook` fake in pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users_revoke_hook_test.go no longer satisfies `RevokeHook`, and no CODE-7 test bullet names that file. Its `TestPartialFullRevokeFansOutOnlyTheCancelledSessions_spec_11_4` is the natural home for the cancel-store-error lease assertion. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go:437-439; users_revoke_hook_test.go:19-24,90
DECISION: I did not file the "release delete before the pod projects `claimed`" departure. In that departure, a failed or crashed session on a recycling `mcs 1` pool can leave a pinned idle pod that was never scrubbed — BECAUSE the spec loop accepted it as a recorded departure, the tree already deletes the claim on those paths, and the acknowledgment gate in §5.2 still refuses pinned pods on pools outside the rule — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as a regression of the spec's `failed`-write control. I rejected that because the directive bars reopening SPEC-2 except for a defect, and no code ever implemented the `failed` write.

### [spec.21.review-performance.1]
FACT: `Counter.rehydrate` re-checks the `rehydrated` flag under both the in-process per-pod mutex and the cross-replica `SET NX` lock before it seeds, so two releases that both see `-2` after a Redis restart seed once. The second finds the flag set and seeds nothing, so a SPEC-1a release-path rehydration cannot run the occupancy-zero disposition twice across two different sessions. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go `rehydrate` (the `flagSet` checks before and after `SetNX`)
FACT: the shipped `releaseScript` has no Postgres dependency and returns 0 on a missing key. A SPEC-1a release now reads Postgres after a Redis restart. When a Redis restart coincides with a Postgres failover, the release fails and takes register row A4. The session's member is then absent from the later reseed, because its row is terminal, rather than Counted. That outcome is no worse than shipped, since shipped reported a false zero and ran the disposition under live siblings. EVIDENCE: slotcounter.go `Release` doc comment ("Release does not rehydrate")
FACT: `Counter.Reset` has no non-test caller, so the §10.1.4 whole-pod reset that SPEC-1f rewords is not implemented in the tree. EVIDENCE: grep for `.Reset(ctx` under pkg/ and cmd/
FACT: `RevokeUserLeases` and `denyList.Revoke` return no error, so a Redis outage cannot block SPEC-3a step 1 ahead of the cancel writes. `terminateLocal` is serial in the shipped tree, so SPEC-3b's "bounded duration" is no worse than before. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go `RevokeUserLeases`, `terminateLocal`
UNVERIFIED: register row A4's doc-site reason ("the release fails only while Redis fails") omits the post-restart Postgres read that SPEC-1a adds. This is non-spec; the non-spec loop's reliability lens should confirm whether A4's Bound and reason need the Postgres case.

### [spec.21.review-security.1]

The remaining non-test mention after CODE-7 is the `Client.Shutdown` doc comment ("the §11.4 full_revoke fan-out passes `USER_REVOKED`"), which is a Go comment outside CODE-7's and CODE-8's targets and below the finding bar — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go:948-950; docs/reference/adapter-contract.md:302.

### [non-spec.6.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding in non-spec round 6 — BECAUSE the r19-prefix-to-current diff only moves the A13 and A15 bounds into the register and expands the CODE-4, DOCS-1, and TEST-1 index lines, and a re-walk of the controls found each bound on a trusted, durable source: the slot cap and its rehydration read Postgres rows (GetActiveSlotsByPod), the §12.4 fallback (pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go ReserveSlotUnderLock) is untouched, a pinned idle pod left by A15 is refused and drain-stamped on any pool outside the runtime-process acknowledgment rule (spec/05 "Deployer acknowledgment (runtime process kept across sessions)."), and every release that ends the occupancy without reuse deletes the claim, which projects `claimed -> draining` (spec/04 §4.6.1 Occupancy projection, last bullet) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the Token Service delay below (rejected as "less strict than it could be"); filing the podTerminationFailures change below (rejected: the release completes and the leak stays counted).
FACT: full_revoke Order step 2 with the Token Service client removes each lease record only after its RevokeCredentials RPC returns (`Client.Release`: revoke, then `c.leases.Remove`; DefaultClientTimeout 5s), so lease i's token stays valid until the first i RPCs finish or the breaker opens. Today the deny write and the record removal for every lease are local and immediate. With the in-process assigner `Service.Release` is local and cannot fail. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go `Release`, `DefaultClientTimeout`; cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go `RevokeUserLeases`.
FACT: `Binder.Release` and `Binder.ReleaseSlot` swallow every adapter `Shutdown` error, a transport error included and not only a cut context, so after CODE-7 an adapter Shutdown failure no longer reaches the full_revoke audit detail `podTerminationFailures` (users.go builds the detail from `FailedSessions`). On mcs >1 such a release records a `leaked:` member. Register row A5 names only the cut-context case. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go `shutdownAdapter` (`_, _ =`); slotbinder.go `ReleaseSlot` (`leaked = err != nil || !cleanly`); pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go `detail["podTerminationFailures"]`.
UNVERIFIED: `pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go` doc comment on `Shutdown` ("the §11.4 full_revoke fan-out passes `USER_REVOKED`") goes false under CODE-7 item 6, and neither CODE-7 nor CODE-8 lists that comment (CODE-8 lists only the `ShutdownRecycle` comment in the same file). This is a code comment, so it is outside the (d) surfaces. The edit-sites lens should decide whether CODE-7 lists it.
USEFUL [remedy.departure-register.2026-10-18]: the A5 correction (cut Shutdown leaks on mcs >1 and counts in podsTerminated) matched the tree and saved re-deriving the timeout path.

DECISION: A14's Bound cell now reads "None in time: the pod admits one more session for each uncounted session until that session ends or a reseed counts it, and ..." — BECAUSE the fallback admission writes no member and recovery runs no rebuild, so the fast path admits past the cap; per-session phrasing matches A11 — ALTERNATIVES: "one session over its cap" (understates: one outage can leave several sessions uncounted on a pod).

### [non-spec.8.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Delete summary **Watch out for.** bullet 6 (the full_revoke step-3 claim fence) outright, with no citing replacement — BECAUSE **Order** steps 3 and 4 are the rule home and already carry the trap (step 4: "The claims end before step (b), so step (b)'s `startRelease` is not refused by them"), Decision 19 is the reason home, and the Order lead says no other non-spec site restates the order — ALTERNATIVES: re-synchronising the bullet with the 4(b) half (a third copy that drifts again); the reviewer's citing one-liner (a pointer with nothing the reader acts on that the neighbouring "full_revoke release table is the only full statement" bullet does not already give).
DECISION: Close the missing claim-end test by adding test INPUTS that reach the branches plus one leak oracle, rather than re-describing the claim order — BECAUSE round 6 deleted a sentence that restated Order steps 3-4 inside the test bullet; the gap is that no seeded case reaches the superseded branch and no assertion checks the claim after the store-error return. A superseded row seeded into the ordered recorder, plus "no claim the recording hook's `BeginTerminal` opened is open when the handler returns" in both admin tests, is a different kind of answer: a case and an invariant, citing the Order for timing — ALTERNATIVES: restoring the round-6-deleted sentence (restates Order; the round-6 defect); asserting "ends at once" per branch (restates step 3 and needs an ordering oracle a leak check already subsumes for the hazard that matters).
FACT: `terminateUserSessions` today has two non-append branches after the cancel write: `ErrSuperseded` → `continue`, store error → `return terminated, err`. Neither row reaches the returned slice, so the deferred end over `[]RevokedSession` cannot cover their claims; only an explicit end in those branches does. A leaked claim makes `startRelease` refuse that session on the handling replica for the process lifetime. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go `terminateUserSessions`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replica_records.go `startRelease`.
FACT: no admin test fixture produces `ErrSuperseded` today; `failSecondUpdate` only returns a plain store error. The superseded case needs a store wrapper whose `Update` makes the target row terminal before delegating, so the mutator's `IsTerminal` guard fires. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users_revoke_hook_test.go `failSecondUpdate`.
WATCHOUT: Decision 19 ("until the pre-running reclaim has run") and CODE-7 item 3's doc-comment instruction ("until **Order** step 4(a) ends") both omit the end-before-4(b) half. Neither contradicts the Order, so they were left alone this round; a later single-source pass may cut Decision 19's rule clause to its reason.

### [non-spec.8.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: A14's Bound cell becomes "None in time: the pod admits one more session for each uncounted session until that session ends or a reseed counts it, and when it outlives its siblings ..." (rest unchanged) — BECAUSE the label must name the cap relaxation in the register's own vocabulary (A11's "one more session for each dropped member"), and an outage can leave several fallback-admitted sessions uncounted on one pod, so the suggested "one session over its cap" understates the bound — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "one session over its cap" wording (undercounts the multi-admission case); rewording the edge-case paragraph or REC-1 (both stay true; REC-1 files the cell verbatim, so fixing the cell fixes the filing).
FACT: the Postgres fallback writes no Redis member and recovery runs no rebuild: `fallbackReserve` calls `ReserveSlotUnderLock` (Postgres count) only, and `clearOutage` only zeroes `outageSince` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go `fallbackReserve`, `clearOutage`.
OPEN: A14 is arguably a tree defect against §12.4 rather than an accepted departure: spec/12_storage-architecture.md failure-behavior row "Session slot counter" says "On Redis recovery, the counter is rebuilt through the standard blocking-rehydration path before fast-path enforcement resumes" (SPEC-1d keeps it), and `clearOutage` runs no rebuild. A14's Doc-site reason "the fallback admission is §12.4 behavior" therefore covers the admission but not the missing rebuild. A later round should decide whether CODE-1 adds a rebuild on outage clear (closing A14) or the row says the tree departs from §12.4's recovery sentence. Not part of G2's fix.

A tail that publishes while a step-3 claim is open has its `ReleaseTerminalBinding` refused, and step 4(b)'s snapshot then releases the binding. ### [non-spec.8.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The gateway's retire path writes no Sandbox status. `Binder.drain` is only `podclaim.DeleteClaim` (reclaim.go `drain`), and `Binder.Release` documents the WarmPoolController as the sole Sandbox.status writer. CODE-2 item 8 changes only the context of these writes, so it needs no RBAC change. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go `func (b *Binder) drain`; release.go `Release` doc comment.
FACT: The claim-orphan GC classifies `bound` and `recycling` claims by `BindingStateTransitionTime` (falling back to creationTimestamp) and empty-phase claims by creationTimestamp. It drains the Sandbox through its own SSA status patch, then deletes the claim, and only after `PodHasActiveSession` returns false. The register's GC backstop matches this. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go `classify`, `evaluate`, `reclaimByDraining`; flag `--claim-orphan-timeout` at cmd/lenny-controller/flags.go:177; interval default 60s at gc.go:51.
FACT: `make generate` regenerates charts/lenny/crds and pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml, and that manifests file holds no CRD description text. It does not copy pkg/embedded/crds. CODE-8's explicit copy step is required by pkg/embedded/crds/crds_test.go (byte-identical check). EVIDENCE: Makefile `generate` target; crds_test.go:80.
FACT: The only writers of a `failed` or `released` claim are the recycle boundary's `WriteDispositionStatus` callers, which supports disposition Invariant 1. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/recycle/recycleboundary.go:228; scrubreporter_seams.go:982.
DECISION: Kubernetes lens, round 8: no findings — BECAUSE the proposal adds no new status writer, field manager, finalizer, webhook, or controller dependency on the request path; every claim write stays with the gateway (§4.6.3 SandboxClaim row) — ALTERNATIVES: filing A15/A16/A21 by-name deletes without preconditions as idiom findings (rejected: register-accepted departures, and a UID precondition would not close A21 because the successor rebinds the same claim object).
UNVERIFIED: CODE-2 item 8 does not say where `detachedReleaseContext` is created on the retire path. If it is created at the top of `Release`, a `Shutdown` that blocks longer than 5 s (`slotCleanupFloor`) would leave the claim `Get`/delete with an expired context, and the item 8 tier-1 test would not discriminate this case. A mechanism or reliability reviewer should check this; it is outside this lens.

### [non-spec.8.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The release-path rehydration seeds its own member only when this release takes the rehydrating lock and finds the flag still absent. A release that waits on a peer's lock, or finds that a Reserve already seeded, removes nothing and skips the disposition. This is still correct: whichever release removes the last seeded member runs the disposition exactly once, and a Reserve-triggered seed adds the reserver's own member. Do not file it as a lost disposition. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go `rehydrate` (flagSet re-check, SetNX, waitForFlag)
FACT: Today all of full_revoke runs on req.Context(). The proposal's step 4(a) still releases after a client disconnect, because `ReclaimClaimed` calls `releaseCredentials` with no context and deletes the claim through `deleteClaimDetached`, and the perSlot branch's `ReleaseSlotReservation` also runs on a detached context. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go `ReclaimClaimed`; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go handleInvalidateUser
FACT: A24 is consistent with the tree. `ReleaseUncommitted(requireReclaimed=true)` returns `ErrReclaimUnconfirmed` before `releaseResumeSlot` and `releaseAttemptCredentials` run, on both pool kinds. The existing unconfirmed subtest covers only pool 1. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go `ReleaseUncommitted`; resume_credentials_test.go `TestReleaseUncommittedReturnsOnlyTheAttemptsPodAndLeases_spec_7_3`
WATCHOUT: For A5 to hold on the retire path, CODE-2 item 8's `detachedReleaseContext` must be created after the plain `Shutdown`, because a context created at function entry is spent by a Shutdown that runs the full 20 s callCtx. The CODE-2 tier-1 test does not detect the difference when its caller context is shorter than 5 s. The proposal does not state where the context is created. I judged this below the bar; an implementor should create the context immediately before the Get and the drain. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go `Binder.Release` (shutdownAdapter, then Get, then drain)

### [non-spec.8.review-security.1]
DECISION: one finding: register row A14's bound reads "Fails closed", yet an uncounted fallback-admitted session lets the pod admit one session over `maxConcurrentSessions` until a reseed. Rows A11 and A18 state that same over-cap effect as "the pod admits one more session". BECAUSE fallbackReserve adds no member (pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go `fallbackReserve`), and clearOutage only resets the outage clock, so no rebuild runs when Redis kept its data (fallback.go `clearOutage`). The departure itself predates this proposal and is narrower than today's (an unkeyed DECR used to under-count permanently). ### [non-spec.10.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-2 item 8 now states that the retire path's detached context is created after `shutdownAdapter` returns, and its tier-1 test discriminates on the deadline of the context the Sandbox `Get` receives (at least `slotCleanupFloor` after the fake `Shutdown` returned) — BECAUSE the plain retire `Shutdown` passes grace 0 and runs to the caller's deadline (20 s on full_revoke's callCtx), so a detached context created at `Release` entry (5 s) is spent before `Get` and `drain` — ALTERNATIVES: an injectable `slotCleanupFloor` (a test seam on a spec-fixed constant); a fake `Shutdown` sleeping past 5 s (slow tier-1); running `Shutdown` on the detached context too (changes Decision 20's best-effort Shutdown). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go `Binder.Release` retire path and `shutdownAdapter`; reclaim.go `detachedReleaseContext`; slotbinder.go `slotCleanupFloor`.
CORRECTS [non-spec.9 UNVERIFIED on the item 8 context placement and the WATCHOUT that the CODE-2 tier-1 test does not detect it]: verified and closed by the edits above.
DECISION: `ReleaseRevokedBinding`'s outcome cases moved from the CODE-7 `cmd/lenny-gateway` test bullet into a new `sessionserver` bullet, which adds the lock-wait expiry case (`(false, err)`, binding still registered, a later `ReleaseTerminalBinding` releases it once); register row A5's Pinning test cell names it — BECAUSE `slotAccountLocks` and `startRelease` are unexported in `sessionserver`, so the case cannot be built in `cmd/lenny-gateway`, and the lock wait moves from `s.lifetime()` to the bounded callCtx (CODE-7 item 5), making the expiry reachable — ALTERNATIVES: extending the `cmd/lenny-gateway` sentence (unbuildable there); folding into the step-3 fence bullet (wrong label). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go `releaseTerminalBinding` (`s.lifetime()` lock wait); slot_bind.go `sessionLocks.Lock` returns `ctx.Err()`; terminal_binding_internal_test.go already holds `slotAccountLocks`.
WATCHOUT: "a later `ReleaseTerminalBinding` releases it once" is the assertion that pins `endRelease` running on the lock-wait error path; a kept release mark makes that later call refuse. Keep it if the bullet is reworded.

### [non-spec.10.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: Registered the full_revoke cancel write that commits and reports a store error as its own register row A25 (GC; Counted; annotate F-4.6.29; DOCS-1 item 10; pinned by a committed-error variant of `TestPartialFullRevokeFansOutOnlyTheCancelledSessions_spec_11_4`), added it to **Releases no replica performs**, pointed the full_revoke table's third post-row bullet at it, and replaced A23's false Doc-site clause "a repeated call completes the revoke" — BECAUSE `terminateUserSessions` returns `terminated, err` without the row whose `Update` may have committed, so step 4(a) never reclaims it, its claim ends at once, and a repeated call skips it as terminal; a pre-running row with no binding (R1) then has no releaser — ALTERNATIVES: folding it into A23 with per-case cells (rejected: every cell differs, and A3/A24 register the same commit ambiguity as separate rows); a `ReadPrimary` re-read after the error to add the row to the cancelled set (rejected: a new mechanism on a failing-store path that cannot tell its own cancel from another writer's, whose hook already releases); running 4(a) for every listed pre-running row (rejected: deletes the claim of a live row whose cancel did not commit); annotating F-11.4.15 (rejected: REC-1 item 3 keeps annotated findings OPEN, and F-11.4.15 is closed).
FACT: a pgstore `Update` error can hide a commit: `pgtenant.InTx` returns `pgtenant: commit: %w` after `fn` succeeded. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/pgtenant/pgtenant.go `InTx` (commit branch); pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go `terminateUserSessions` (`return terminated, err` before the append).
WATCHOUT: a hidden-commit cancel row that holds a binding is still released by the Sweeper's D6, only because **Order** step 3 ends the claim on any write error. A change that keeps the claim open after an error strands that binding too.
USEFUL [remedy.departure-register.2026-10-18 WATCHOUT]: starting at the register row and citing it from the bullet and the list kept the fix to one rule site.

### [non-spec.10.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: Closed "CODE-6's lifetime-context lock wait and its shutdown skip have no test" by appending two cases to the CODE-6 Tier 1 bullet (writer ctx cancelled during the hook's lock wait while the funnel holds the lock with its edge refused: release runs once after unlock; lifetime ctx cancelled during the wait: no `terminalReclaimPreRunning` or executor call) — BECAUSE an implementation waiting on the writer's `ctx` passed every listed test and leaks the claim or slot when the funnel's edge is refused — ALTERNATIVES: TEST-1 tier-7a interleavings (rejected: each case is one deterministic ordering a tier-1 fake discriminates); a shutdown case with the lock free (rejected: flaky).
WATCHOUT: `sessionLocks.Lock` selects between `e.sem <- struct{}{}` and `<-ctx.Done()`; when both are ready Go picks at random, so any lock-wait cancellation test must hold the lock before cancelling — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go `sessionLocks.Lock`.
CORRECTS [review-log DECISION "Did not file a cancelled-caller-context test for ... the lock-wait error path of CODE-6"]: CODE-6's lock-wait error path and the writer-ctx-cancelled path now have tier-1 cases; the cancelled-caller test for `Binder.ReleaseSlot`'s claimer call (CODE-1 item 5) is unaffected by this round.
OPEN: after a successful lifetime-context lock wait, CODE-6 step 5 still releases on the writer's `ctx` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go `AfterTerminalWrite` passes `ctx` to `terminalReclaimPreRunning` and `releaseExecutor`). With the writer's request cancelled, a real executor release may fail on the cancelled context. Whether step 5 should run on a detached context is not decided here and needs its own finding; the new tier-1 case asserts only that the release call is made, which a fake executor observes regardless.
FACT: callers of `AfterTerminalWrite` passing a request-derived ctx: `expireSession` and the second writer in pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_terminal.go, `recordSessionCompleted` in usage.go; the funnel `applyFailureFromActive` (failure.go) takes `slotAccountLocks` on its own ctx and "A conflict or no-op runs neither step".

### [non-spec.10.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-2 item 8 states that the retire path's `Get`/`drain` context is created after `shutdownAdapter` returns, and the item 8 tier-1 test discriminates by asserting the deadline of the context the Sandbox `Get` receives is at least `slotCleanupFloor` after the fake `Shutdown` returned — BECAUSE the retire path runs `shutdownAdapter(ctx, …, false)` (plain `Shutdown` deadline 0, so it runs to the caller's deadline) before `Get` and `drain`, and `detachedReleaseContext` starts its 5 s timer at creation — ALTERNATIVES: an injectable `slotCleanupFloor` (a test-only seam on a spec-fixed constant); a fake `Shutdown` blocking over 5 s (a slow tier-1 test); both rejected because the deadline assertion discriminates entry-vs-after placement with a short caller context. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go `Binder.Release` retire path, `shutdownAdapter`; reclaim.go `detachedReleaseContext`; binder_test.go already uses `interceptor.Funcs`, so the `Get` context is capturable.
DECISION: the lock-wait-expiry case for `ReleaseRevokedBinding` lands in a new `sessionserver` CODE-7 test bullet, which also takes the existing `ReleaseRevokedBinding` outcome sentence out of the `cmd/lenny-gateway` bullet — BECAUSE `slotAccountLocks` and `startRelease` are unexported in `sessionserver` (terminal_binding_internal_test.go already holds the lock that way), so neither the existing cases nor the new one can be built from `cmd/lenny-gateway` — ALTERNATIVES: extending the `cmd/lenny-gateway` sentence (unbuildable there).
CORRECTS [non-spec.8.review-kubernetes.1 UNVERIFIED on the detached-context creation point]: confirmed; closed by the G1 design. Also closes the WATCHOUT in [non-spec.8.review-reliability.1].
WATCHOUT: the recycle branch needs no placement rule: `WriteRecyclingStatus` precedes the recycle `Shutdown`, so only the retire path can spend the context before its claim writes. EVIDENCE: release.go `Binder.Release`.

### [non-spec.10.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: The full_revoke cancel write whose store error hides its commit becomes its own register row A25 (bound GC; Counted; REC-1 Annotate F-4.6.29; doc site DOCS-1 item 10; pin a commit-then-error variant of `TestPartialFullRevokeFansOutOnlyTheCancelledSessions_spec_11_4`), listed in **Releases no replica performs**; A23's Doc site cell drops "a repeated call completes the revoke"; the third bullet after the full_revoke rows says step 4 runs for the rows whose write returned no error and points at A25 — BECAUSE the residue differs from A23 in every register cell (A23 fails closed and is cured by a repeat; the hidden commit leaves a cancelled row no repeat lists and no releaser reaches), which is the pattern A3 (`failFinalizing`) and A24 (resume commit) already follow — ALTERNATIVES: folding it into A23 as the finding suggested (rejected: every cell would carry two cases, which is a conditional row); re-reading the row with `ReadPrimary` after the error and reclaiming it if cancelled (rejected: a new mechanism on a path where the store is failing, and it cannot tell its own cancel from another writer's, whose hook already releases); running step 4(a) for every listed pre-running row (rejected: deletes the claim of a row whose cancel did not commit).
FACT: only R1 (pre-running, no binding on any replica) is left without a releaser. A hidden-commit row with a binding is released by the Sweeper's D6: on the handling replica because step 3 ends the claim at once on an error, on a peer because the peer's Sweeper lists terminal rows. Step 4(b) does not reach it, because `TerminateUserSessions` carries `SessionIDs` from the returned set. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go (`SessionIDs: sessionIDs`); pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go `terminateUserSessions` (the `return terminated, err` branch); pkg/gateway/storage/pgtenant/pgtenant.go `InTx` (`pgtenant: commit: %w`); pgstore `Update` runs in `pgtenant.InTx`.
UNVERIFIED: a hidden-commit row also skips `AfterRevokeWrite` (`CancelResumeAttempt` and the inbox/DLQ drain). Whether any other path drains a terminal session's inbox and DLQ was not checked. A reliability lens should check it before anyone files it.

### [non-spec.10.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: CODE-6's lock-wait context and shutdown skip are pinned by two sentences appended to the CODE-6 Tier 1 bullet, both with the funnel holding the session's lock during the wait — BECAUSE only a held semaphore makes `sessionLocks.Lock` return deterministically on a done context; with the semaphore free its select picks at random — ALTERNATIVES: a shutdown case with the lock free (flaky, and passes for a wrong implementation half the time); a TEST-1 tier-7a interleaving (the case is a single-goroutine ordering a tier-1 fake executor already discriminates).
USEFUL [WATCHOUT on sessionLocks.Lock random select, review-log ~:1056]: it is why both new cases hold the lock before cancelling a context.
CORRECTS [review-log ~:1320 DECISION "Did not file ... the lock-wait error path of CODE-6"]: the lock-wait context choice is not hardening-adjacent; an implementation waiting on the writer's ctx leaks the claim or slot when the funnel's edge is refused, and only a cancelled-writer case discriminates it.
OPEN: after the lifetime-context lock wait succeeds, step 5 still calls `terminalReclaimPreRunning(ctx, ...)` and `releaseExecutor(ctx, ...)` on the writer's ctx (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:72-73), so a cancelled writer request reaches the release on a dead context. Whether the executor and binder paths detach (Decision 20 covers Binder.Release claim writes only) was not checked here; a mechanism or reliability lens should decide whether CODE-6 must also run step 5 on a detached context. Not part of the G3 fix.

### [non-spec.10.review-fresh.1]

### [non-spec.10.review-kubernetes.1]
DECISION: Kubernetes-idiom lens, non-spec round 10: no findings — BECAUSE the r8-prefix diff (the A14 bound, the CODE-7 admin-test sentence on in-process terminal claims, and the removed summary watch-out) touches no apiserver write, field manager, finalizer, webhook, or controller dependency, and the whole-proposal sweep still finds every SandboxClaim create, status patch, and delete with the gateway, its §4.6.3 owner — ALTERNATIVES: filing the A15 tier-2 pin (`TestReleaseBeforeClaimedProjectionLeavesPinnedIdlePod_spec_4_6_1`, "with the WarmPoolController reconciler running") as timing-dependent against a level-triggered projection (rejected as test-construction detail: an implementor can delete the bound claim before starting the manager or drive `Reconcile` directly, and either is deterministic).
FACT: The occupancy projection is a pure function of the current claim and the Sandbox's current phase. With no claim, a pod in `claimed` drains, a pod in `reserved` goes `idle`, and a pod in any warm-fill phase such as `idle` is left alone. A claim with an empty binding phase also leaves the pod alone. A15 is therefore exactly "deleted while the Sandbox still reads `idle`" — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go `ProjectOccupancyPhase`
USEFUL [non-spec.8.review-kubernetes.1]: its GC-classify and no-Sandbox-status-write FACTs held unchanged; `detachedReleaseContext` is `WithTimeout(WithoutCancel(ctx), slotCleanupFloor)` (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:99-101), so its UNVERIFIED on placement remains a mechanism-lens question, not an idiom one.

### [non-spec.10.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: One finding. CODE-2 item 8 does not fix where `Binder.Release` creates its `detachedReleaseContext` on the retire path, and its tier-1 test ("a fake adapter whose `Shutdown` outlasts the caller's context") passes for a context created at function entry. A plain retire `Shutdown` passes deadline 0, so the adapter's `Runtime.Close` runs until the caller's context ends (20 s for full_revoke, unbounded for the hook). A 5 s context created before that `Shutdown` is spent before the `Get` and `drain`, which makes register row A5's "the retire path still deletes the claim" false and leaves a claim to the GC on every slow retire. Before this change those writes ran on the caller's context and did not hit that limit. Remedy: create the context after `shutdownAdapter` returns, and give the test a `Shutdown` that blocks longer than `slotCleanupFloor`. BECAUSE spec.?/non-spec UNVERIFIED (review-log line ~2018) and WATCHOUT (~2041) both asked a mechanism reviewer to settle it — ALTERNATIVES: leaving it as an implementor detail (rejected: A5's bound and the no-regression claim of Decision 20 depend on the ordering, and the named test cannot catch it).
FACT: The Decision 19 fence holds against the tree. `startRelease` refuses while `claims > 0` (replica_records.go `startRelease`), and both `ReleaseTerminalBinding` callers, `sweepTerminal` (coordination.go) and the resume tail `landed` (resume_driver.go), go through it. `/start`'s refused `commitStart` leaves the binding registered and makes no release of its own (start.go `handleStart`), so in R2 step 4(a) sees the binding and skips. `beginSuspension` and `releaseUnderSlotLock` also refuse while the claim is open (suspend_release.go `claimed`). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replica_records.go `startRelease`, `claimed`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go `handleStart`.
FACT: A release-path rehydration that loses the cross-replica `rehydrating` SET NX waits for the peer's seed, which lacks its extra member. When the releasing row is terminal, that release removes nothing and skips the disposition. Traced: in every interleaving another release or reservation runs the one disposition, or the reservation's session occupies the pod. Not a finding. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go `rehydrate` (`!acquired` → `waitForFlag`).
USEFUL [review-log UNVERIFIED on CODE-2 item 8 placement, and the WATCHOUT after it]: pointed straight at the gap; confirmed by release.go `Binder.Release` (shutdownAdapter, then Get, then drain) and pkg/adapter/session.go `contextWithGraceDeadline` (grace <= 0 returns the parent).

### [non-spec.10.review-performance.1]
FACT: In the production chart (`tokenService.enabled`), `w.credAssign` is the Token Service `credassign.Client`. Its `Release` sends one synchronous `RevokeCredentials` RPC (5 s per-call timeout, `DefaultClientTimeout`) through the token_service breaker (5 consecutive failures, 30 s cooldown) and only then removes the local lease record. The shipped `RevokeUserLeases` made no Token Service call: it ran deny then `Remove`, after the cancel writes and the pod step. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:112,363-376,390; pkg/gateway/core/subsystem/breaker.go:63,66; charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:478-482; cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:184-191; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go:563-577.
WATCHOUT: Under **Order** step 2 the full_revoke critical path (deny list, cancel writes, 4(a), 4(b), and the token step) waits on N serial Token Service RPCs. A hung Token Service costs about 25 s before the breaker opens. A slow Token Service that still answers costs N x latency, and no breaker bounds it. The SPEC-3a order permits a concurrent Release fan-out, provided every Release finishes before the first deny write. — EVIDENCE: proposals/0093_*/0093_*.non-spec-changes.md:84,113
FACT: `sweepTerminal` re-lists every terminal row on each pass (`Sweep` lists all sessions of every tenant), so a refused `startRelease` is retried on the next pass. The A5 claim "the Sweeper releases it on a later listing" holds. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:398-433,530-535
FACT: `Counter.Reset` has no production caller (summary Defects already says so), so `rehydrated` flags and the counter keys of dead pods already persist in Redis today. Release-path rehydration adds no new garbage-key class, and an empty set auto-deletes. EVIDENCE: grep of `Reset(` under pkg/ and cmd/; pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:482
DECISION: did not file A5's leaked-slot-on-cut-`Shutdown` (the per-slot cleanup timeout `max(cleanupTimeoutSeconds/mcs,5)` can exceed `userTerminateRPCTimeout`) — BECAUSE A5 already registers it as a bounded departure under the owner's residual-gap directive — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as a regression versus the shipped D6 release on the lifetime context (rejected: registered and accepted).

### [non-spec.10.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: one finding: a full_revoke cancel write whose store error hides a committed `cancelled` row is left out of the cancelled set, so step 4(a) never reclaims a pre-running unbound session (R1) and a repeated full_revoke cannot list it; no register row covers it, and A23's "a repeated call completes the revoke" is false for that row — BECAUSE the proposal registers the same ambiguous-commit pattern for `failFinalizing` (A3) and the resume commit (A24), and `pgtenant.InTx` returns a commit error whose outcome is unknown — ALTERNATIVES: (1) re-filing the `reserveSlot` rollback single attempt (rejected: non-spec.1 UNVERIFIED already weighed it as pre-existing and GC/Counted-bounded); (2) filing CODE-2 item 8's context placement (rejected: non-spec.8 WATCHOUT judged it below the bar); (3) filing the same-replica in-flight `/start` against step 4(a)'s registry check (rejected: every pre-running terminal writer has the same exposure today, and it fails closed).
FACT: the Decision 19 fence holds against every handling-replica releaser I traced. `startRelease` refuses on `claims > 0` (replica_records.go `startRelease`); `beginSuspension` and `releaseUnderSlotLock` refuse on `claimed` (suspend_release.go); `commitStart` refuses after the cancel and leaves the binding registered (start.go `commitStart`); the funnel's guarded write misses a terminal row; and the resume tail's terminal re-check goes through `ReleaseTerminalBinding`. The only releasers that bypass `startRelease` are the funnel, `releaseEarlierBinding`, and `ReleaseUncommitted`, and none of them acts on a `ready` R2 binding. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replica_records.go; suspend_release.go; start.go; failure.go `applyFailureFromActive`; resume_driver.go `landed`
FACT: the coordination Sweeper lists every row (`ListFilter{}`) and gates `sweepTerminal` on `boundHere`. A release that the step-3 claim refuses is therefore retried on the next 15 s listing, including a binding whose row no longer names a pod. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go sweep List, `sweepTerminal`, interval default
FACT: `adapterclient.Shutdown(ctx, id, "", 0)` has no internal timeout, so a release's `Shutdown` is bounded only by its caller's context. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go `shutdown`
USEFUL [non-spec.8.review-reliability.1]: its FACT on the release-path rehydration settled the peer-seeded-release interleaving without re-reading slotcounter.go.
USEFUL [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]: its WATCHOUT and UNVERIFIED on the rollback single attempt stopped me from re-filing it.

### [non-spec.10.review-security.1]
DECISION: one finding. Register row A14 and the code-level bullet **A reservation made during a Redis outage** record the over-cap admission after an outage as a §12.4-compliant consequence. The departure is the missing §12.4 recovery rebuild: the failure-behavior row requires "On Redis recovery, the counter is rebuilt through the standard blocking-rehydration path before fast-path enforcement resumes", and SPEC-1d keeps that sentence ("the slot set is rebuilt"). `clearOutage` only resets the outage clock, and CODE-1 item 2 leaves `fallback.go` unchanged — BECAUSE the security lens counts a capacity bound that relaxes after a store outage for lack of the recovery half the Redis failure-behavior table requires — ALTERNATIVES: no finding, as non-spec.9.review-security.1 decided (rejected: that decision weighed only the fallback half and not the recovery sentence that the non-spec.8.fix-G2.1 OPEN raised); requiring a CODE-1 recovery rebuild (offered as the alternative fix and not required, because the owner prefers bounded departures).
FACT: Slot reservation clears the outage marker on the first Redis answer and runs no rehydration while the pod's `rehydrated` flag survives, so a session the fallback admitted stays uncounted after Redis recovers with its data. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:288-290 (`c.clearOutage()`); pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go:138-144; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:219.
FACT: No BUILD-GAPS finding records the missing slot-set rebuild on Redis recovery. EVIDENCE: grep "On Redis recovery|clearOutage" BUILD-GAPS.md returns only the experiment-cache row.
CORRECTS [non-spec.9.review-security.1 DECISION on A14]: "the §12.4 Postgres fallback is the durable fallback" covers admission during the outage and does not cover recovery. §12.4 also requires a rebuild on recovery, and the tree does not run one.
USEFUL [non-spec.8.fix-G2.1 FACT/OPEN]: it pointed straight at `clearOutage` and the §12.4 recovery sentence.
FACT: Re-walked the r8-to-current diff (A14 bound, CODE-7 admin claim-end test, deleted summary watch-out), the Decision 19 fence (refcounted `terminalClaimRecord.open`, so full_revoke's claim composes with other holders), terminateLocal's session matching (`want[bind.SessionID]` only), and `w.credAssign` (non-nil in both assigner modes). Nothing else meets the security bar. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replica_records.go:71-125; cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:112-140; cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:134-158.

### [spec.22.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: §5.2 **Post-recovery rehydration atomicity.** is the single home of the `rehydrated` flag lifetime (restart clears it, §10.1.4 whole-pod reset deletes it, outage end deletes it for each pod a non-terminal session names). SPEC-1b gained a fifth substitution for the paragraph's closing clause and its parenthetical became "(indicating the set must be rehydrated)"; SPEC-1c gained an in-place cut of the §12.4 `rehydrated` key row's restart-only lifetime ("from Postgres. No TTL."), leaving the row's existing "See [§5.2]" as the pointer — BECAUSE flag absence is the only rehydration trigger in §5.2, so the §12.4 failure-row rebuild kept by SPEC-1d needs a spec-stated flag delete on outage end (CODE-1 item 8 `rebuildAfterOutage`, and `Counter.Reset` at pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go deletes the flag too) — ALTERNATIVES: a new SPEC-1k item (rejected: new ID the checklist and files-touched list would have to absorb); restating the clearing events in the key row (rejected: second home); stating the outage clock, probe, or `ListPodsWithActiveSlots` in spec (rejected: implementation mechanics owned by CODE-1 item 8).
WATCHOUT: the outage-end flag delete is scoped to pods a non-terminal session names. Writing "every pod's flag" in spec drops the scope, and reseeding a pod holding only leaked members empties its set and over-admits. — EVIDENCE: non-spec CODE-1 item 8 (`ListPodsWithActiveSlots`, "The rebuild covers every pod a non-terminal per-slot row names").
FACT: both anchors occur once in the tree at this round: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md **Post-recovery rehydration atomicity.** closing sentence ("it persists until the next Redis restart, ... on first access") and spec/12_storage-architecture.md key row `lenny:pod:{pod_id}:rehydrated` ("from Postgres post-Redis-restart. No TTL — naturally cleared on next Redis restart."). Slugs `#1014-coordinator-loss-detection-and-hold-state` and `#124-redis-ha-and-failure-modes` are already used by existing spec links. — EVIDENCE: grep over spec/.
OPEN: non-spec TEST-1's tier-11 list ("§5.2, §12.4 (both rows), ...") pins no assertion on the new flag-lifetime clause; whether one is owed is for the non-spec loop.

### [spec.22.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Close the `rehydrated` flag-lifetime finding with in-place edits only: SPEC-1b's parenthetical replacement becomes "(indicating the set must be rehydrated)"; a fifth SPEC-1b substitution replaces the §5.2 closing clause "it persists until the next Redis restart, at which point all flags are naturally cleared and rehydration is triggered again on first access" with the three clearing events (restart, §10.1.4 whole-pod reset, the gateway's delete for each pod a non-terminal session names when a Redis outage ends, citing §12.4); SPEC-1c gains a second substitution in the `lenny:pod:{pod_id}:rehydrated` key row deleting "post-Redis-restart" and "— naturally cleared on next Redis restart" (the row already ends "See [§5.2]") — BECAUSE §5.2 is the home of the flag protocol, flag absence is the only rehydration trigger, and §12.4's failure-row rebuild sentence (kept by SPEC-1d) has no trigger the spec states without it — ALTERNATIVES: a new SPEC-1k item (rejected: the checklist's S1 says "all of SPEC-1", but folding into SPEC-1b/1c keeps the files-touched list and item IDs unchanged); restating the clearing events in the §12.4 key row (rejected: second statement of the §5.2 rule); keeping the outage clock and probe in spec (rejected: implementation detail owned by CODE-1 item 8).
WATCHOUT: The outage-end delete is scoped to pods a non-terminal session names. Do not write "deletes every flag": a reseed of a pod with only `leaked:` members and no session rows empties its set and over-admits. CODE-1 item 8 (`ListPodsWithActiveSlots`) is scoped this way for that reason. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8; spec-changes.md edge bullet "A reseed drops leaked members".

OPEN: The §12.4 `rehydrated` key row's description column still reads "Slot counter rehydration flag" after SPEC-1c renames the active_slots row to "slot set". This is counter vocabulary SPEC-1 left behind and is a separate finding. No tier-11 case pins the new flag-lifetime clause. TEST-1 Tier 11 "§12.4 (both rows)" means the two set-describing rows and stays true.

### [spec.22.review-performance.1]
DECISION: one spec finding, the `rehydrated` flag lifetime (§5.2 **Post-recovery rehydration atomicity.** parenthetical that SPEC-1b rewrites and its closing sentence; §12.4 key row `lenny:pod:{pod_id}:rehydrated`), filed from the Redis-reset angle; it duplicates spec.22 citations, mechanism, and security, so merge rather than count it thrice — BECAUSE CODE-1 item 8 deletes the flag when an outage without a restart ends, and the three sites say only a restart clears it — ALTERNATIVES: leaving it to the sibling lenses (rejected: the failure-mode trace of a Redis outage is this lens's core).
FACT: the staged spec edits add no etcd write per session. A release delete of the claim replaces the WarmPoolController's termination-time delete (one delete per occupancy episode either way), and SPEC-2d removes the gateway's `failed` status patch on the exclusive failed-session path, so the claim write count per occupancy falls or stays equal. The Redis set adds one O(1) script per reserve and release on the same per-pod key the counter used, so no new hot key. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.6.1 **Pod claim mechanism** ("Claim traffic therefore scales with pod-occupancy episodes"); spec-changes SPEC-2b, SPEC-2c, SPEC-2d.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, CODE-1 item 8]: "After a Redis restart the flags are already gone and the deletes do nothing" holds only for the first replica's rebuild. Each replica that observed the outage runs its own `rebuildAfterOutage` on its first call or probe tick after Redis returns (up to one probe interval apart), and a later replica's rebuild deletes flags that traffic through an earlier replica already re-set. A pod can therefore be reseeded once per gateway replica per outage, each reseed re-opening the A11 and A12 drops (leaked members, unpersisted reservations), and the §5.2 burst sentence "the per-pod `SET NX` lock ensures at most one query per pod" stops holding (up to R `GetActiveSlotsByPod` per pod plus R cross-tenant `ListPodsWithActiveSlots`). A remedy that keeps §5.2 true is a Redis-side recovery marker (for example, delete only flags whose set predates the outage end, or a single-flight `SET NX` on a cluster-wide rebuild key with the Redis run ID) rather than a spec change. The non-spec loop owns whether this is a register row (frequency of A11/A12) or a CODE-1 item 8 fix.
WATCHOUT: full_revoke deny-listing a pool credential (SPEC-3a step 1) denies every lease on that credential platform-wide, a top-tier availability concern; it is owner-settled to P5b (deny-list granularity) and is not a finding here. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md "Credential deny list" tagged-union key `{source: "pool", poolId, credentialId}`; summary P5b impact row.

### [reconcile.index-checklist.2026-10-18.3]
DECISION: Rebuilt the summary **Deliverable index** from the current staging. It lists SPEC-1 to SPEC-3, CODE-1 to CODE-8, DOCS-1, TEST-1, and REC-1 once each, and no deliverable was added or removed. The SPEC-1 line now names the `rehydrated` flag's clearing events, which the spec round 22 fix added to SPEC-1b (fifth substitution) and SPEC-1c (the `rehydrated` key row) without a new SPEC id. Every other line already matched its deliverable's Targets and the **Spec files touched** list — BECAUSE the only staging change since the 2026-10-18.2 reconcile is that SPEC-1b and SPEC-1c fix, together with the owner's remedy content in CODE-1 item 8, which the CODE-1 line already names — ALTERNATIVES: leaving the SPEC-1 line unchanged (rejected: it would describe SPEC-1 without the flag lifetime it now states).
DECISION: Kept the leading spec block in apply order, one lane per step: S1 (SPEC-1), S2 (SPEC-2, after S1), and S3 (SPEC-3). The S1 line now names the `rehydrated` flag lifetime. S1 to S3 keep Tiers 0, because DOCS-1 at S4 retargets the tier-11 gates that the spec steps break. Every non-spec Depends-on still names the spec steps that stage what its work implements: S4 (DOCS-1) on S1 to S3; S5 (CODE-8) on S2; S6 (CODE-1, including the item 8 recovery rebuild that SPEC-1b and SPEC-1d state) on S1 and S2; S7 (CODE-2) on S1, S2, and S6; S8 (CODE-3) and S9 (CODE-4) on S2 and S7; S10 (CODE-5) on none; S11 (CODE-6) on S10; S12 (CODE-7) on S3, S7, and S11; S13 (TEST-1) on every earlier step; and S14 (REC-1) on S13.
DECISION: Closed no DEFERRED as a repair in this pass. The 2026-10-18.2 reconcile closed or carried every DEFERRED before it. Two DEFERRED entries were recorded after it, and neither names a statement the spec staging made false that an edit to the four reconcile files can repair, so both are carried forward below.
OPEN: [adapterclient `Shutdown` comment] Carried from non-spec.8.review-client-surface.1's DEFERRED. The `Client.Shutdown` doc comment in `pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go` says "the §11.4 full_revoke fan-out passes `USER_REVOKED`", which CODE-7 item 6 makes false, and no CODE item lists it (CODE-8 names only the `ShutdownRecycle` comment in that file). What remains is the non-spec loop's choice of whether to stage an edit to that comment. Earlier lenses judged it below the bar (non-spec.6.review-client-surface.1, non-spec.8.review-edit-sites.1, and non-spec.8.review-fresh.1). If the edit is staged, it goes in non-spec-changes.md under the CODE-7 Targets or the CODE-8 Targets.
OPEN: [per-replica outage rebuild] Carried from spec.22.review-performance.1's DEFERRED. CODE-1 item 8's sentence "After a Redis restart the flags are already gone and the deletes do nothing" holds only for the first replica's rebuild. Each replica that observed the outage runs its own `rebuildAfterOutage`, and a later rebuild deletes flags that traffic through an earlier replica already re-set. A pod can therefore be reseeded once per replica per outage, which reopens the A11 and A12 drops each time and issues up to one `GetActiveSlotsByPod` per replica per pod. What remains is the non-spec loop's choice between a register row that bounds the repeat and a CODE-1 item 8 change that runs one rebuild per outage, such as a Redis-side recovery marker. Either remedy also corrects the quoted sentence. It lands in non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 and the **Accepted-departure register**, and in the TEST-1 tier-8 rebuild case if the remedy changes the mechanism.
DECISION: Added nothing to the summary's **Open decisions for human to make**, and its "None." line stands — BECAUSE no unclosed OPEN routes a decision to the human. The OPENs recorded after the 2026-10-18.2 reconcile are closed or routed to a loop. The A24 concurrent-pin OPENs are closed by non-spec.6.follow-up-fix.1. The A14 OPENs (non-spec.8.fix-G2.1 and non-spec.8.fix-design-G2.1) are closed by the owner's 2026-10-18 rebuild answer (summary Decision 21, A14 retired). The CODE-6 step-5 context OPENs (non-spec.10.fix-G3.1 and non-spec.10.fix-design-G3.1) are closed by the 2026-10-18 DECISION in Standing context. spec.22.fix-G1.1's tier-11 flag-lifetime assertion and spec.22.fix-design-G1.1's "Slot counter rehydration flag" label route work to the non-spec loop, and later spec lenses declined the label as naming-only. The two OPENs above route work or a design choice to the non-spec loop.

### [non-spec.11.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: Only `fallbackReserve` (as in the tree) and an unreachable `RunRecovery` tick stamp the outage clock; a `Release` or a release-path rehydration that hits one Redis error no longer stamps it — BECAUSE a non-admitting call leaves nothing uncounted, yet a stamp from it would trigger a cluster-wide `rehydrated` flag delete that drops every in-flight create and resume reservation (A12) — ALTERNATIVES: keep every call's stamp and record the single-error trigger in A12 (rejected: a fail-open trigger that adds no coverage); gate the rebuild on "this replica's fallback admitted" (rejected: loses the redundancy for an admitting replica that dies, A26 third case); a Redis-side once-per-outage marker (rejected: needs a cross-replica time comparison and an exception clause).
CORRECTS [remedy.failure-outcomes.2026-10-18, owner remedy 2]: "every slot-counter call that finds Redis unavailable stamps it" is no longer the staged design; CODE-1 item 8 bullet 3 now names `fallbackReserve` and the unreachable probe tick only. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go `fallbackReserve` is the only caller of `outageExceeded`, the sole stamp site today.
WATCHOUT: the recovery rebuild's reseed REPLACES the set (SPEC-1b "replaces ... with a set of those identifiers"), so it drops any fast-path reservation whose row does not yet name the pod: a create before its row is persisted (`claimAtCreate` reserves first) and a resume before its commit writes `pod_assignment` (`reserveResumeSlot` runs after `claimPod`). A union (SADD) reseed would avoid that but contradicts the converged SPEC-1b; the consequence is bounded in register row A12, widened in place to cover both the release-path and the reservation-path consequence and the per-replica repeat. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create.go `claimAtCreate`; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go `reserveResumeSlot`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go commit sets `PodAssignment`.
WATCHOUT: the rebuild deletes flags and changes no set, so any test asserting set contents after the rebuild must first drive the pod's next reservation or release; asserting right after a probe-tick rebuild fails against a correct implementation and pushes toward the eager reseed Decision 21 rejected. TEST-1 tier-4 and tier-8 cases now order the reservation first.
DECISION: A26's bound applies the one-probe-interval bound to the first case only; a fallback-admitted session whose row lands after the listing or the reseed stays uncounted until it ends, because the fallback writes no row (`ReserveSlotUnderLock` counts rows only; `pod_assignment` lands at bind) and only a new stamp re-arms the rebuild. The tier-4 case requires the admitted session's create to return during the outage so its row names the pod before the reseed. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go `ReserveSlotUnderLock` comment; fallback.go `outageExceeded` stamps only from zero.
DECISION: summary line "The one rebuild the design runs is §12.4's after a Redis outage" left unchanged (rung 1): it remains true, as the design runs one kind of rebuild.
DECISION: summary Decision 21 reads "retiring register row A14 for the narrower row A26" rather than the design's "replacing register row A14 with ...", because A14 no longer exists in the register and the wording should not suggest it does.
DECISION: the tier-1 `slotcounter` source-failure case asserts `Release` returns the source error; the `ErrSlotCounterRelease` wrapping is pinned by the tier-1 `podclaim` bullet, because `slotcounter` cannot import `podclaim` (podclaim/slotclaimer.go imports slotcounter).
DEFERRED [spec-changes.md]: the bullet **A reseed drops leaked members and unpersisted reservations (departure)** says "a create reserves its slot before it persists the row that names the pod" and names only the release-path consequence; true is that a resume also reserves before its commit names the pod, and that a reservation-path reseed admits one session over the cap per dropped reservation (now in register row A12).

### [non-spec.11.fix-G2.1]
FACT: tier 0 (`runStaticTier` in cmd/lenny-test/cmd_run.go) vets only untagged packages and the `contract`-tagged tier-3 tree, so a `chaos`-tagged tier-8 file broken by a signature change compiles nowhere until a step runs tier 8 — EVIDENCE: tests/tier8_chaos/slot_counter_redis_outage_test.go:3 `//go:build chaos`, :69 `counter.Reserve(ctx, pod, maxConcurrent)` against CODE-1 item 2's 4-argument `Reserve`.
DECISION: S6 runs tier 8 and the CODE-1 **Retargets.** bullet names the tier-8 outage test beside the 7a scenario as a session-identifier caller — BECAUSE the Testing lead puts a test a deliverable breaks in that deliverable's step — ALTERNATIVES: leaving the retarget to TEST-1's tier-8 extensions (S13) leaves S6 to S12 with a non-compiling chaos file.
WATCHOUT: when a step changes a signature, grep the build-tagged tiers too (`chaos`, `contract`, `e2e`, etc.); tier 0 does not catch them — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-test/cmd_run.go runStaticTier.
OPEN: tests/tier8_chaos/compensation_loss_test.go:244 and :261 call `binder.ReleaseSlotReservation(ctx, pod, slotID, leaked)`. CODE-1 keeps that signature (non-spec-changes CODE-1 item 5), so the file compiles, but nobody has checked whether its assertions survive the keyed-set release semantics; a test-coverage lens should decide whether it belongs in the CODE-1 Retargets bullet.

### [non-spec.11.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: The recovery rebuild's drop of in-flight reservations is recorded by widening register row A12 in place (both consequences of one mechanism: a reseed drops a reservation whose row does not yet name the pod, from a create before its INSERT or a resume before its commit), not by a new row and not by changing the reseed — BECAUSE the converged staged spec fixes both the replace semantics (SPEC-1b "replaces `lenny:pod:{pod_id}:active_slots` with a set of those identifiers") and the flag delete "for each pod a non-terminal session names when a Redis outage ends", so no non-spec design can keep in-flight members — ALTERNATIVES: a union reseed (SADD rows into the existing set; rejected: contradicts SPEC-1b's "replaces" and the spec loop converged); a Redis-side once-per-outage marker (rejected: cross-replica clock comparison plus an exception for a replica that admitted after another's rebuild, i.e. hair); remembering admitted (pod, session) pairs per replica and SADDing them on recovery (rejected: Decision 21 already rejected per-replica pod memory, and SPEC-1b mandates the flag delete).
DECISION: The outage clock is stamped only by `fallbackReserve` (as in the tree today) and by an unreachable probe tick. The `Release` and rehydration stamps are deleted from CODE-1 item 8 — BECAUSE only a fallback admission leaves anything uncounted, every replica's probe stamps within one interval of any outage longer than that, and a release-side stamp only adds cluster-wide rebuilds (each one drops every in-flight create and resume reservation on every per-slot pod) after single release timeouts that admitted nothing — ALTERNATIVES: keeping every call's stamp and recording the noise (rejected: it keeps a fail-open trigger that buys no coverage); gating the rebuild on "this replica's fallback admitted" (rejected: loses the redundancy that covers an admitting replica dying during the outage, A26's third case).
DECISION: summary Non-goals "Rehydration as the slot backstop" ("The one rebuild the design runs is §12.4's after a Redis outage") is left unchanged — BECAUSE it is true (one kind of rebuild, after an outage) and no implementor builds differently; the over-admission it names for a periodic reseed is now bounded per outage by A12.
FACT: `isRedisUnavailable` treats any `net.Error` and the substring "i/o timeout" as unavailable, and today only `fallbackReserve` (via `outageExceeded`) stamps `outageSince`; `Reserve` calls `clearOutage` on any Redis answer. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go `fallbackReserve`, `outageExceeded`, `isRedisUnavailable`; slotcounter.go `Reserve`.
FACT: A resume reserves its slot right after `claimPod` and its row names the new pod only at the resume driver's commit, so the reservation-without-row window spans the whole resume, much longer than a create's reserve-to-persist window. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go `reserveResumeSlot` call after `claimPod`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go `r.PodAssignment = b.sandbox`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create.go `claimAtCreate` then `row.PodAssignment = createClaim.SandboxName`.
DEFERRED [spec-changes.md]: the edge case **A reseed drops leaked members and unpersisted reservations** says only that "a create reserves its slot before it persists the row that names the pod" and gives only the release-path consequence. True instead: a resume also reserves before its commit names the pod, and a reservation-path rehydration that drops such a reservation admits one session over the cap until it ends (register row A12 after non-spec.11 G1). This loop does not edit spec-changes.md.
WATCHOUT: a test that asserts a slot set's contents "after the recovery rebuild" must first drive a reservation or release on the pod, because the rebuild only deletes flags and the reseed runs on the pod's next call. A fallback-admitted session is counted only if its row names the pod by that reseed; a test of the counted case must let the create return before Redis comes back. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 bullet 2.
WATCHOUT: `ErrSlotCounterRelease` lives in `podclaim`, which imports `slotcounter`; a `slotcounter` test can assert only the raw source error from `Counter.Release`. The wrapping is pinned by the Tier 1 `podclaim` bullet. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go import of slotcounter.
USEFUL [remedy.failure-outcomes.2026-10-18]: the Decision 21 entry's list of rejected alternatives saved re-deriving the union, start-time, and per-replica-memory options.

### [non-spec.11.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: S6 tier line becomes "Tiers 0, 1, 2, 4, 7a, 8."; the CODE-1 **Retargets.** bullet's last sentence names the tier-8 outage test beside the tier-7a scenario as a caller that passes the session identifier — BECAUSE CODE-1 item 2's `Reserve` signature change breaks the chaos-tagged file, which tier 0 does not compile — ALTERNATIVES: moving the signature fix into TEST-1's tier-8 extensions (rejected: TEST-1 lands in a later step, so S6 would leave the file non-compiling).
WATCHOUT: tier 0 (`runStaticTier`) vets only untagged packages and the contract tag, so a signature change to a type that a build-tagged tier test calls is invisible until that tier runs — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-test/cmd_run.go runStaticTier; tests/tier8_chaos/slot_counter_redis_outage_test.go:3 `//go:build chaos`.

### [non-spec.11.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The recovery rebuild (CODE-1 item 8) deletes flags only; the set changes at the pod's NEXT reservation or release, which reseeds from `GetActiveSlotsByPod`. Any test or doc that asserts set contents "after the rebuild runs" without an intervening call is asserting a state the mechanism never produces. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 ("changes no set"); pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go `rehydrate`, `seed`.
FACT: Both the create and the resume reserve a slot before any row names the pod: create.go `claimMintPersist` runs `claimAtCreate` before the INSERT; resume.go `Binder.Resume` runs `reserveResumeSlot` before resume_driver.go's commit writes `PodAssignment = b.sandbox`. A reseed in that window drops the member. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create.go:329-357; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:196; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go:529.
WATCHOUT: the register covers the reseed's drop of an in-flight reservation only on the release path that empties the set (A12). The reservation-path reseed over-admits, and the recovery rebuild makes that happen on a Redis that kept the member, which is new with CODE-1 item 8. The summary Non-goals bullet on rehydration as a backstop states the over-admission itself. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md register rows A11, A12, A26; summary.md Non-goals "Rehydration as the slot backstop".
FACT: No tier-11 or other test pins the §5.2 "persists until the next Redis restart" sentence or the §12.4 `rehydrated` row text, so SPEC-1b's fifth substitution and the SPEC-1c row edit break no gate. The gateway binary is outside the tier-0 deployment-boundary flag gate (`boundaryBinaries` lists the adapter and runtimes only), so the new probe-interval flag needs no chart render to pass tier 0. EVIDENCE: tests/tier0_static/deployment_boundary_scan_test.go:60.

### [non-spec.11.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed two findings on CODE-1 item 8, both in non-spec text. (1) The rebuild is per-Counter (one per gateway process, cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:245) and is triggered by any single slot-counter call that sees a connectivity error (fallback.go:150-190 matches net.Error and "i/o timeout"), so each replica that observed the outage, and each isolated blip, deletes every per-slot pod's flag again. Each reseed drops fast-path reservations whose rows do not yet name the pod; on the reserve path that over-admits, and no register row bounds it (A11 = leaked members, A12 = release-path disposition, A26 = fallback-admitted sessions). The "deletes do nothing" sentence (non-spec:216) and summary line 66 "The one rebuild the design runs" are false. This closes the reconcile OPEN [per-replica outage rebuild] as a finding rather than leaving it open. (2) A26's second category (row names the pod only after its rebuild) is the dominant case for a short outage, because the rebuild runs on the replica's next slot call (milliseconds) while the admitted session's row is written at bind; its bound is the session's lifetime, not "one probe interval", and DOCS-1 item 9's "uncounted until a replica that observed the outage runs that rebuild" is false for it — ALTERNATIVES: filing the transient-error trigger alone (folded into 1); filing the per-call cross-tenant listing during an outage (performance lens owns it); filing the lost-stamp race when a failure lands during an in-flight rebuild (only the probe or a Release can land there, neither admits, and an admission there falls into A26 category 2 anyway).
FACT: the fallback admission writes no row or member; ReserveSlotUnderLock only counts rows under an advisory lock, and the row's pod_assignment is persisted at bind. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:854-908.
FACT: outageExceeded stamps only when the clock is zero, so a failure observed while the clock is already set records nothing new. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go:124-133.
WATCHOUT: a remedy for finding 1 that uses a cluster-wide SET NX "rebuild once" marker must still rebuild for a replica whose own fallback admitted after another replica's rebuild, or it reopens A26 category 2 for that replica.

### [non-spec.11.review-test-coverage.1]
FACT: tier 0 (`lenny-test --tier static`) vets only untagged packages and `-tags=contract ./tests/tier3_contract/...`; chaos-, integration-, component-, load_local-, and security-tagged tests are compiled only when their own tier runs. A signature change in a step that does not run tier 8 leaves a chaos test uncompiled until a later step runs tier 8. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-test/cmd_run.go `runStaticTier` (checks list).
WATCHOUT: `tests/tier8_chaos/slot_counter_redis_outage_test.go` calls `counter.Reserve(ctx, pod, maxConcurrent)` at :69, :80, :101, :121, :140, :154, so CODE-1 item 2's new `Reserve` signature breaks it in S6, which runs tiers 0, 1, 2, 4, and 7a only. EVIDENCE: implementation-checklist S6.
WATCHOUT: `ErrSlotCounterRelease` is a `podclaim` sentinel (CODE-1 item 3). `slotcounter.Counter.Release` cannot return it, so a `slotcounter` tier-1 assertion that a failed rebuild makes `Release` return an error wrapped in it belongs at the `podclaim` layer. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes Testing, "Tier 1, `slotcounter`, the recovery rebuild".
FACT: the staged SPEC-1b flag-lifetime sentence breaks no tier-11 gate. No test under tests/ quotes "naturally cleared", "until the next Redis restart", or "post-Redis-restart". `Counter.Reset` already deletes the `rehydrated` flag (slotcounter.go `Reset` keys), so the §10.1.4 clause needs no new test. EVIDENCE: grep over tests/; pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go `Reset`.
UNVERIFIED: CODE-1 item 8 does not say what `rebuildAfterOutage` does when no `SlotSource` is wired. `tests/tier7a_load_local/scenarios/redis_disconnect_midflight` builds `slotcounter.New(client)` with no source and restarts miniredis in the middle of the run, so after item 8 it reaches the rebuild with a nil source. Production always wires `WithSlotSource` (cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go). This is a mechanism-lens question, not a test-lens one.
